# Changelog

Summaries of the history of the whole repository, newest first. Each subproject
keeps its own detailed changelog, and those are the record of what changed in
the code:

- [`project-portfolio-management-matcher-with-rust/CHANGELOG.md`](project-portfolio-management-matcher-with-rust/CHANGELOG.md)
- [`project-portfolio-management-service-with-rust/CHANGELOG.md`](project-portfolio-management-service-with-rust/CHANGELOG.md)
- [`project-portfolio-management-ui-with-svelte/CHANGELOG.md`](project-portfolio-management-ui-with-svelte/CHANGELOG.md)

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and the project follows [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).
It is pre-1.0; the first release cut from the root release workflow is
[0.2.0](#020---2026-10-21) (tag `v0.2.0`). Dates are 2026.

## Unreleased

### 14 November (research: the DHCW Roadmap Editor)

- **Research on the DHCW Roadmap Editor, with 14 proposed tasks.** Digital Health and Care Wales's tool lets Product
  Leads keep public Now / Next / Later product roadmaps, and it plans a PostgreSQL-backed API in place of its Google
  spreadsheet. The map is [`spec/dhcw-roadmaps-editor-lessons/index.md`](spec/dhcw-roadmaps-editor-lessons/index.md);
  the assessment and advice are `plan.md` §88 to §92; the tasks RE-1 to RE-14 are in `tasks.md`; and the open questions
  OQ-14 (anything served without a token) and OQ-15 (offering this service as their API) are in
  `spec/16-open-questions.md`. Ideas only: the source is MIT, and no code was copied.
- Not done: nothing is built, and no task is confirmed by the owner. Not verified: the editor was read from its
  documents, types and a public clone at commit `cc81478`, not run; its public site was not read; and no one at DHCW
  was contacted.

### 14 November (syncpack)

- **syncpack keeps the UI's and the site's shared packages on the same version.** A private root `package.json`
  (repository tooling only, never released) has syncpack 15.3.3 as its only dev dependency, installed with pnpm
  (`pnpm-lock.yaml`). `.syncpackrc` lists the UI's and the site's `package.json` files, and the root package is
  ignored because it has no version. Run `make lint-packages`, which `make lint` now includes. Its first finding is fixed: the
  site's `@lilydesignsystem/svelte-headless` goes from 0.6.1 to 0.8.0, the UI's version. The `Slider` API is
  unchanged, and the site's checks and all 239 Playwright tests pass.
- Not done: no CI job runs syncpack yet, and Dependabot does not watch the root `package.json`.

### 13 November (the path to production in German, Spanish and French)

- **`de-001`, `de-de`, `es-001` and `fr-001` now have the path to production**
  (`locales/<locale>/path-to-production/index.md`; `de-001` is a copy of `de-de`). They are the interface's languages
  with no other document, and the page is their first. On the site: `/<locale>/path-to-production/` with that locale's
  `lang`. The page's locale picker lists all fifteen locales. The site's own words stay English on these four (they are
  in five languages), so a slider's label there is German, Spanish or French with "Readiness estimate" in English. The
  site translation review gains `site-de.csv`, `site-es.csv` and `site-fr.csv`, each with the page's row alone.
  Specs: `spec/locales.md`, `spec/github-pages-site.md`.
- Tests: the site's 239 Playwright tests pass. Every locale's page is checked for one slider per item at the English
  estimates, with axe under light and dark. A new test checks `lang` and the heading in German, Spanish and French, the
  switch from English to Deutsch, and that `de-001` equals `de-de`.
- Not done or not verified: the three translations are machine-written and not reviewed. These locales have no home
  page, briefs or topics; the root sitemap lists their page, but there is no per-locale sitemap.

### 13 November (a dropped connection under concurrent requests; a load test in Rust)

- **Fixed: under concurrent requests, about one in a thousand connections dropped.** The tracing middleware
  (`observability::trace_mw`) held a `Span::enter` guard across `next.run(req).await`. Concurrent requests stacked each
  other's spans on the worker threads, the `tracing` registry's reference counts went wrong, and it panicked inside the
  connection task. The client saw `IncompleteMessage`: 30 of about 30,000 requests in a local run. The span is now
  attached with `Instrument`. A new test, `tests/trace_span_concurrency.rs`, needs no database and runs in the `api` job.
  It failed before the fix and passes after, in three runs. This is likely the "unexplained" error in the issues-register
  row and the 4 errors of the soak in `BENCHMARKS.md`; those runs were not repeated to confirm it.
- **A load test in Rust, `tests/load_test.rs`** (replacing an untracked draft). Workers send read requests back to
  back against an already running service, configured by `PPM_LOAD_TEST_BASE`, `PPM_LOAD_TEST_SECONDS` and
  `PPM_LOAD_TEST_CONCURRENCY`. It prints p50, p95, p99 and max per endpoint, and fails on any 5xx or transport error. It
  is `#[ignore]`d, and CI's `api-db` loop skips it, because CI starts no service. Its percentile unit test runs in the
  `api` job. Against a local service with login off, after the fix: 43,191 requests in 10 s at 16 workers, all 200,
  p50 2.3 to 5.0 ms. `scripts/load-test.mjs` stays the source of the published numbers.
- **SeaORM wording.** The service spec's outbox entries and open task T-3 in `spec/13-tasks.md` described the
  current code as SeaORM; they now say sqlx and axa. The frozen migration files, the `seaql_migrations` bridge and dated
  history keep their SeaORM names.
- Verified: service fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,266 unit tests, and the DB-free binaries;
  `generate-status.sh`. Not verified: the database suites (CI), and the new load test on any machine but one laptop.

### 13 November (path to production)

### 11 November (SeaORM removal, step (c): SeaORM gone)

- **The service has no ORM (task SQ-1 (c), ticked; spec §8.3b rewritten as done).** axa's `sea-orm` feature is off and its `sqlx` feature on, so `AppContext::db` is sqlx's `PgPool` (opened by `axa::db::connect`, pinged by `/_health/ready`); `sea-orm` is gone from the service's `Cargo.toml` and from `Cargo.lock` (48 crates fewer, `sea-orm`, `sea-query`, `sea-orm-migration`, `axa-seaorm` among them), and the migration crate names no ORM dependency. `src/db.rs` reaches the pool directly (`pool`, `begin`, `for_axa_crate`, `for_migrations`) and every statement is still counted; `query_count`'s SeaORM metric callback (`install`) is gone, as nothing can run a SeaORM statement. `chrono` gains its `serde` feature and `rust_decimal` (with `serde`) becomes a direct dependency, both features SeaORM used to switch on for the row structs' JSON.
- **The row structs** (`src/models/_entities`, 173 files): each keeps its `Model` with the same fields, order, types and derives (`Clone`, `Debug`, `PartialEq`, `Eq` where it had it, `Serialize`, `Deserialize`, `sqlx::FromRow`), so its JSON is the same; the SeaORM derive and attributes, the `Relation` enums, the `ActiveModelBehavior` impls and the `prelude` are gone, with SeaORM's type names kept as three aliases in `_entities/mod.rs` (`DateTimeWithTimeZone`, `Date`, `Json`). The module keeps its path, so the 136 files under `src/` and the tests that read rows into it did not change. `models/behaviors`, `models/canvases`, `models/strategy` and `models/saved_views` (only `ActiveModelBehavior` impls) are deleted; `models/plans`, `merge_records`, `audit_logs` and `webhook_deliveries` re-export only `Model`. Three controllers (`prioritization`, `scoring`, `task_get`) import `uuid::Uuid`, which axa's prelude gave them under the `sea-orm` feature.
- **The tests:** the 44 test files that used SeaORM (`execute_unprepared`, raw `Statement`s, `Entity::find` counts and reads, `ActiveModel` inserts and updates, `update_many`, `delete_many`, a SeaORM transaction) use sqlx through `db::pool(&ctx)` and `db::begin(&ctx)`, asserting the same things: a count is `SELECT COUNT(*)` with the same filters, a read the same `WHERE` (and `LIMIT 1` where SeaORM's `one` added it), an insert lists the columns the `ActiveModel` set, and an update by id names the columns it set and fails when no row comes back, as SeaORM's did; `webhook_redelivery` writes its outbox event through `db::Tx::write_outbox`; `fluvio_relay` and `event_outbox`, which took SeaORM's raw pool, read through `db::pool`.
- **Census, stricter:** the test that nothing takes the raw pool read `src/` for `get_postgres_connection_pool` (SeaORM's way to the pool); it now also refuses any `<context>.db` field access outside `src/db.rs`, and reads `tests/` and `benches/` as well as `src/` (and fails if it reads fewer than 500 files). A probe (`&ctx.db` in a test file, `c.db.clone()` in `src/`) was caught. The transaction census keeps the raw pool's `begin` among what opens a transaction. `drum_pipeline`'s `items_after_statements` expectation went with the `use` that needed it.
- **Comments:** the SeaORM mentions in `src/` and `tests/` that described the code as it was before SQ-1 now say "before SQ-1"; the migration crate's `seaorm_name`, `SEAORM_HISTORY` and `seaorm_names.txt` are `old_name`, `OLD_HISTORY` and `old_names.txt`, and `tests/migrations.rs`' helpers likewise. Left on purpose: the header line of each of the 285 frozen migration files (it says which `sea-orm-migration` migration it was frozen from; editing it would change the file's checksum, which `db migrate` refuses on a database that has applied it), and one sentence of the migration crate's module documentation naming the old migrator, whose table `seaql_migrations` the bridge still reads.
- **Docs:** spec §8.3b (now "The database: plain sqlx"), the persistence spec, the service spec, the root and service `AGENTS.md`, `agents/review-checklist.md` (a new database rule), `agents/models.md`, `agents/subprojects.md`, `llms.json`/`llms.txt`, the CTO briefs in every locale and the site's copy of them, the time-based-analysis traceability row, a UI type comment and both `deny.toml` comments. Historical CHANGELOG entries, dated task notes and `operations/upgrade-and-migration.md` (which describes the bridge) are as they were.
- **Behaviour:** none intended, and none found. A throwaway comparison (not committed) ran the earlier batches' twelve dump tests (batches 7 to 17, batch 13 under both transports), thousands of answers and every row they write, on the code before and after: the same, but for lines that also differ between two runs before (the sandbox's `plan_etag` over a `HashMap`, a list of audit actors read with no `ORDER BY`, generator ties). `openapi.json` did not change.
- Not done: the `_entities` module was not renamed (a rename is a mechanical change of its own, to 136 files and the tests); nothing reads the 11 row structs no code uses (`attachment_texts`, `strategic_aims` and nine more), kept because the personal-data inventory test reads every row struct as the schema.
- Not verified: CI; the container image build; the chaos test; a long-lived database.
- Verified: fmt; clippy (all targets, all features); 1,266 unit tests (one new: the census's line rule); the migration crate's clippy and 7 tests; the three DB-free binaries (7 tests); `cargo deny --all-features check` (the same two unmatched-licence warnings as before, 30 duplicate warnings, 32 before); `cargo machete`; `cargo check --features fluvio --all-targets`; `personal_data_inventory` (5); on a throwaway database the whole `mod` suite (220 passed, one run, nothing listening on port 8001 before it) and the 58 other database test binaries (92 tests, `keycloak_entra_broker` and `keycloak_guard` with the `keycloak` feature, `migrations` 7, no failure); the service binary booted on a fresh database (`APP_ENV=development`, auto-migrate): `/_health/live`, `/_health/ready` and `/_health/startup` `200`, `POST` and `GET /api/plans` `200`, and nothing left listening after it stopped; the restore drill (`scripts/restore-test.sh -t` with the whole `mod` suite on the restored copy, alone: passed, 220 passed); the status, OpenAPI-bodies (228, current), llms and site-content checks; the offline link check (0 broken).
### 11 November (SeaORM removal, step (b): migrations on sqlx)

- **Breaking for operators: migrations move from `sea-orm-migration` to sqlx, with a one-time history bridge (task SQ-1 (b), spec §8.3b; decided by the owner on 10 November).** Each of the 143 migrations is now a SQL file in `migration/migrations/`, frozen from the statements its Rust migration ran on PostgreSQL 18, in order: they were captured from a scratch server's statement log (`log_statement = 'all'`, JSON log) while the old migrator ran every `up`, and every `down` one at a time, one migration per transaction, so each file is what ran, including the statements that came from axa's constants (108's frozen `V01_SETUP` and move, 109's `FREE_THE_NAME`, `SETUP_STATEMENTS` and `IMPORT_STATEMENTS`, 107's idempotency `SETUP_STATEMENTS`) and the seed rows (100's six issue types). Migrations whose effect depends on existing data or objects keep their logic as SQL, not one run's effect (no migration branched in Rust; numbers below are the SeaORM sequence numbers): `m20260902_000001_automation_multi_action` (rewrites `automations`' actions), 049 (respells `materialised`), 066 (imports organizations from `capacity_commitments` and links them), 088 (copies each framework into `framework_versions`), 100 (a `DO` block that retires issue workflows only if `workflows.issue_type` is missing), 107 (drops `idempotency_keys` if present), 108 (moves `event_outbox` into `axa_outbox`), 109 (renames an index if present, imports the outbox into the record), and every `IF NOT EXISTS`/`IF EXISTS`; downs that delete or rewrite rows (`automation_multi_action`, 045, 049, 066, 071, 081, 084, 085, 100, 108) the same. 142 migrations roll back (`.up.sql` and `.down.sql`) and one refuses, as before: `m20261109_000109_axa_outbox_record`, a plain `.sql` refused by `db down` with its old reason. The old module doc comments are kept as a comment at the head of each up file.
- **The runner** (`migration/src/lib.rs`): sqlx's migrator for `up` (each migration its own transaction, under sqlx's advisory lock, refusing a history that names an unknown version or a changed file), and the crate's own `down`, because sqlx's `undo` would skip a migration with no down file and roll back the older ones beneath it: newest first, each with sqlx's `revert`, stopping with the refusal, as SeaORM's `down` did. `db reset` drops what SeaORM's `fresh` dropped (the base tables, then the types that are not arrays and belong to no extension, in `current_schema()`), then migrates; `db status` prints the same `Migration '<SeaORM name>'... Applied|Pending` lines and now only reads. `build.rs` embeds the files; a unit test holds the embedded set equal to sqlx's own reading of the directory (versions, descriptions, kinds, checksums), others that the 143 SeaORM names map one to one in order, and that exactly the refused migrations lack a down file. `sea-orm-migration` and `axa` are no longer dependencies of the migration crate (axa's `sea-orm` feature still brings `sea-orm-migration` in, until step (c)).
- **The bridge:** on `db migrate`, on `db down`, and on a boot that migrates (development and test), if `seaql_migrations` exists, every name in it must be a migration this binary has, or the runner refuses, changing nothing (a database newer than the binary); if `_sqlx_migrations` does not exist yet, it is made and every recorded migration copied into it in one transaction, marked applied, with its frozen file's checksum, SeaORM's time and an execution time of 0; then sqlx applies what is pending. After that, sqlx's table is the history, so a second run copies nothing and a migration rolled back by the new `db down` is not marked applied again. `seaql_migrations` is never written or dropped. The version is the SeaORM name's digits (`m20261109_000109_…` is `20261109000109`), so the order is unchanged. `scripts/backup.sh`'s `MANIFEST` and `scripts/restore-test.sh`'s history check read `_sqlx_migrations` (and `seaql_migrations` where it still exists); `scripts/generate-status.sh` counts the SQL files; CI's `api` job now also runs the migration crate's clippy and unit tests. **One-way:** once this release has applied a migration, an older image must not run against the database; going back needs the backup ([upgrade guide](operations/upgrade-and-migration.md#upgrading-to-sqlx-migrations)).
- **Deliberate changes:** the runner's statements are no longer counted in `query_count` (they reach the pool through the new `db::for_migrations`); `db status` no longer creates `seaql_migrations` on a database that has none; a refusal now names the migration (`Migration Error: <reason> (<name>)`); a database newer than the binary is refused with the names it does not know (SeaORM said a migration file was missing).
- Not done: step (c) (the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature, the sea-orm dependencies). The production `start`, which does not migrate, does not read either history, so a database newer than the binary is refused by the `migrate` job, not by `start`. The brief's estimate of about 19 migrations refusing to roll back was not borne out: running every `down` on the old migrator, one at a time, found one refusal (109); the other 142 rolled back on an empty database (a down whose statements fail on some data still fails the same way, as its SQL is the same).
- Not verified: CI; a bridge on a production-sized or long-lived database (only on fresh ones and on ones seeded at the data migrations); two processes migrating at once (sqlx's lock is taken around the bridge and the run, not tested); the container image build; the chaos test.
- Verified: (a) a database built by the old migrator and one built by the new runner give identical `pg_dump --schema-only --no-owner` output apart from the two history tables (12,442 lines), and identical data in every table (only `issue_types` has rows after migrating; equal once `gen_random_uuid()` and `now()` are normalised); the same with rows seeded before `automation_multi_action`, 049, 066, 088, 100 and 108 and the rest run by each runner, and with the old migrator stopped at 141 and the rest run by the new binary; (b) a database built by the old migrator, then booted and `db migrate`d by the new binary, dumps (schema and data, `seaql_migrations` included) byte for byte as before apart from `_sqlx_migrations` (143 rows, all copied), and a second run leaves `_sqlx_migrations` unchanged; (c) on a throwaway database the new runner built: the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 passed, one run; the known intermittent `requests::attachments` failure did not recur) and the 58 other database test binaries (90 tests, the new `migrations` among them; `keycloak_entra_broker` and `keycloak_guard` with the `keycloak` feature); the restore drill (`scripts/restore-test.sh -t` with the whole suite on the restored copy, alone: passed, `mod` 220 passed on it; and once more on an old-built database bridged by the new binary, which keeps both histories: passed); fmt, clippy (all targets, all features, and the migration crate), 1,265 unit tests and the migration crate's 7, `cargo deny check`, `cargo machete`; the status, OpenAPI-bodies and llms checks. New database tests (`tests/migrations.rs`, 7): a database the SeaORM migrator built fully is bridged with nothing run again and its schema unchanged; one built part way gets the rest from sqlx (a risk spelled `materialised` is respelled); a second run changes nothing; a SeaORM history naming an unknown migration is refused before and after bridging; the event record's rollback is still refused with its reason; a rollback that worked still works, and is not undone by the bridge; `db reset` drops both histories.

### 11 November (SeaORM removal, batch 18)

- **The infrastructure moves from SeaORM to sqlx, the last of step (a) (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `app.rs` (`db truncate`), `scheduled_jobs`, `retention`, `webhooks`, `relay`, `idempotency`, `flow_metrics`, `tasks/search`, `tasks/outbox_retry_failed`, `controllers/mod.rs`, `db.rs` and the delivery log's writer now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction. No statement of the service's own runs through SeaORM now; what is left is the migrator (step (b)), `query_count`'s SeaORM callback and the pool reached through `ctx.db` while it is SeaORM's connection, and the `ActiveModelBehavior` impls and entity re-exports (step (c)). A scheduled job's claim (the advisory lock, the interval check, the run row and its audit row) and its closing record are each one transaction, the audit rows still refused with `audit_failed`; the retention sweep is still a dry run unless applied, follows the same schedule and settings, and deletes each table with its flag and audit row in one transaction; webhook signing (v1 and v2), retries, backoff and the URL-free `transport_error` are untouched; `outbox_retry_failed` still needs `by:` and `reason:` and releases every failed event in one transaction, now a counted `db::begin` one (`db::Tx::release_outbox`). `AuditModel::record`, `controllers::db_err` and the unused `webhook_deliveries::Model::recent` are gone. The census that no code uses the pool while it holds a transaction now reads every file under `src/` and finds `db::begin` transactions (it read `src/controllers/` alone, for `ctx.db.begin()`, which nothing calls now): 299 transactions, no fault. 99 lines naming SeaORM are gone; 15 files and 173 lines are left by batch 6's measure (13 by spec §8.3b's count, 12 that name the API).
- **Deliberate changes:** a database failure carries sqlx's error text rather than SeaORM's (no `Query Error: ` or `Execution Error: ` prefix) in a task's output, a scheduled run's `detail` and audit snapshot, and the log. Statements SeaORM ran uncounted (retention's flag, `db truncate`'s outbox statements) and the outbox release are now counted in `query_count`. The claim's interval check and the read before the closing update read only the id; the flow pass reads only pids; the delivery log's insert returns only its id.
- **Found, not changed:** `db truncate` stops at `audit_logs`, which refuses `DELETE` outside the retention task (RG-3), after emptying the tables before it and remaking the outbox, before and after this change; the flow pass's cap of 1000 plans has no `ORDER BY`; the search rebuild pages by `OFFSET`, so a plan written during it can be read twice or missed; a scheduled run whose closing audit row cannot be written stays `running`.
- Not done: step (b) (the migration crate) and step (c) (the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature, the dependencies); the sqlx code reads rows into the `_entities` structs, which step (c) must keep as plain `sqlx::FromRow` structs.
- Not verified: a real database outage (only a table renamed and audit inserts refused by a trigger); the relay's webhook fan-out with a running relay (the delivery path was exercised through the redeliver route; `fluvio_relay` was not run, as in CI's `db` job); `db truncate` on a database where `audit_logs` may be emptied. ### 11 November (SeaORM removal, batch 17)

- **Strategy, scoring, prioritization, modules, milestones, reports, the dashboard, PDFs, settings, compliance, handling marks and data-subject requests move from SeaORM to sqlx (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `strategy`, `scoring`, `prioritization`, `modules`, `pdf`, `handling_marks`, `settings`, `compliance`, `subjects`, `visibility/milestones`, `visibility/reports`, `visibility/dashboard` and `visibility/mod` now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction, and `models::visibility`'s finders (`all_dependencies` among them, which the board pack, insights, confidence and visibility reach) take the context. Handling marks give the same answers and the hiding still fails closed; the subjects export, pseudonymisation and erasure name only the fixed tables and columns, bind every form, and change and delete exactly the same columns and rows (audit rows are re-pointed, never deleted); the integrity verifiers read the same rows and give the same counts; the Smart Score, WSJF, RICE, the scenario generator, frontier, evaluation and comparison give the same numbers; the PDFs are the same bytes for the same data. Every write was already one transaction with its audit row, and still is. `settings::refresh` and `spawn` and `modules_in_force` take the context; `db::Tx` gains `rollback`, so the subjects preview still answers an error when its rollback fails. No file under `src/controllers/` names the SeaORM API now but `controllers/mod.rs` (`db_err`, still called by `scheduled_jobs`). 317 lines naming SeaORM are gone; 26 files and 272 lines are left by batch 6's measure (24 by spec §8.3b's count, 23 that name the API); `AuditModel::record` has 3 SeaORM calls left in 2 files.
- **Deliberate changes:** a database failure carries sqlx's error text rather than SeaORM's in the log (the answers were the same). Lists are bound as one array (`= ANY($1)`) where SeaORM bound one parameter each (empty is false, as before); reads that used a few columns read only those; inserts whose row is not used return nothing, and some updates return only the columns used.
- **Found, not changed:** a vote and a benefit's realisation write a total computed from the row read before the update, so two at once lose one; an idea's conversion, a scenario's commit and rollback and a milestone's completion check a status read before the update, so two at once can both pass (two conversions make two proposals); a first handling mark and a first objective link read before their transaction, so a second at once meets the unique index (`409`); `webhook_deliveries` still hands `ctx.db` to `webhooks`.
- Not done: the other 23 files that name the API (the infrastructure: `app.rs`, `scheduled_jobs`, `retention`, `relay`, `webhooks`, `idempotency`, `query_count`, `flow_metrics`, `tasks/*`, the `models/*` behaviour impls, `db.rs`); `AuditModel::record`'s last 3 calls and `controllers::db_err`; the migration crate; the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature and the dependencies.
- Not verified: the races above (no test reaches them, before or after); a real database outage (only tables made unreadable); a failed rollback of the subjects preview; statement counts per route are not pinned for these routes; the time-zone ticker was run only through `tests/today_time_zone.rs`; no new request test was added. A throwaway test (not committed) dumped 1,713 answers, 17 tables and all 132 personal-data columns for five kinds of caller under a three-level marking scheme across every route of these files, then with 22 tables made unreadable in turn: the same before and after, once pids, times, hashes and the generator's tied candidates (in pid order) were normalised.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,265 unit tests, the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 tests, one run, no failure; nothing listened on port 8001 before the run, and the 3 MiB upload test passed) and the 57 other database test binaries (85 tests, `keycloak_entra_broker` and `keycloak_guard` with the `keycloak` feature), on a throwaway database; the status, OpenAPI-bodies and llms checks.

### 11 November (SeaORM removal, batch 16)

- **Cost rates, change requests, canvases, ceremonies and the oversight views move from SeaORM to sqlx (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `cost_rates`, `change_requests`, `canvas`, `ceremony`, `oversight` and `snapshots` now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction. Rates stay append-only, are found on the person's own table and then their role's, and price work in minor units per currency by the same rules, so the planned cost, the sandbox's labour totals and recorded actuals give the same numbers; the finance-only `404` is unchanged. The batch import's rate and fixed-cost writers have sqlx forms with the same signatures (`mspdi_batch` is unchanged at the call), each still one transaction with its audit row. Canvases, ceremonies and the commitment behave as before. The auditor's trail and evidence pack still write their audit row before reading and are not served when it cannot be written (NFR-6), an outage still `503`. `snapshots::capture` is `capture_on` (a `db::Session`), and the snapshot ticker is given the context. 362 lines naming SeaORM are gone; 39 files and 589 lines are left by batch 6's measure (37 files by name); `AuditModel::record` has 27 SeaORM calls left in 10 files.

Lists are bound as one array (`= ANY($1)`, and a relink's `NOT IN` as `<> ALL($1)`, true for an empty list as before) where SeaORM bound one parameter each; reads that used a few columns read only those; inserts whose row is not used return nothing.
- **Found, not changed:** a change-request step does not check the status in its update, so an approval and a rejection at once can both pass (the later wins, both audited); the baseline step takes the baseline and links it in two transactions; two promotions of one canvas item at once can both create a target; adding canvas items counts and then inserts (the cap and position can race); the batch import creates a rate table and its first rate in two transactions; the auditor's trail is audited before its `entity` filter is checked; a ceremony's audit row names the plan.

A throwaway test (not committed) dumped 1,367 answers and 18 tables for seven kinds of caller across every route of these files, then with 25 tables made unreadable in turn: the same before and after, once pids, times, hashes, `ETag`s and the cost plan's task order (keyed by random pid) were normalised, but for the error text above.

MS Project, Jira, Asana and pasted-row imports still store `mspdi:<UID>`, `jira:<key>` and `asana:<id>` (`asana:paste-…`), so importing the same file again updates what it made and creates nothing; the MS Project import's tasks, transitions and links are still one transaction with its audit row after it on the pool, failing the request when it cannot be written (not one transaction, NFR-6); the tracker import's writes and audit row are one transaction and its audit failure is passed on (`503` for an outage); the batch import is still not one transaction. Every upload and import route keeps its `axa::middleware::body_limit` ceiling. The MS Project export writes the same bytes for the same plan. Blueprint sync and the bulk job routes (request, cancel, claim, progress, finish, export audit, sweep, review queue) behave as before. Gone, with no caller left: the SeaORM `tba::transition_row` and `AuditModel::record_or_log` (`record_or_log_on` serves `bulk`; the census still counts both names and still allows `bulk.rs` two). ### 11 November (SeaORM removal, batch 14)

- **Attachments, email deliveries, scheduled reports and plan templates move from SeaORM to sqlx (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `attachments`, `email_deliveries`, `report_schedules` and `plan_templates` now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction. An upload is still refused before a byte is stored (allow-list, the cap behind the route's `axa::middleware::body_limit`, the content checks, the malware scan, `503` `scan_unavailable` when a configured scanner does not answer), its artifact, attachment and audit rows are still one transaction, a refused-by-scan upload is still `record_or_log_on`, downloads still re-check the SHA-256 and carry `content-disposition: attachment`, `nosniff` and `no-store`, and only the uploader or an administrator removes one. An email is still one row per message and recipient, sent once, retried on its schedule, with the sender in force recorded; what is stored and logged is unchanged. A scheduled report is still claimed with a conditional update and a failed run still recorded on the schedule and through `record_or_log_on`. Plan templates instantiate as before, and now write each task's transition with `tba::insert_transition_on`; the SeaORM `tba::transition_row` stays for `mspdi_import`, `tracker_import` and `blueprint_sync`. 223 lines naming SeaORM are gone; 52 files and 1,277 lines are left by batch 6's measure (50 files by name).
- **Deliberate changes:** a scheduled report run's artifact row, the schedule's `ok` outcome and its audit row are now one transaction (they were three statements), so a run whose audit row cannot be written leaves no artifact row and keeps the schedule's previous output. A database failure carries sqlx's error text rather than SeaORM's (same status; only on a failure), including what is stored for a failed report run. A template capture binds the parent pids as one array; counts are a plain `COUNT(*)`; the duplicate-name check reads only the names; writes whose row is not used return only its id.
- **Found, not changed:** the attachment search's `unindexed_attachments` counts hidden and deleted attachments too; the search checks access after reading 200 matches, so many hidden matches can crowd out readable ones; an attachment ignores its artifact's `deleted_at`; two simultaneous deliveries of one message to one recipient can both send; a permanently failed delivery is tried again when asked again; two simultaneous schedule creations can pass the per-person cap, and two template saves the name check; creating and removing a schedule are not audited; template instantiation is not one transaction and its undo ignores its own errors.
- Not done: the other 50 files (52 by the grep); `tba::transition_row`, and `AuditModel::record` and `record_or_log` (their callers are not yet moved); the migration crate; the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature and the dependencies.
- Not verified: the races above (no test reaches them, before or after); a real database outage (only tables made unreadable); statement counts per route are not pinned for these routes; no new request test was added. A throwaway test (not committed) dumped 729 answers, 85 function results and 16 tables for six kinds of caller across uploads accepted and refused every way, lists, searches, downloads and deletes, deliveries sent, retried, refused and listed, schedules created, refused, swept (twice at once too), failed, downloaded and removed, templates saved, listed, read, instantiated (and undone part-way), hidden and deleted, and thirteen tables made unreadable in turn, before and after: the same, once pids and times were normalised, but for a failed report run's error text and the one run, with `audit_logs` unreadable, that now leaves no artifact row.
- Verified, over this batch and the `record_on` fix below together: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,265 unit tests, the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 tests, one run, no failure; `requests::attachments::an_upload_larger_than_the_service_wide_body_limit_is_accepted_up_to_the_cap` passed, and nothing was listening on port 8001 before the run) and the 57 other database test binaries (85 tests; `keycloak_entra_broker` with `--features keycloak --ignored`, one test; `keycloak_guard` with `--features keycloak`, one test, not `#[ignore]`d), on a throwaway database, then dropped; `generate-status.sh`, `generate-openapi-bodies.py` and `generate-llms.py` checks.

### 11 November (SeaORM removal, the audit row's 503)

- **An audit row written through `AuditModel::record_on` keeps the `503` again (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b, §8.3a, NFR-7).** `record_on` wrapped sqlx's error as a plain model error, so where a caller passed it on a database outage answered `500` where SeaORM's `record` had answered `503` `unavailable` with `Retry-After: 5` (and a broken constraint `500` for its `409`/`422`). Batch 13 recorded it for the outbox transport and the plan export; it had also stood since batch 1 at 22 calls in `controlled_documents`, `processes`, `sandbox`, `hammock`, `cpm`, `custom_fields`, `discussion`, `handover`, `onboarding` and `task_relations`. `record_on` now fails with sqlx's own error (`axa::Error::Sqlx`) and those calls pass it on with `?`. `audit_failed` takes any displayable error, so the calls that refuse a write with it still answer `500` `audit_unavailable`. A unit test checks the `503` and `Retry-After` through `record_on`.
- Found, not changed: the five local `db_err`s that map a named constraint (`organizations`, `capacity_heatmap`, `deescalation`, `ledger`, `programme_gates`) answer `500` for an outage, as they did with SeaORM's error before SQ-1. No other moved helper wraps sqlx's error.
- Not verified: a real database outage against a running service (the unit test uses a pool on a closed port); no request test pins the `503` for these routes.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features) and the unit tests on this commit; the database suite and binaries ran once over this change and batch 14 together (see batch 14 above).

### 11 November (SeaORM removal, batch 13)

- **The plan record's core moves from SeaORM to sqlx (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `streaming` (create, update, soft delete and merge, and the recent events), `models/plans` (the writers and every finder), `models/merge_records`, `controllers/plans`, `plan_links` and `planner` now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction, as do `models/event_outbox` and `AuditModel::recent` and `for_entity`. Under the `outbox` transport a plan's write, its `axa_outbox` event and its audit row still share one transaction (a merge's two writes, two events and two audit rows too): `db::Tx::write_outbox` hands it to `axa::outbox::write`, the sqlx form of `write_seaorm` (the same statement and writer), and counts it in `query_count`. Under `memory` the write, the live event and the best-effort audit are as before. The SeaORM `plans::ActiveModel::update_data` and `soft_delete` are gone (`update_data_on`, the new `soft_delete_on` and `create` take a `db::Session`); the plan finders take the context, so `bulk`, `goals`, `lessons`, `tolerances`, `frameworks`, `governance`, `mspdi_batch`, `plan_templates` and `visibility/reports` changed at the call. Links and planned slots keep their writes and audit rows in one transaction.
- **Deliberate changes:** a database failure carries sqlx's error text rather than SeaORM's (same status; only on a failure), except that an audit row written through `AuditModel::record_on` (the outbox transport's, the plan export's) fails as a plain `500` where an unreachable database answered `503`. Lists SeaORM bound one parameter per value are bound as one array; the parent check, the ancestor walk and the planner's plan names read only the columns they use; the slot count is a plain `COUNT(*)`; removing a link or a slot returns the row's id rather than every column.
- **Found, not changed:** a plan may be created or moved under a soft-deleted parent; under `memory` a merge's two writes are two statements, and either transport writes the merge record after the merge with its failure only logged; promoting a proposal creates its plan outside the transaction that marks it promoted; the link cap and duplicate-URL check and the slot cap are checked before the insert, so two requests at once can pass them; removing a link or a slot reads it before its transaction; `list_children` and the three name-search finders have no caller.
- Not done: the other 54 files (56 by the grep); `models/plans` and `models/merge_records` keep their `ActiveModelBehavior` impls and `models/audit_logs` its SeaORM `record` and `record_or_log`, for the callers not yet moved; the migration crate; the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature and the dependencies.
- Not verified: the races above (no test reaches them, before or after); a database outage (only tables made unreadable); statement counts per route are not pinned for these routes; no new request test was added. A throwaway test (not committed) dumped 644 answers under the `memory` transport and 642 under `outbox`, and every row written (plans with every column and their three digests recomputed, audit, merge records, links, slots, plan modules, outbox events and payloads with their fingerprints recomputed) for seven kinds of caller across plan create, read, mask, export, list, search, duplicate check, match, update, merge and delete, links and slots added, refused, listed and removed, the finders with no route, and seven tables made unreadable in turn, before and after: the same, once pids, times, event ids and cursors were normalised and the search index's order was compared sorted, but for a direct `find_by_pid` call's error (`NotFound` for `EntityNotFound`, the same `404`).
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,264 unit tests, the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 tests, one run, no failure; `requests::attachments::an_upload_larger_than_the_service_wide_body_limit_is_accepted_up_to_the_cap` passed this time, and nothing was listening on port 8001 before the run) and the 57 other database test binaries (85 tests; `keycloak_entra_broker` with `--features keycloak --ignored`, one test; `keycloak_guard` with `--features keycloak`, one test, not `#[ignore]`d), on a throwaway database, then dropped; `generate-status.sh`, `generate-openapi-bodies.py` and `generate-llms.py` checks.

### 11 November (SeaORM removal, batch 12)

- **Automation, break-glass and notification sinks move from SeaORM to sqlx (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `automation` (rules, runs, scheduled actions and both sweeps), `break_glass`, `sinks` (registration, the delivery queue and the dispatcher) and the `exception_breaches` task now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction, each handler's writes and audit row (`AuditModel::record_on`) in one transaction, as they already were. With them go the last SeaORM callers of `capabilities::notify`, `sinks::enqueue` and `plan_access::is_none_of`, which are removed, as are the SeaORM finders and writers only `automation` used (now sqlx: `find_automation`, `find_scheduled_action`, `enabled_automations`, `due_actions`, `record_run_on`, `schedule_on`); `models/capabilities` keeps only its `ActiveModelBehavior` impls. Break-glass writes its grant and its notice (`notify_on`) on one sqlx transaction, so they still stand or fall together; its audit, daily limit, one live grant per plan, expiry, revoke and the open owner decision on `notify_ref` (a space after the colon is still accepted) are unchanged. A scheduled action is still claimed with a conditional update and a milestone-due pair with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so neither fires twice (FR-16d); an automation run's audit is still `record_or_log` (now `record_or_log_on`; the census still allows `automation.rs` two). Sink claims, retries, backoff, the masked destination and `transport_error` (no URL in a stored error) are unchanged. `plans::Model::update_data_on` is new, the sqlx twin of `update_data`. 249 lines naming SeaORM are gone; 61 files and 1,672 lines are left by batch 6's measure (59 files by name).
- **Deliberate changes:** a database failure carries sqlx's error text rather than SeaORM's (same status; only on a failure), including what is stored for a failed automation action (`automation_runs.detail.reason`) or scheduled action (`scheduled_actions.outcome`). Lists SeaORM bound one parameter per value (`IN (…)`, `NOT IN (…)`) are bound as one array; counts are a plain `COUNT(*)`; updates whose row is not used return only its id. A break-glass whose notice is switched off rolls back by dropping its transaction, so a failed rollback no longer answers `500` instead of the `422`.
- **Found, not changed:** the sink dispatcher does not claim a delivery before sending it, so the ticker and `POST /api/notification-sinks/dispatch` at once can send one twice; two sink registrations at once can pass the per-person cap; registering and removing a sink are not audited; `expire_review` matches reviews by subject pid alone, whatever their kind; break-glass's audit failure is a plain `500`, not counted in `audit_write_failed_total`, and a `notify_ref` with surrounding spaces is refused although the insert trims it; the exception-breach task notifies and then stamps the exception in two statements, so a failed stamp re-sends the notice; an automation's `add_label` and `shift_dependents` write the plan without an outbox or live event.
- Not done: the other 59 files; `streaming` keeps the SeaORM `plans::ActiveModel::update_data`; the migration crate; the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature and the dependencies.
- Not verified: the races above (no test reaches them, before or after); a real concurrent sweep by the ticker (two sweep requests at once were run, below); statement counts per route are not pinned for these routes; no new request test was added. A throwaway test (not committed) dumped 590 answers and every row written (audit, automations, runs, scheduled actions, notifications, sinks, sink deliveries, grants, tasks, plans and their integrity digests, reviews, exceptions, milestone claims, emails) for eight kinds of caller across rules and every action kind, board moves, timeframe changes, a submitted review, three milestone sweeps, scheduled actions created, cancelled and swept by two callers at once, break-glass refused, opened, revoked, expired and run to its daily limit, sinks dispatched to exhaustion, the breach task twice, and thirteen tables made unreadable in turn, before and after: the same, once pids, times and ports were normalised, but for the dispatcher's error text when `sink_deliveries` is gone.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,264 unit tests, the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 tests, one run, no failure; `requests::attachments::an_upload_larger_than_the_service_wide_body_limit_is_accepted_up_to_the_cap` passed this time, and nothing was listening on port 8001 before the run) and the 57 other database test binaries (85 tests; `keycloak_entra_broker` with `--features keycloak --ignored`, one test; `keycloak_guard` with `--features keycloak`, one test, not `#[ignore]`d), on a throwaway database, then dropped; `generate-status.sh`, `generate-openapi-bodies.py` and `generate-llms.py` checks.

### 11 November (SeaORM removal, batch 11)

- **The inbox moves from SeaORM to sqlx (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `collaboration` (reviews, assignees, the notification list), `feeds` (the calendar and Atom feeds and their tokens) and `notification_prefs` (preferences and the inbox) now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction, with each handler's writes and audit row (`AuditModel::record_on`) in one transaction, as they already were; their notifications go through `notify_on` on the pool, after commit and best effort, as before. `HiddenScope::notes_visible` (what an inbox, the notification list and the Atom feed leave out once a plan is hidden from the caller, OC-23) is now sqlx; `plan_access::is_none_of` keeps its SeaORM form for `automation` and gains a sqlx form, `push_none_of`. `models/capabilities` moves `find_review`, `find_notification` and `reviews_for_subject` (so `prioritization` changed at its one call). The notification kinds that cannot be switched off (`process_todo`, `process_notice`, `break_glass`; an open owner decision) are unchanged. 157 lines naming SeaORM are gone; 66 files and 1,921 lines are left by batch 6's measure (64 files by name).
- **Deliberate changes:** a database failure carries sqlx's error text rather than SeaORM's (same status; only on a failure). The subject checks, the calendar's plan names and slot tasks, the preference lookup and the inbox's daily kinds read only the columns they use; lists SeaORM bound one parameter per value (`IN (…)`) are bound as one array (`= ANY($1)`).
- **Found, not changed:** two simultaneous `PUT /api/me/notification-prefs` for the same choice can both insert, and the second answers `500` from the unique index; the review routes and the task assignment read their row before their transaction, so a racing change is overwritten; minting and revoking a feed token are not audited; the review list matches the caller's own invitations on a hidden plan case-sensitively, while answering one ignores case.
- Not done: the other 64 files; the SeaORM `notify` and `sinks::enqueue` stay, because `break_glass` calls `notify` inside its own SeaORM transaction and `automation` and the exception-breach task still call it, and `models/capabilities` keeps the finders and writers only `automation` uses; the migration crate; the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature and the dependencies.
- Not verified: the races above (no test reaches them, before or after); statement counts per route are not pinned for these routes; no new request test was added. A throwaway test (not committed) dumped 609 answers and every row written (audit, reviews, notifications, preferences, feed tokens, sink deliveries, tasks, watchers, outbox) for an administrator, an owner, a member of private plans, a writer who is a member of nothing and no token, across preferences, reviews and their consensus, assignment, workload, 288 notifications of nine kinds on visible and hidden subjects, the inbox, the notification list, both feeds and feed tokens, then again after the member lost two private plans (OC-23), then with five tables made unreadable in turn, before and after: the same, once pids, times, tokens and cursors were normalised and calendar events compared sorted.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,264 unit tests, the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 tests, one run, no failure; `requests::attachments::an_upload_larger_than_the_service_wide_body_limit_is_accepted_up_to_the_cap` passed this time, and nothing was listening on port 8001 before the run) and the 57 other database test binaries (85 tests; `keycloak_entra_broker` with `--features keycloak --ignored`, one test; `keycloak_guard` with `--features keycloak`, one test, not `#[ignore]`d), on a throwaway database, then dropped; `generate-status.sh`, `generate-openapi-bodies.py` and `generate-llms.py` checks.

### 11 November (SeaORM removal, batch 10)

- **The issue tracker moves from SeaORM to sqlx (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `issues`, `issue_configuration` and `custom_fields` now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction, with each handler's writes and audit row (`AuditModel::record_on`) in one transaction, as they already were. Role permissions, field rules per workflow state, plan types, translated labels and the private-plan checks are meant to give the same answer for every input: the same statuses, error bodies, JSON, list order (unordered reads stay unordered), soft-delete filters, and the issue save's guard (`409` when the status or type changed since it was read). Inserts list the columns SeaORM set and updates name the columns SeaORM's did; an update by id that finds no row is still an error. `custom_fields::store`, the SeaORM writer of custom values, is gone: the MSPDI batch import now calls `store_on` on the pool (each statement still on its own), and `PUT /api/custom-fields/values` calls it in its transaction. Every other function other modules call kept its signature, so no other caller changed. 240 lines naming SeaORM are gone; 69 files and 2,078 lines are left by batch 6's measure (67 files by name).
- **Deliberate changes:** a database failure carries sqlx's error text rather than SeaORM's (same status; only on a failure). The plan and catalogue existence checks and the type and status vocabularies read only the columns they use, and the status check binds the declaring workflows as one array (`= ANY($1)`) rather than one parameter per pid.
- **Found, not changed:** `PUT /api/custom-fields/values`'s audit failure maps to a plain model error, not `audit_failed`; a subject's custom values look up their definitions with no `ORDER BY`, so after a withdrawn key is declared again its label and `live`/`withdrawn` mark come from whichever definition the database returns first; the custom-field search scans 5,001 values in no set order, so a truncated search does not say which it left out; the MSPDI import's find-then-write per value can race with a second import.
- Not done: the other 67 files; the migration crate; the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature and the dependencies.
- Not verified: the issue save's `409` race (no test reaches it, before or after); statement counts per route are not pinned for these routes; no new request test was added. A throwaway test (not committed) dumped 699 answers and every row written (issues, issue types, roles, plan types, role members, custom field definitions and values, audit) for seven kinds of caller (administrator, plan-role members with different permissions, token roles gated by moves and by field rules, a writer with no role, a private plan's owner, no token) on a governed plan, a plan with field rules, a private plan and a plan limited to some types, before and after: the same, once pids and times were normalised and the search's random-pid list compared sorted.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,264 unit tests, the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 tests) and the 57 other database test binaries (85 tests; `keycloak_entra_broker` with `--features keycloak --ignored`, one test; `keycloak_guard` with `--features keycloak`, one test, not `#[ignore]`d), on throwaway databases, then dropped; `generate-status.sh`, `generate-openapi-bodies.py` and `generate-llms.py` checks. The first full `mod` run failed one test, `requests::attachments::an_upload_larger_than_the_service_wide_body_limit_is_accepted_up_to_the_cap`, the same test batch 9 saw fail once: its first request (`POST /api/plans`, before any upload) failed with "error writing a body to connection: Connection reset by peer (os error 54)" on the test server's port 8001. It passed five times run alone and in a second full `mod` run (220 of 220). The cause is not known; it fails before reaching any code this batch changed, but that is not proven.

### 11 November (SeaORM removal, batch 9)

- **The shared lookups move from SeaORM to sqlx (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `governance` and `discussion` now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction, with each handler's writes and audit row (`AuditModel::record_on`) in one transaction, as they already were; so do `plan_access` (the private-plan guard and `HiddenScope`), `find_live_plan` and `find_live_task` in `controllers/mod.rs`, and `governance::find_item`. Their signatures are unchanged (they take the context and use the pool), so none of their many callers changed. The privacy answers are meant to be the same for every caller, plan, membership, handling mark, break-glass grant and guest grant, and every lookup failure still fails closed (`404`, `503` or `500`, as before). `models/governance` loses its SeaORM finders, whose only caller was the governance controller.
- **Deliberate changes:** the visibility lookups bind their pid list as one array (`= ANY($1)`) instead of one parameter per pid, so a list longer than 65,535 pids is now answered instead of failing closed (no test reaches that size); `resolve_subject` and `readable_plans` read only the columns they use.
- **Found, not changed:** a comment edit whose audit row cannot be written answers a plain model error rather than `audit_failed`; reactions are not audited, and any failed reaction insert answers `created: false`; promoting a proposal creates the plan (through `streaming`, still SeaORM) outside the transaction that marks the proposal promoted; the intake forecast counts soft-deleted proposals among arrivals.
- Not done: the other 70 files; the migration crate; the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature and the dependencies. `plan_access::is_none_of` and `HiddenScope::notes_visible` still build SeaORM conditions for `automation`, `feeds`, `notification_prefs` and `collaboration`, and `controllers::db_err` stays for every unmoved controller. Promote still creates its plan through `streaming::create_and_emit`, and the duplicate check lists plans through `models::plans`, both SeaORM.
- Not verified: statement counts per route are not pinned for these routes; racing callers were not exercised beyond the existing tests; a pid list over 65,535 was not tried; no new request test was added. A throwaway test (not committed) dumped 4,348 answers and every row written, for eight kinds of caller (admin, member, member of some plans, live, revoked and expired break-glass grantees, a guest with granted, ungranted and revoked grants, a stage-denied outsider, no token), public, private, marked, staged, deleted and promoted plans, every subject kind, and each visibility table made unreadable in turn, before and after: the same.
- **A query-count guard went blind, and now counts every statement.** `requests::scale` counted only SeaORM's driver tracing spans, so once `/capacity/utilization`'s last SeaORM statements (the `HiddenScope` lookups) moved it counted zero and failed. It now counts through `query_count`, which sees SeaORM's statements (metric callback) and sqlx's (`db::Counted`) alike: the same assertions, over more statements. Not verified: that it still fails when a fan-out is put back in (not tried).
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,264 unit tests, the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 tests) and the 57 other database test binaries (85 tests; `keycloak_entra_broker` with `--features keycloak --ignored`, one test; `keycloak_guard` with `--features keycloak`, one test, not `#[ignore]`d), on a throwaway database, then dropped; `generate-status.sh`, `generate-openapi-bodies.py` and `generate-llms.py` checks. Of three full `mod` runs, the first failed only the scale guard above, the second (after its fix) failed `requests::attachments::an_upload_larger_than_the_service_wide_body_limit_is_accepted_up_to_the_cap` once, and the third passed all 220; that test passed three times run alone and in the first run, and the cause of the one failure was not found (its output was not kept), so it is not known whether it is this change or a flake.
