### 10 November (SeaORM removal, batch 3)

- **Ten more controllers move from SeaORM to sqlx (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `financials`, `funding`, `tolerances`, `thresholds`, `effort`, `timesheets`, `milestones`, `milestone_signoffs`, `announcements` and `components` now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction, with each handler's writes and audit row (`AuditModel::record_on`) in one transaction. The behaviour is meant to be unchanged: the same statuses, error bodies, JSON, ordering (an unordered SeaORM read stays unordered), limits, soft-delete and privacy checks, and an update or insert names only the columns SeaORM's did (`timesheets::set_state`, now on `&mut impl db::Session`, keeps its three shapes of update). 400 lines naming SeaORM in these files are gone; 121 files and 4,404 lines are left (batch 2's measure, without import lines). No caller changed, `ledger`'s call of `milestones::derive` included: every function other modules call already took the context.
- **One deliberate change:** `funding`'s three writes (create a funding source, retire or restore one, set a budget line's source) each wrote the row and its audit row as separate statements on the pool, so a failed audit row left the write in place; they now share one transaction, so the write is rolled back too. The error answered is the same 500.
- **Not changed, on purpose:** the timesheet findings left for the owner (the approved-week lock compares `person_ref` as exact text; anyone with plan write can record time for another person; a submitted week is not locked). **Found, not changed:** `funding`'s three audit failures and `thresholds`' decision map to a plain 500 rather than `audit_failed`, so `audit_write_failed_total` does not count them.
- Not done: the other 121 files; the migration crate; the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature and the dependencies. These files still reach SeaORM through other modules' helpers on the pool, never inside their own transactions: `find_live_plan`, `governance::find_item`, `plan_access`, `plans::Model::ancestor_pids`, `models::visibility::find_milestone` and `all_allocations_with_placeholders`, `workflow::in_force`, `cpm::load_network`, `escalations::insert` (in its own transaction), `confidence::find_exception`, `schedule_baselines::current`, `earned_value::subtree_ids`, `cost_risk::p80_by_currency`.
- Not verified: statement counts per route are not pinned for these routes; racing callers (two submissions of one timesheet week, two `PUT`s of one tolerance or threshold) were not exercised beyond the existing tests; no new request test was added (the existing tests reach every moved file: in `mod` the `components`, `effort`, `financials`, `tolerances`, `cpm`, `audit_failure` and `visibility` request tests, and the `funding`, `timesheets`, `organizations`, `private_aggregates`, `private_routes`, `director_packs` and `portfolio_evm` binaries); a failed audit row in `funding` rolling back its write was reasoned, not tested.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,264 unit tests, the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 tests) and the 57 other database test binaries with `--ignored` (83 tests; `keycloak_guard` and `keycloak_entra_broker` also with `--features keycloak`), on a throwaway database, then dropped; `generate-status.sh`, `generate-openapi-bodies.py` (unchanged, 228 bodies) and `generate-llms.py` checks.

### 10 November (SeaORM removal, batch 2)

- **Eight more controllers move from SeaORM to sqlx (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `okr`, `kpis`, `controls`, `value`, `benefit_validation`, `frameworks`, `key_persons` and `ledger` now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction, with each handler's writes and audit row (`AuditModel::record_on`) in one transaction. The behaviour is meant to be unchanged: the same statuses, error bodies, JSON, ordering (an unordered SeaORM read stays unordered), soft-delete and privacy checks, the same named-constraint mappings (`value_points_first` to 422, `frameworks_name_once` and `ccm_once` to 409), and an update names only the columns it changes. 523 lines naming SeaORM in these files are gone; 131 files and 4,804 lines are left (batch 1's measure). No caller changed: the three functions other modules call already took the context.
- **One deliberate change:** `benefit_validation`'s classification and validation each wrote the row and its audit row as separate statements on the pool, so a failed audit row left the write in place; they now share one transaction, so the write is rolled back too. The error answered is the same 500.
- Not done: the other 131 files; the migration crate; the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature and the dependencies. These files still reach SeaORM through other modules' helpers on the pool, never inside their own transactions: `find_live_plan`, `governance::find_item`, `plan_access`, `plans::Model::ancestor_pids`, `workflow::in_force`/`in_force_issue`, `plan_roles::standing`, `milestones::derive`, `earned_value::compute`. **Found, not changed:** `benefit_validation`'s two audit failures map to a plain 500 rather than `audit_failed` (so `audit_write_failed_total` does not count them); `PUT /api/key-persons/contingent` audits under a fresh random entity pid, so its audit row cannot be found from the person; batch 1's note had the call backwards (`value` calls `earned_value`, not the reverse).
- Not verified: statement counts per route are not pinned for these routes; racing callers (two `PUT`s of a framework link or of the same cost code) were not exercised beyond the existing tests; no new request test was added (the existing `mod`, `benefit_validation`, `framework_versions`, `funding` and `director_packs` tests cover these routes).
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,264 unit tests, the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 tests) and the 57 other database test binaries with `--ignored` (83 tests; `keycloak_guard` and `keycloak_entra_broker` also with `--features keycloak`), on a throwaway database, then dropped; `generate-status.sh`, `generate-openapi-bodies.py` (unchanged, 228 bodies) and `generate-llms.py` checks.

### 10 November (SeaORM removal, batch 1)

- **Eight controllers move from SeaORM to sqlx (task SQ-1, spec §8.3b).** `approvals`, `processes`, `controlled_documents`, `onboarding`, `handover`, `meetings`, `meeting_actions` and `lessons` now run every statement through `db::pool` or a `db::begin` transaction, and each handler's writes and audit row (`AuditModel::record_on`) share one transaction, as they did on SeaORM. The behaviour is meant to be unchanged: the same statuses, error bodies, JSON, ordering, paging, soft-delete and privacy checks, the same conditional updates (`… AND status = 'pending'`/`'planned'`), the same row locks (`FOR UPDATE`) and advisory locks, and an update names only the columns it changes (an update with none reads the row back, as SeaORM's did). 447 lines naming SeaORM in these files are gone; 139 files are left (147 before).
- New shared pieces: `db::Session` (a helper that runs several statements serves a transaction or the pool), `controllers::advisory_lock_on`, `capabilities::notify_on` and `sinks::enqueue_on`, the sqlx twins of the SeaORM helpers, which stay for the controllers not yet moved. `meetings::describe` takes the context, so its two calls in `status_updates` changed. `tasks.md` gains SQ-1 with the plan, and the owner's decision on carrying migration history across.
- Not done: the other 139 files; the migration crate (still `sea-orm-migration`); the entities, axa's `sea-orm` feature and the dependencies. These eight files still reach SeaORM through other modules' helpers on the pool, never inside their own transactions: `governance::find_item`, `workflow::in_force`, `discussion::resolve_subject`, `plan_access`, `guests::guest_covers_plan`, `plans::Model::find_by_pid`, and the post-commit notification goes through `notify_on` on the pool. `onboarding`'s `DELETE /api/onboarding/templates/{kind}` writes no audit row, before and after: found, not changed here.
- Not verified: statement counts per route are not pinned for these routes, so a change in how many statements a route runs (the handover now writes each row by `SELECT id` then `UPDATE … WHERE id`, as before; the close-out counts in SQL rather than loading rows) is not guarded by a test; behaviour under two racing callers was not exercised beyond the existing tests.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,264 unit tests (the raw-pool census included), the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 tests) and every other database test binary run with `--ignored` (55 binaries, 83 tests; `keycloak_guard` and `keycloak_entra_broker` also with `--features keycloak`), `generate-status.sh`, `generate-openapi-bodies.py` and `generate-llms.py` checks. No request test was added: the existing `approvals`, `processes`, `controlled_documents`, `references_privacy` and `mod` tests (onboarding, handover, meetings, lessons) cover these routes.

### 10 November (NC-1 slice 7: the access review and emergency access)

- **A screen for the access review and break-glass access (`/security/access`, linked from `/security`; NC-1 stays open).** The RG-9 and RG-10 routes existed only at the API. The access review (`GET /api/access-review`) is read only when the person asks, because every read is audited; it takes a plan id and a review age in calendar days (refused on the page as the service would), shows the date and how many grants stand and are due, lists them oldest first with "due for review" in words and a shape, and links to the service's own spreadsheet with its keep or revoke column (the table offers no export of its own). Emergency access says before its form that every use is recorded and audited, that the person named is told at once and the plan is not opened if they cannot be, that it lets the person see the plan only, and when it ends; it asks for the private plan's id, a reason of at least 20 characters, 1 to 240 minutes, a person to tell, and a tick that the person understands; the page is never looser than the service (the person to tell must have a UUID, which the service does not require but needs to deliver the notice) and its button is not a primary action. An opened grant's end is shown in the browser's time zone, which is named; the grants are listed newest first with their state in words and a shape, and one in force can be ended early (`POST /api/break-glass/{pid}/revoke`). A 401, 403, 404, 409, 422 or 429 is a catalogue sentence, never the service's text. New: five `PpmClient` methods and their types (bodies checked against `openapi.json` by `client-bodies.test.ts`), `src/lib/access-view.ts`, 85 catalogue keys in all eight page catalogues (Welsh and six other languages machine-written; the review CSVs are regenerated), a section in the UI `spec/index.md`, and the RG-9, RG-10 and NC-1 notes in `tasks.md`. The service is unchanged. **Not done:** recording a reviewer's decision (no service route); a plan picker for a private plan; a person picker; hiding either section from people without the right, or checking that the person to tell is not the caller (the page does not know the caller); a countdown as a grant runs out; paging past the 500 newest grants; offering emergency access from a plan that answered 404. **Not verified:** against a real service (the e2e stubs it), so a real eligible token, the notice reaching the second person and the spreadsheet download through the proxy are not seen; CI; no speaker has read the new strings in any language. **Found in the service, not changed:** the person to tell (`notify_ref`) is checked only for a `person:`/`worker:` prefix and some text, so a grant can be opened by naming a reference that is no one (`person:nobody`, or a UUID nobody holds), and the notice then reaches no inbox while the grant still stands, which weakens the second-person control; a reference with a space after the colon (`person: <uuid>`) passes the check but is not one `recipient_sub` reads, so the person's opt-out is not consulted and the notice is stored under a reference no inbox matches; the OpenAPI text for revoke says an ended grant is answered unchanged, but a grant that has expired without being revoked is stamped revoked and audited again; the reason's 1000-character limit counts the untrimmed text while the 20-character minimum counts the trimmed one; `GET /api/break-glass` returns at most the 500 newest grants with no paging, so an administrator cannot see older ones; a 409 on opening puts the existing grant's expiry in the service's English text in UTC.

### 10 November (axa 0.11)

- **axa 0.10.0 → 0.11.0, and `axa-test` 0.1 → 0.2.0.** `axum-test` stays at 21, which `axa-test` 0.2 uses; `jsonwebtoken` is unchanged. Only axa's `sea-orm` database feature is on (`idempotency` and `queue` bring in the `sqlx` crate, not the `sqlx` feature), so `AppContext::db` is still the SeaORM connection and nothing that uses it changed. No migration.
- **Operators: settings renamed** (read [Upgrading to axa 0.11](operations/upgrade-and-migration.md#upgrading-to-axa-011) before deploying). `DATABASE_URL` → `DB_URL`; `LOCO_ENV` → `APP_ENV` (only `APP_ENV` is read); `BODY_LIMIT_BYTES` → `HTTP_REQUEST_BODY_LIMIT_BYTE_COUNT`; `HEADER_READ_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` → `HTTP_REQUEST_HEADER_READ_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`; `HTTP_CSP` → `HTTP_CONTENT_SECURITY_POLICY` (neither does anything here: the policy is `http_security`'s). axa ignores the old names; **the service now refuses to start, in every environment, when one of the first four is set and its new name is not**, naming both (`app::renamed_settings`, also run by `check`), so a deployment not yet renamed cannot fall back to a default database, environment or limit. New: `SHUTDOWN_DRAIN_SECONDS` (0, unchanged behaviour), documented in `operations/deployment.md`. Our scripts follow: `scripts/backup.sh` and `scripts/restore-test.sh` read `DB_URL` and stop if only `DATABASE_URL` is set; `restore-test.sh -t` exports `RESTORED_DB_URL` (was `RESTORED_DATABASE_URL`); `restore.sh`'s argument is named `TARGET_DB_URL`; the chaos test sets `DB_URL`. The database tests read `DB_URL` (`DB_URL=postgres://loco:loco@localhost:5432/project_portfolio_management_test cargo test --test mod -- --ignored --test-threads=1`); `AXA_TEST_DB_URL` is axa's own and not used here.
- **Operators: health routes renamed.** `/_ping` and `/_health` → `/_health/live`; `/_readiness` → `/_health/ready`; new `/_health/startup` (database only; `503` `not_started` until it has answered once). The old routes answer `404`, or `401` with login on, as they are no longer public. `/_health/ready` answers `503` `shutting_down` from the moment shutdown begins. Repointed: the Dockerfile `HEALTHCHECK` (live), the three `compose.yaml` healthchecks (ready), the CI container smoke test (all three, and `/_readiness` must not be `200`), the chaos test (live), `scripts/seed-demo.mjs` (ready), our login exemption list (`auth::enforcement::is_public_path`) and the rate-limit exemption (axa's list, which moved with it), and every document that named them (runbook, deployment, upgrade, INSTALL, README, the spec, `agents/restful.md`, the skill, the site's copy). The UI calls no health route.
- **Fixed, a regression axa 0.11 brought:** axa's stack now binds every request body to the service-wide 2 MiB limit, so the six routes that raised it with Axum's `DefaultBodyLimit` (attachment upload, plan import, MS Project import and batch import, Jira and Asana import) refused anything over 2 MiB with `413`. They use `axa::middleware::body_limit(post(..), ceiling)`. A new request test uploads 3 MiB under a 4 MiB cap (it failed with `413` before the fix), and a census test keeps `DefaultBodyLimit` out of the controllers.
- **Headers.** axa 0.11's new `permissions-policy` is turned off (`config.http.permissions_policy = None`, beside CSP, CORS and HSTS), so `http_security` stays the one source and `HTTP_PERMISSIONS_POLICY` changes nothing; our list gains `browsing-topics=()` and a unit test holds it equal to axa's default. axa's new `cross-origin-opener-policy` and `cross-origin-resource-policy` are `same-origin`, the values we already sent; ours only fill a gap, so nothing is sent twice. Credential headers are marked sensitive and a response that sets a cookie is `no-store` (the service sets no cookie).
- **Errors.** A database that cannot serve for now (no free connection, a lost connection, a read-only, shutting-down or full server) answers `503` `unavailable` with `Retry-After: 5`, not `500`, through our `db_err` and plain-sqlx paths alike. The spec (§8.3a), `agents/restful.md`, the service spec and the runbook say so; the shipped alert already counts `5xx`.
- Not done: our own `401` (the PASETO, OIDC and Keycloak guard is this service's, not axa's auth crates) still carries no `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`, which RFC 9110 asks for. A write whose audit row fails because the pool is exhausted still answers `500` `audit_unavailable`, not `503`. The OpenAPI document lists no `503` for database trouble and documents no health route (it never did). The 0.1 → 0.2 outbox index clash (migration 109) is still not fixed or reported upstream.
- Not verified: a real drain under Kubernetes (`SHUTDOWN_DRAIN_SECONDS` was not exercised); a `503` from a really exhausted or lost database through the router (unit test of the mapping only); the Fluvio relay and Keycloak container tests (need a broker and a container runtime); the container image build and the compose stack (CI's container job will run the new smoke checks); the chaos test.
- Also: `src/openapi_bodies.rs` is regenerated; it was out of date with the comment-delete wording of the change before this one.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all targets, all features), 1,264 unit tests (new: the old health paths are not public, the probe exemptions, the renamed-settings refusal, axa's headers off, the permissions policy equal to axa's, the `DefaultBodyLimit` census, the `503` mapping), `cargo deny` (advisories, bans, licenses and sources ok; crates.io only), the whole database suite (`mod`, 220 tests, with `DB_URL`) and the other 57 database binaries as CI's `db` job runs them (83 tests), `keycloak_guard` with the `keycloak` feature; `generate-status`, `generate-openapi-bodies`, `generate-llms` and the site's `sync-content` all `--check` clean, and `check-links` finds no broken link. The binary, started with `APP_ENV=development` and `DB_URL`, answered `200` on `/_health/live`, `/_health/ready` and `/_health/startup` and `404` on `/_readiness`, `/_ping` and `/_health`, with one each of `permissions-policy`, `cross-origin-opener-policy` and `cross-origin-resource-policy`; started with only `DATABASE_URL`, it refused to start, naming `DB_URL`.

### 10 November (NC-1 slice 6: my timesheet and approving one)

- **A screen for timesheets and their approval (`/me/timesheet`, linked from `/me`; NC-1 stays open).** The LT-11 routes existed only at the API. The page shows the caller's own week (`GET /api/timesheets?week=`) as a row per plan and a column per day, Monday to Sunday (the service's ISO week, whatever the person's first day of the week), each row's total with its billable and not billable halves, the day totals, and a sentence with both halves and the total, so the two are never merged unseen; times are the service's whole minutes written as hours and minutes; an empty week is a sentence, never zeros; entries in plans the reader cannot see are counted. "This week" is the week of today in the browser's time zone; weeks move back and forward or are typed (`2026-W46`), and one the service would refuse is refused on the page. The week is sent for approval (`POST /api/timesheets/submit`) when it is open or sent back and has time in it; the status is in words and a shape; a sent-back week shows the approver's reason; an approved week says it is final and locked. Below, an approver gives a `person:` or `worker:` reference, reads that person's week and approves it or sends it back (`POST /api/timesheets/decide`); sending back without a reason, and deciding one's own week, are refused on the page. A 403, 409 or 422 is a catalogue sentence, never the service's text. New: three `PpmClient` methods and their types (bodies checked against `openapi.json` by `client-bodies.test.ts`), `src/lib/timesheet-view.ts`, `src/lib/components/TimesheetGrid.svelte`, `formatWeekdayDate` in `locale-format.ts`, 76 catalogue keys in all eight page catalogues (two of them plural groups; Welsh and six other languages machine-written; the review CSVs are regenerated), a section in the UI `spec/index.md`, and the LT-11 and NC-1 notes in `tasks.md`. The service is unchanged. **Not done:** recording, editing or deleting time entries on a screen; a list of the weeks waiting for a decision (the service has no such query, so an approver must know the person's reference); a person picker or names for a reference; the time reports (RM-10); a week in the address; hiding the approver section from people without the right. **Not verified:** against a real service (the e2e stubs it), so how the default policy answers a write-level user on these routes is not seen; CI; no speaker has read the new strings in any language. **Found in the service, not changed:** the approved-week lock compares the time entry's `actor_ref` with the week's stored `person_ref` as exact text, and the time-entry create path checks only the `person:`/`worker:` prefix, so an entry for the same person written as `worker:<id>` or with the id in capitals is accepted into an approved week (and is not in that week's grid either); `POST /api/plans/{pid}/time-entries` does not tie `actor_ref` to the caller, so anyone who may write to a plan can record time in another person's week; a submitted week is not locked, so time recorded after submission is in what the approver approves without having been submitted; `GET /api/timesheets` echoes the `week` query as given rather than the parsed week.

### 10 November (a comment is deleted only by an author who can still read it)

- **Fixed: deleting a comment (`DELETE /api/comments/{pid}` and `POST …/delete`) checked only that the caller wrote it.** An author removed from a private plan could still delete their comment there by its pid, which also told them it still existed. Delete now resolves the subject first, as edit and history already did, so that author gets `404`. Found by the NC-1 slice 5 review. Spec: the service spec's §9.3 note on comments; LT-4 in `tasks.md`.
- Not done: the slice's other findings are open: `GET /api/comments` and `…/history` return the stored rows with the internal `id` and `deleted_at`; no route tells a client the deployment's attachment size limit.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all features), `tests/plan_access.rs` (it fails with the old delete: `200`, not `404`), the `discussion` request tests and the whole database suite.

### 10 November (NC-1 slice 5: a task's discussion and files)

- **A task's comments, reactions, followers and files on its page (`/plans/[pid]/tasks/[tpid]`; NC-1 stays open).** The comments and reactions (LT-4), attachments (LT-5) and watchers (RM-3) existed only at the API. A new component, `Discussion.svelte`, written for any subject kind and mounted on the task page only, follows and stops following (the `POST …/unwatch` twin; without a sign-in it says to sign in), toggles the five reactions (counts and names in words, the `POST …/remove` twin), lists every page of comments oldest first, posts one, lets the author edit one (earlier versions shown) and delete one after a question (the `POST …/delete` twin), and lists, attaches and removes files. A file is only ever a link to the service's own download, which says `attachment` and `nosniff`; nothing uploaded is drawn in the page. An upload sends the file itself with its type; the page refuses an empty file, one over 10 MiB and a type outside the service's twelve before sending. A 401, 403, 404, 413, 422 and 503 are catalogue sentences, never the service's text. The UI container now sets `BODY_SIZE_LIMIT=10304K` (the service's transport ceiling for uploads), as adapter-node's 512 KiB default would have refused most files at the proxy; it applies to every route of the UI server. New: fifteen `PpmClient` methods and their types (bodies checked against `openapi.json`), `ApiClient.followPages` and a `Blob` raw body, `src/lib/discussion-view.ts`, 90 catalogue keys in all eight page catalogues (Welsh and six other languages machine-written; the review CSVs are regenerated), a section in the UI `spec/index.md`, and the LT-4, LT-5, RM-3 and NC-1 notes in `tasks.md`. The service is unchanged. Verified here: lint, svelte-check, vitest (1068 passed, 3 skipped) and the full Playwright suite (1060 passed, none flaky). **Not done:** the section on any other subject kind; `GET /api/me/watching`; attachment search (PL-8); names for a `sub`; hiding Edit and Delete on other people's comments; live updates; reading a deployment's own attachment cap; several files at once or upload progress. **Not verified:** against a real service (the e2e stubs it), so a real upload through the proxy and the raised body limit, and the default policy's answer to a write-level user, are not seen; the container image was not built; CI; no speaker has read the new strings in any language. **Found in the service, not changed:** deleting a comment (`DELETE` and `POST /api/comments/{pid}/delete`) checks only that the caller is the author and does not re-run `resolve_subject`, so an author who has lost read access to the plan can still delete their comment by its pid (editing re-checks); `GET /api/comments` and `GET /api/comments/{pid}/history` return the stored rows as they are, with the internal numeric `id` and `deleted_at`; the attachment cap can be changed by `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES`, but no route tells a client what it is, so a page can only check the default.

### 10 November (notification documentation corrected)

- The OpenAPI descriptions of `channel` on `PUT /api/me/notification-prefs` and on its two reset forms said a missing channel meant every channel, or the kind-wide choice; the code means `in_app` (`notification_prefs::DEFAULT_CHANNEL`), and they now say so. FR-16d no longer says notifications are in-app only: since LT-6 and RM-11 they also go to a person's webhook, chat and email destinations. Found by the NC-1 slice 4 review.
- Not done: the other findings of that review are open: `process_todo`, `process_notice` and `break_glass` are sent but cannot be switched off (a decision for the owner, for `break_glass` above all), and `GET /api/me/notification-prefs` returns its raw rows, with the internal `id` and `sub`.
- Verified: `openapi.json` regenerated by its unit test, fmt, clippy; the descriptions only changed.

### 10 November (a failed delivery no longer stores its secret URL)

- **Fixed: a transport failure on an outbound webhook or a notification sink stored reqwest's error text, which names the full request URL.** A chat webhook's token is in its URL, so `GET /api/me/notification-sinks/{pid}/deliveries` (to the sink's owner) and the webhook delivery log (to administrators) showed the secret that the sink's masked destination hides. Both now store the error without the URL (`webhooks::transport_error`). Found by the NC-1 slice 4 review. Spec: `spec/09-api-surface.md`.
- Not done: rows already stored keep their old text (no migration rewrites `last_error`); the email path's errors are unchanged (they come from the email sender, not reqwest, and were not shown to carry the address).
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all features), the unit tests (one new: a real refused send to `127.0.0.1:1/hooks/s3cret-token` names the URL in reqwest's text and not in the stored one), and the `sinks` and `webhook_redelivery` database binaries.

### 10 November (NC-1 slice 4: my notifications)

- **A screen for the caller's own notifications (`/me/notifications`, linked from `/me`; NC-1 stays open).** The inbox (`GET /api/me/inbox`), the notification preferences (PL-5) and the delivery sinks (LT-6, RM-11) existed only at the API. The page shows the inbox with the person's daily digests applied in the browser's time zone, can show only unread lines and marks a line read (every notification it stands for, through the existing `POST /api/notifications/{pid}/read`); switches each kind on or off per channel (inbox, signed webhook, chat, email), sets the daily digest and puts a kind back to the default (the `POST …/reset` twin); and lists, adds and removes the person's destinations (the `POST …/delete` twin) with their recent deliveries. A destination is a secret: the page shows only the masked form the service returns, clears the box once it is saved, and never shows a delivery's `last_error`, the service's text, which can quote the destination's URL. Before sending, the page checks the service's rules (an email address; otherwise https to a public host with no credentials and no local, private, link-local or internal address; a chat for Telegram; at most ten). A 403, 404 or 422 is a catalogue sentence, never the service's text; a sign-in identity the service cannot address says so. New: eight `PpmClient` methods and their types (bodies checked against `openapi.json` by `client-bodies.test.ts`), `src/lib/notifications-view.ts`, four enum-label groups, 105 catalogue keys in all eight page catalogues (Welsh and six other languages machine-written; the review CSVs are regenerated), a section in the UI `spec/index.md`, and the LT-6, PL-5 and NC-1 notes in `tasks.md`. The service is unchanged. Verified here: lint, svelte-check, vitest (1054 passed) and the full Playwright suite (1048 passed). **Not done:** pausing a destination (the service has no route for it), a test send, marking everything read at once, paging the inbox past 500 rows, a link from a line about a task or a review, a confirmation before removing a destination. **Not verified:** against a real service (the e2e stubs it), so how the default policy answers a write-level user on these routes is not seen; CI; no speaker has read the new strings in any language. **Found in the service, not changed:** the `process_todo`, `process_notice` and `break_glass` notifications are not in `notification_prefs::KINDS`, so they cannot be switched off; a sink delivery's `last_error` keeps reqwest's error text, which can carry the full destination URL, and `GET …/deliveries` returns it; the OpenAPI descriptions of the preference body's and reset's `channel` say a missing one means every channel, but the code reads it as `in_app`.

### 10 November (a task's flow type can be edited)

- **Fixed: `PUT /api/plans/{pid}/tasks/{t_pid}` checked `flow_type` and then dropped it**, so a declared Flow Framework type (FR-31) could be set only when the task was made, and the task view never returned it, so no client could see it. Now a type replaces it, `null` withdraws it, and leaving it out keeps it: unlike the other fields of that `PUT`, which it replaces, so the task editor (NC-1 slice 3), which does not show the type, does not erase it. The task view returns `flow_type`. Spec: `spec/05-domain-model.md` (Flow Distribution).
- Not done: the UI does not show or edit the flow type (the task editor sends no `flow_type`, which now keeps it); other task fields keep their replace-on-`PUT` behaviour.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all features), a unit test of the three cases, a database test (`a_task_edit_keeps_changes_or_withdraws_its_flow_type`: kept, changed, withdrawn, and `unclassified` refused), and the whole database suite (`mod`, 219 tests). Not run: the other database test binaries (none touches a task's flow type).

### 10 November (NC-1 slice 3: task editing and links between tasks)

- **A task is edited, and linked to other tasks, on its own page (`/plans/[pid]/tasks/[tpid]`), reached from each board card's title (NC-1 stays open).** The task edit (`PUT …/tasks/{t_pid}`) and the task-relation routes (LT-10) existed only at the API; the board could create and move a task but not edit it, and the task page showed a task read-only with its links to outside systems. The page now has one edit form (title, description, assignee, story points, due date). The service's `PUT` replaces every field and clears one left out, so the body always carries the sprint, component and parent task as loaded, and never a status (status stays the board's `PATCH` move). A second section lists the task's links to other tasks in words from its own side ("Blocks" / "Blocked by", "Comes before" / "Comes after", "Related to", "Duplicate of" / "Duplicated by", "Copied from" / "Copied to"), counts those to tasks in plans the caller cannot read, adds a link to another task of the plan and removes one. The page checks the service's rules before calling: a title of at most 1024 bytes, a `person:`/`worker:` UUID reference or no assignee, whole points from 0 to 100, no second copy of a link, no two-task loop of `blocks`/`precedes`, and `precedes` dates in order. A 403, 404, 409 or 422 is shown as a catalogue sentence, never the service's text. The task routes take no `If-Match`, so the edit is last-writer-wins and there is no 412. New: `UpdateTaskBody`, `TaskRelationBody` and four client methods (checked against `openapi.json` by `client-bodies.test.ts`), `src/lib/task-edit-view.ts`, 53 catalogue keys in all eight page catalogues (Welsh and six other languages machine-written; the review CSVs are regenerated), a section in the UI `spec/index.md`, and a note on LT-10 in `tasks.md`. No FR's not-built note named this screen. The service is unchanged. Verified here: lint, svelte-check (0 errors, 0 warnings), vitest (1035 passed, 3 skipped) and the full Playwright suite (1034 passed). **Not done:** editing the sprint, component or parent task on this page; a link to a task in another plan, or an incoming link made from this side; a confirmation before removing a link; links shown on the board's cards; custom fields; an assignee picker. **Not verified:** against a real service (the e2e stubs it); CI; no speaker has read the new strings in any language.

### 10 November (NC-1 slice 2: plan change requests)

- **A screen to draft, submit, decide and withdraw a plan's change requests (`/plans/[pid]/change-requests`; NC-1 stays open).** The change-request routes (IR-4) existed only at the API, and the all-plans register `/change-requests` is read-only; its rows and the plan page now link here. The page lists the plan's change requests, has one form to draft one and edit a draft (only a draft can be edited; every field is sent because the service replaces them all, and any what-if edits already on the request go back unchanged rather than being cleared), and opens a request's steps by its status as the service's state machine allows: submit naming the approver, start the review, record an approve or reject decision with a reason, withdraw. The page checks the service's rules before calling (title, description and reason; whole business days within ±3650; a cost with its currency, in the currency's own minor unit; an approver who is a `person:` or `worker:` reference and not the requester; a decision reason of at least 10 characters). A 403, 409 or 422 is shown as a catalogue sentence (who may take the step, that the request has moved on, that the input was not accepted), never the service's text. New: `ChangeRequestBody`, submit and decision bodies and seven client methods (checked against `openapi.json` by `client-bodies.test.ts`), `src/lib/change-request-view.ts`, 56 catalogue keys in all eight page catalogues (Welsh and six other languages machine-written; the review CSVs are regenerated), a section in the UI `spec/index.md`, and FR-14's note. `welsh.spec.ts` and `every-locale.spec.ts` now allow the Welsh "cost" (the change-request kind, the same word in Welsh and already on the unit catalogue's allow-list). The service is unchanged. Verified here: lint, svelte-check, vitest and the full Playwright suite (1024 passed). **Not done:** marking an approved change implemented, links to issues and risks, the what-if preview, a baseline for an approved change; a confirmation before withdrawing; an approver picker; hiding steps the caller may not take (the page does not know who the caller is, so the service's 403 is what stops them). **Not verified:** against a real service (the e2e stubs it); CI; no speaker has read the new strings in any language.

### 10 November (axa 0.10)

- **axa 0.9.1 → 0.10.0.** The test helpers left axa's `testing` feature for their own crate: the dev-dependency is now `axa-test` 0.1, and the 121 test files import `axa_test::prelude::*` instead of `axa::testing::prelude::*`. axa's parts were renamed (`axa-outbox` is now `axa-transactional-outbox`, `axa-idempotency` is `axa-idempotency-key`, and so on); the `axa::` paths, features and table names are the same, so there is no migration and no change in behaviour. `axum-test` stays at 21, which `axa-test` uses.
- Not done: the 0.1 → 0.2 outbox index-name clash that migration 109 works round is not fixed upstream in 0.10, and is not yet reported upstream.

### 9 November (axa 0.9: the outbox keeps a record)

- **axa 0.7 → 0.9.1**, with `axa-outbox` 0.2, `axa-idempotency` 0.2.1 and `axa-worker` 0.2. `axum-test` stays at 21, which axa 0.9 still uses.
- **The event outbox is now a record, kept with no end date (owner decision).** Every event, its payload (with its `actor`) and every attempt are only ever inserted, into `axa_outbox_events`, `axa_outbox_payloads` and `axa_outbox_attempts`, whose triggers refuse `UPDATE`, `DELETE` and `TRUNCATE`. Sent events are no longer swept: `EVENT_RETENTION_DAYS` does nothing, and the relay warns when it is set. The retention schedule (class `EVT`), the personal-data inventory, `src/subjects.rs` and the persistence spec say so. The operator's id on a release (`axa_outbox_attempts.by`) is new personal data that the subject tools neither search nor erase, as its table refuses changes. It is listed as excluded, with that reason.
- **Migration 109** makes the record and imports `axa_outbox`: a `sent` row as `delivered`, `failed` as `failed`, `pending` or `claimed` as `imported`, which the relay then sends. It is safe to run twice and cannot be rolled back (`db down` errors; restore the backup). Stop every relay before it runs. `axa_outbox` is left, no longer written or swept, for a later release to drop.
- **Fixed in our migration, an upgrade bug in axa:** 0.1 named an index `axa_outbox_owed`, the name 0.2 gives a table, so 0.2's `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` made nothing and its next statement failed. Migration 109 renames that index to `axa_outbox_v01_owed` first. Migration 108 now carries 0.1's setup SQL itself, as `axa::outbox::SETUP_STATEMENTS` means 0.2's tables under axa 0.9.
- **The relay** passes `decide::deliver_all` and `record::nothing`, so it applies no policy before an event goes and writes no audit row of its own; the attempts table records what went out. A sink error is logged in full and kept on the attempt only as the code `sink_unavailable`.
- **`task outbox_retry_failed`** releases each failed event with `axa::outbox::release`, all in one transaction, and `apply:true` now needs `by:<who>` and `reason:<code>`, which the record keeps on each release. The dry run reads the record.
- `/events/recent` and webhook redelivery read the record; redelivering an erased payload is `422`. `ppm_event_outbox_rows` gains `held` and `tampered`. `db truncate` drops and remakes the record, as it refuses `DELETE`.
- **Idempotency (NFR-7):** a retry of a run still going, or lost to a crash, is `409` `idempotency_in_progress` until the key expires a day later, not for a minute, so a write never runs twice under one key. `Retry-After` grows with how long the first has run. The spec and API surface tell clients to send a new key once they have checked the write did not happen.
- Not done: nothing calls `axa::outbox::erase`, so a person's `actor` in a payload is never removed; nothing audits a release beyond the record's own attempt row; the relay applies no consent or policy decision; `axa_outbox` and `event_outbox` are not dropped; no data migration test runs in CI.
- Not tested here: the day-long hold of a lost idempotent run (axa-idempotency's own tests cover it; staging a crash in ours would test only the staging); a release under the Fluvio sink (`fluvio_relay` needs a broker).
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all features), 1,256 unit tests; `requests::event_outbox`, 5 tests, each state reached as a relay reaches it; migration 109 on the test database, which had 0.1's index, and on a throwaway database seeded with `sent`, `pending`, `failed` and `claimed` 0.1 rows, which came across as `delivered`, `pending`, `failed` and `pending` with all four payloads, a second run changing nothing and the record refusing `DELETE`; promtool on the alert rules; the whole database suite (`mod`, 218 tests) and the 57 other database test binaries.

- **Documents that described the service as loco.rs now say axa**: `llms.json` (summary and stack, which also named loco.rs 1.2), `agents/restful.md` (health routes, JSON rejections), `agents/subprojects.md`, `agents/review-checklist.md`, the service's `index.md`, the Dockerfile's healthcheck comment, and the CTO page in all 11 locales, where only the name changed. The service's `AGENTS.md` said axa 0.1; it is 0.7. Dated history (`tasks.md`, the task log in `spec/13-tasks.md`, past entries in the service spec) is left as written, and the database user and password `loco` are unchanged.
- Not done: no newer Rust syntax was adopted; nothing in clippy or the code pointed at a 1.97 feature to use. No MSRV check runs in CI.
- Verified: `cargo +1.97 check --all-targets` for the matcher and the service; `generate-llms.py --check`, the translation review checks and the site's content sync.

### 9 November (alert rules)

- **Prometheus alert rules ship in `operations/prometheus/alerts.yml`** (NFR-7): audit rows not written (critical), visibility lookups failing closed, five or more lockouts in 15 minutes, more than 5% `5xx` for 10 minutes, a failed scheduled job (per job), and, while a relay runs, failed outbox rows, an event owed over 5 minutes and outbox counts stale for 2 minutes. The outbox rules take `max()`, as every relay replica reports the same database-wide counts. `alerts.test.yml` holds promtool unit tests for each rule; CI's docs job runs `promtool check rules` and `promtool test rules` (Prometheus 3.7.3, pinned by digest). A unit test in `src/metrics.rs` fails if a rule names a metric the registry does not render. The metric inventory in `src/metrics.rs` now lists `audit_write_failed_total` and `visibility_lookup_failed_total`, which it had left out.
- Not done: the thresholds are guesses, not measured on real traffic; no rule for the scrape itself (`up`), the database, the broker or the flow gauges; no Alertmanager routing; the rules have not been loaded into a running Prometheus.

### 9 November (lockouts counted)

- **Each failed-login lockout is now counted once in `client_address_lockouts_total`** (`/metrics.prom`) and logged at `warn`, on axa's first refusal for that address in its window, not on every refused request after it. The log line leaves the address out, as it is personal data. This closes the lockout entry's "nothing counts a lockout".
- Not done: no audit row is written for a lockout; the counter has no label, so it cannot say which route the guessing was on; nothing alerts on it.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all features), 1,254 unit tests (one new: three refused requests in a row are one lockout, and an allowed response is none). On the binary in production a lockout logged the warning once; the counter itself was not read there, as `/metrics.prom` needs a token the test key set cannot sign.

### 9 November (`task outbox_retry_failed`)

- **`project-portfolio-management task outbox_retry_failed`** lists the event outbox's `failed` rows (their count, and the first 50 with topic, kind, attempts and last error) and changes nothing; `apply:true` puts every one back to pending with its attempts reset (`axa::outbox::Outbox::retry_failed`), so the relay sends it again in its record's order. It replaces the raw `UPDATE` that `operations/deployment.md` gave. An argument other than `apply` is refused; as with `retention_purge`, an `apply:` value other than `true`, `yes` or `1` is a dry run. It is in the task census, decided for production.
- Not done: it retries all failed rows or none, not one topic or row; it is not a route, so retrying needs a shell on a machine that reaches the database; nothing audits who ran it.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all features), 1,253 unit tests (three new) and the census; `requests::event_outbox`, where a dry run lists a seeded failed row and leaves it, and applied it is `pending` with no attempts; the binary, dry (`1 failed`, the row and its error), applied (`1 back to pending`), and refusing `bogus:1`.

### 9 November (the outbox in `/metrics.prom`)

- **A running relay now puts `axa-outbox`'s own counts in `/metrics.prom`** every 30 seconds: `ppm_event_outbox_rows{status}` (`pending`, `claimed`, `failed`), `ppm_event_outbox_oldest_owed_seconds` (`0` with none owed) and `ppm_event_outbox_last_refresh_timestamp_seconds`. A `failed` row is one no relay will send until an operator retries it, so `failed` above zero is the alert the outbox commit said was missing; `operations/deployment.md` says what to alert on.
- With no relay running the family is absent, never a `0` nobody measured: each gauge is a vector, label-less where it has no label. Counts only, as every scraper can read them. A failed read is logged and skipped, so the refresh time goes stale and says so. The counts share the relay's own two connections.
- Not done: no alert rule ships (the service has none); with several relays each publishes the same table-wide counts from its own process; `retry_failed` still has no route or task; a relay dying mid-claim is still untested.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all features), the unit tests (one new: the family is absent until published, then the three series, and none owed is `0`), and `requests::event_outbox`, where a running count publishes the failed row's count and stops when told.

### 9 November (the Compose database offers TLS)

- **`compose.yaml`'s PostgreSQL now offers TLS, and the API connects with `sslmode=require`.** Since axa 0.7, production refuses a `DATABASE_URL` that leaves the machine without `sslmode`, and the Compose stack runs in production, so `migrate` stopped at boot: the container smoke and ZAP jobs both failed on it, the first time CI ran this branch. `ci/postgres-tls/Dockerfile` is the same pinned `postgres:18-alpine` plus `openssl`; its entrypoint makes a self-signed certificate (P-256, ten years) on first start, beside the data on the existing `postgres-data` volume, and Postgres runs with `ssl=on` and TLS 1.3 at least.
- `sslmode=require` encrypts the link but does not check the certificate. That is enough on the stack's own network and no more: a database outside it should use `verify-full` (spec §8.3a, `operations/deployment.md`).
- CI's container job builds the database image before it starts the stack (`--no-build`), and a new step fails unless the API's database connections all use TLS. Dependabot watches the new Dockerfile.
- Upgrading a stack already running: rebuild (`podman compose up -d --build`); the old data volume is kept and gets a certificate on the next start. A `psql` through `podman compose exec postgres` uses the local socket and is unchanged.
- Not done: the certificate never rotates, and nothing reads its expiry; the GHCR image and release workflow are unchanged, as they hold the API only; the new CI step has not yet run on GitHub.
- Verified: the stack built and started with Podman: `migrate` applied every migration in production, `/_readiness` `200`, an anonymous `GET /api/plans` `401`; `pg_stat_ssl` showed 3 API connections on TLS 1.3 and none without; after `restart postgres` the certificate's hash was the same, the key `600` and owned by `postgres`, and the API was ready again.

### 9 November (failed-login lockout by axa-ratelimit)

- **A client address that earns `LOCKOUT_CLIENT_ADDRESS_AFTER_COUNT` failed authentications (`401`, 20 by default) is now locked out by `axa-ratelimit`'s `lock_out_failures`**, in place of the service's own failures bucket in `rate_limit_mw` (NFR-14). Only a `401` counts, a locked-out client is refused before the auth guard and before it is charged to the general limit, and the lockout lasts until its window ends: as long as `…_RATE_LIMIT_FAILURES_PER_MINUTE` (6) takes to earn the count, 200 s by default, so the defaults behave as before.
- The new name has no prefix, as `TRUSTED_PROXIES` has none. `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_RATE_LIMIT_FAILURES_BURST`, the old name for the count, still works alone with a warning; set to a different count, the service refuses to start, and a value that is not a whole number above zero is refused under either name (before, it fell back to the default).
- axa's lockout reads the client address axa records, which the service's layers run before. A new outermost layer, `client_address_mw`, records the address axa would, so each client behind a trusted proxy is locked out on its own and not the proxy for all of them.
- A refusal is axa's `429` problem (`code: too_many_requests`, `Retry-After`, `RateLimit-*`): it no longer carries the `detail` "too many failed authentication attempts".
- Not done: no request test produces a `401` through the real router (the `hardening` binary runs with login enforcement off), so the order of the three layers in `app.rs` is shown by the unit tests, which build them the same way on axa's stack, and by one run of the binary; lockouts are still per process, so each replica counts its own, and a restart clears them; no lockout is audited or counted in a metric.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all features), 1,249 unit tests (three new: only failures count, a lockout behind a trusted proxy is per client, the two names), `hardening` on the real router; the binary in production with `LOCKOUT_CLIENT_ADDRESS_AFTER_COUNT=3` answered `401` three times, then `429` with `Retry-After: 30` and the security headers; with the two names at 3 and 7 it refused to start, naming both. All 217 database tests in `mod` pass.

### 7 November (event outbox by axa-outbox)

- **The durable event bus's outbox is now `axa_outbox`, kept and relayed by `axa-outbox`**, in place of the service's own `event_outbox` table and drain loop. An event is still written on the handler's own transaction (`axa::outbox::write_seaorm`), and its payload is still the whole envelope, so a consumer sees the same message, with the same `event_id` to dedupe on. The relay is `Outbox::run`, with the `EventSink` (logging, Fluvio, and the webhook fan-out behind it) as its publisher, and it stops on shutdown after the server.
- What the crate brings: one record's events go out in order while other records' go ahead (before, one failing send held back every row behind it); a relay that dies has its claim taken back after five minutes; a row is retried with a doubling wait, and one whose send panics or runs past 30 seconds ten times is left `failed` and kept, as it is still owed (`operations/deployment.md` has the query and the fix); the relay uses two connections of its own, not the request pool. `/events/recent` reads `axa_outbox` and no longer takes row locks to do it. `FluvioSink` now waits for the broker's acknowledgement before a row counts as sent; before, a send that only queued locally was marked published.
- Migration `m20261107_000108` makes the table with the crate's own setup statements and moves every `event_outbox` row across in order: unpublished ones as `pending`, so the new relay sends them, and published ones as `sent`, so a webhook redelivery still finds them until the sweep. `event_outbox` is left empty. **Stop the old relay before migrating:** a binary from before this change still writes and relays `event_outbox`, and what it writes after the migration is not moved.
- Every sink error is treated as the broker being unavailable, so a send that fails never uses up a row's attempts; only a panic or a send past 30 seconds does. A sink that keeps refusing one row holds back that record's events, and no other's.
- Privacy: the person who acted is now only inside the payload (`actor`), not a column, so the subject-access and erasure tools no longer find them in the outbox; sent rows still go after `EVENT_RETENTION_DAYS` (seven days by default), but a `failed` row stays until an operator deals with it. The personal-data inventory and the retention schedule say so.
- Not done: `event_outbox` is not dropped (a later release, once no old binary runs); `Outbox::stats` is not exported as a metric, and nothing alerts on `failed` rows; `retry_failed` has no route or task; no test covers a relay that dies holding a claim, or a `failed` row. `tests/fluvio_relay.rs` was rewritten for the new relay and compiles, but was not run (it needs a live broker). The migration's `down` was run as SQL inside a rolled-back transaction, not through the migrator.
- Verified: fmt, clippy (all features, `fluvio` included), 1,246 unit tests, all 216 database tests in `mod`, among them `requests::event_outbox` (with a new relay test: rows held while the sink is down, then each sent once, in order), `requests::subjects`, `outbox_audit`, `webhook_redelivery`, `personal_data_inventory`; the migration's SQL both ways on sample rows; and the real binary with the relay on: a plan's event was sent and marked `sent`, `/events/recent` read it back, a retried `POST` with the same key replayed, and the process stopped cleanly on `SIGTERM`. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 7 November (idempotency keys by axa-idempotency)

- **`Idempotency-Key` is now kept by `axa-idempotency`** in its table `axa_idempotency`, in place of the service's own layer and `idempotency_keys` (NFR-7). Migration `m20261107_000107` makes the table with the crate's own setup statements and drops the old one; a key taken in the day before the deploy is forgotten, so a retry across it runs as a first request. The service still says what is covered (a `POST`, not the bulk import, which keeps its own key) and whose key it is (the token's subject, or `anonymous` with enforcement off), and the layer stays inside the auth, guest and privacy guards.
- **Breaking for API clients:** a key reused on a different request is `422`, not `409`. A key with a space or other invisible character, over 255 bytes, or sent twice is `400` `idempotency_key_invalid`; before, it was trimmed and cut to 200 characters, so two keys could become one. A body over 1 MiB with a key is `413`, not `422`. An answer that ran and could not be kept (over 1 MiB, or a stream) now holds its key, and a retry is `409` `idempotency_answer_not_kept` rather than running a second time. A `3xx` is kept as well as a `2xx`, with `Location`, `ETag`, `Link` and five other headers; before, only the status, body and content type.
- A running request now renews a one-minute claim, so a crashed run is taken over after a minute, not five, and a slow one is never taken over while it runs.
- The crate's statements run on the shared pool through `db::for_axa_crate`, and are **not seen by the query-count guards**; the guard test still refuses raw pool access anywhere else. The retention rule `idempotency_keys` now purges `axa_idempotency` (and never a running claim); the subject-access and erasure lookup and the personal-data inventory name the new table.
- Not done: `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` stay uncovered, as before, though the crate would cover them; nothing calls the crate's `sweep`, `forget` or `forget_caller` (retention and the erasure lookup do their work); no test makes an answer that cannot be kept, or a claim that lapses. The migration's `down` was not run.
- Verified: fmt, clippy, 1,247 unit tests; in `mod`, the idempotency, meeting actions, bulk, retention and subjects tests; `idempotency_callers` and `personal_data_inventory`. Not rerun: the other 213 tests in `mod`, the other real-token binaries and the restore drill. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 7 November (lists page by signed cursor)

- **Every list now pages by cursor, with `axa-cursor-pagination`, in place of offsets.** A list takes `?limit=` and an opaque `?cursor=`, and answers a bare JSON array with `Link: <…>; rel="next"` while more rows follow; the last page has none (FR-46, API surface §9). A cursor is signed over its position, path and filters with a new setting, `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_CURSOR_KEY`, so a made-up, edited or moved one is `422`.
- **Breaking for API clients:** `X-Total-Count`, `X-Limit` and `X-Offset` are gone. A total would count rows the caller's record policy hides, and `GET /api/plans` did: its total counted private plans the caller could not see. `?offset=` on any `GET` is now `400` `offset_not_supported`, so a client written for offsets fails rather than reading page one for ever. A `limit` above 500, zero or not a number is `422`, where it was clamped or `400`. The 10 000-row ceiling stays (`400` `offset_too_large`, SEC-G7), now reached only by following links.
- The ten handlers that page in the database fetch one row more than the page instead of running a count query. The paging layer slices every other list the same way. The board keeps its whole-plan `counts`, `total` and `truncated`, and drops `offset`; its page is 2 000 tasks, as the unpaged answer was, and a `limit` can only make it smaller, where an explicit page was at most 500. The CCPM estimates keep their counts too.
- **Production refuses to start without `CURSOR_KEY`** (32 bytes or more, the same on every replica). Development and test make a random key per process. `compose.yaml` builds one from `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` unless `PPM_CURSOR_KEY` is set, so a local stack still needs only one secret, and that default is guessable from the database password. Changing the key makes cursors already handed out answer `422`.
- UI: `getPage` returns `{items, more, next}`, and the plans, automations and reviews pages show `25+` where they showed `25 / 431`. The proxy no longer relays the three count headers; it already relayed `Link`. No page reads past its first page, as before.
- Not done: the cursor is still an offset inside, so a row added or removed between two fetches can make a page skip or repeat one row; the ten queries paged in the database end their order in a unique column, so the order itself is stable (the deadline queue gained `id` after `due_at` for this). A list the layer slices keeps whatever order its handler gives, as it did with offsets, and still reads the whole list to slice it. The search index still counts every hit, which nothing reports now.
- Verified: fmt, clippy, 1,250 unit tests, the 215 database tests in `mod` (with new walks of every page, a cursor refused on another list, and the board's next links), `list_paging` (all 65 lists it reaches: no total, `limit=1` honoured, the next link gives the next row, a made-up cursor, a bad limit and an offset refused) and `private_routes`. UI: lint, check, 1,008 unit tests and all 1,011 Playwright tests. On the binary, production refuses to start with no key or a short one, and in development `Link` pages through plans, `?offset=` is `400` and a forged cursor `422`. Not rerun: the 55 other real-token test binaries, the restore drill, and a multi-replica deployment sharing one key. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 7 November (axa 0.7)

- axa 0.7.0 and `axa-ratelimit` 0.2.0. Paging now uses the `offset-pagination` feature and `axa::offset_pagination`, the new names for `axa-pagination`, which is superseded. The old names still worked but will go.
- New from axa, with no code here: a client that stops reading a response is dropped after `SEND_STALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (60 s). Since 0.6.2, a violated check or not-null constraint answers `422` `unprocessable_entity`, not `500`, naming no column or value. axa's stack now also answers `304` to a matching `If-None-Match`, which the service's own `conditional_json` already did, so the service's comparison is now redundant but kept. CORS would expose `etag`, but CORS here is `http_security`'s, which already does. The queue's new columns and `axa_schedule` table are made by `Queue::connect`, which this service uses, so no migration is needed.
- Not taken up: `axa-ratelimit` 0.2's `by_key`, `WhenFull`, `first_refusal` and per-key overrides; `axa-worker`'s schedules (the six tasks keep their own timers); `axa-idempotency` and `axa-outbox` (the service keeps its own); `conditional::etag`. Not tested here: the stall timeout and the `422` for a check constraint, which are axa's and have no test in this repository.
- Verified: fmt, clippy, 1,240 unit tests and the 215 database tests in `mod` pass. Not rerun: the 57 other real-token test binaries. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 7 November (tasks decided on for production)

- axa 0.6's `Task::development_only` makes `task <name>` refuse a fixture-loading task outside development and test, as `db reset` is refused. None of the six tasks here loads fixtures: `director_pack_schedule`, `exception_breaches`, `exceptions_digest`, `retention_purge`, `search_reindex` and `tolerance_escalations` all run against real data on a schedule. So none is marked, and a new test lists each one as decided for production. A task added or removed fails it until someone decides whether it is development-only.
- Not changed: the demonstration data comes from `scripts/seed-demo.mjs`, which fills a running API over HTTP. It is not an axa task, so the marker cannot guard it. `operations/demo.md` already says never to run it against real data.
- Verified: the test passes, and 1,241 unit tests pass. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 7 November (one trusted-proxy list, read by axa)

- **One setting:** `TRUSTED_PROXIES`, read by axa. It decides whose `X-Forwarded-For` gives the client address, for axa and for the rate limiter, and whose `X-Forwarded-Proto` is believed for HSTS. `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_TRUSTED_PROXIES` still works when it is the only one set, with a deprecation warning. **If both are set to different lists, the service refuses to start** and says which to keep. The same entries in another order or spacing count as one list. A bad entry still trusts no proxy, as before.
- The service's own CIDR parser and `X-Forwarded-For` walk are gone: entries parse as axa's `ProxyNet`, and the address is axa's `client::resolve`, reduced to `/64` by `axa-ratelimit`'s `client_key`. The limiter's middleware sits outside axa's stack, which records the address only further in. So it resolves the address itself, with the same function and the list axa read (`ctx.config.http.trusted_proxies`).
- Caught on the way: the first version read only the address axa records. On the real binary every client then shared one limit, whatever the setting, because the limiter runs first. The unit tests had placed the limiter inside axa's stack, so they missed it. They now layer it as production does, and putting the broken lookup back fails 3 of them.
- Verified: on the binary with a limit of 2, the new name and the old name each give a second forwarded client its own limit, and with no list the header is ignored. Different lists refuse to start, the same list reordered starts, and `check` prints the merged list. Also 1,241 unit tests (the setting merge, and resolution matching axa's) and all 298 database tests pass. Not verified: that the deprecation warning reaches the log, as the `test` profile logged nothing here, and real proxies such as nginx or a cloud load balancer. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 7 November (paging through axa-pagination)

- `controllers::pagination` now resolves every page with `axa-pagination`: `resolve(limit, offset, default)` returns axa's `Page`, and handlers answer with `Page::respond` (a bare array plus `X-Total-Count`, `X-Limit` and `X-Offset`) or stamp `Page::headers`. The service's own `Page`, `check_offset` and `with_page_headers` are gone, across 7 controllers and the paging layer. The board's task list is an object, not an array, and stamps the headers itself. `task_window` now returns a `Result`, because it checks the offset itself.
- Unchanged on purpose: a limit is still clamped to 500 and an offset past 10 000 is still `400 offset_too_large`. A non-number is still `400`, from the handler's `u64` fields and the paging layer's `invalid_paging` (spec §9). axa's `PageParams` would instead fall back to the default on a non-number, so it is fed only already-parsed numbers.
- Verified: a new unit test refuses an offset past the ceiling, `list_paging` and 24 paging-related request tests pass, and so do 1,241 unit tests and all 298 database tests. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 7 November (axa::extract)

- Handlers now take `axa::extract`'s `Json`, `Path` and `Query` in place of Axum's, through a new `crate::prelude`: `axa::prelude` with those three replaced. 161 files switch from `axa::prelude::*` to it. A bad body, path or query value now answers a problem whose `detail` says which part was wrong (`The request body is not valid JSON`, `A value in the URL is not valid`, `A query parameter is not valid`) and quotes nothing the caller sent. Before, since axa 0.6, these were problems with no `detail` at all. Before 0.6 they were Axum's plain text, which could quote the value (spec §8.3a).
- A census test in `src/lib.rs` refuses `axa::prelude::*` and Axum's extractors anywhere in `src`. Deliberately adding an offending file made it fail.
- Verified: `tests/requests/extractors.rs` (new) sends a broken body, a path and a query carrying a marker string, and checks the status, media type and `detail` of each and that the marker never comes back. `cargo fmt`, `clippy -D warnings`, 1,237 unit tests, and all 298 database tests (215 in `mod` plus every real-token binary) pass.
- Not covered: a body's `415` and `422` answers are axa's and are not tested here. `Form` and `Multipart` are still Axum's, since `axa::extract` has none. The UI needed no change, because it shows text by status. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 7 November (rate limits counted by axa-ratelimit)

- `src/rate_limit.rs` now counts with `axa-ratelimit`'s `Limiter` in place of its own token buckets, 240 lines fewer. The three limits, the settings and their defaults are unchanged. So are the credential-else-address key, the service's own `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_TRUSTED_PROXIES` and the `429` that names which limit tripped (NFR-14).
- **Behaviour changes.** A limit now allows its burst once per fixed window of 60 × burst ÷ per-minute seconds: general 200 per 20 s, expensive 10 per 20 s, failures 20 per 200 s. Where a token bucket refilled steadily, a client can now send up to twice the burst across the end of a window, and `Retry-After` is the rest of the window, not one request's refill. An IPv6 client now counts by its `/64`, so one subscriber cannot rotate addresses for a fresh limit. A token is held as `axa-ratelimit`'s keyed hash, not an FNV hash.
- Not taken: `axa-ratelimit`'s `by_client` and `lock_out_failures` layers, and its `client_key`. These read the address axa resolves from its own `TRUSTED_PROXIES`, and a deployment that sets only this service's variable would then put every client behind its proxy on one limit. Reconciling the two lists is a separate change.
- Verified: 13 rate-limit unit tests (2 new: the window arithmetic, and IPv6 `/64`), 1,236 unit tests in all, `hardening` and `break_glass`. On the production binary with a burst of 3 at 6 a minute: three `401`s, then a `429` with `retry-after: 30`, the `RateLimit-*` headers and a problem body. `/_health` still `200`, and SIGTERM exits 0. Not verified: the rest of the database suite was not rerun, and nothing was measured under load. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 7 November (axa 0.6)

- axa moves from 0.5 to 0.6.1. **Every error body changes:** it is an RFC 9457 problem, `application/problem+json`, with `type`, `title`, `status`, `detail` where there is more to say, and `code`. `{error, description}` is gone: `error` is now `code` and `description` is `detail`. Successes are unchanged. An API client that reads `error` or `description`, or checks for `application/json` on an error, must change (spec §8.3a).
- The service's own refusals now match. Before, these were plain text, which axa 0.6 would have sent with the reason dropped, or hand-written `{error, description}`: the login guard and `AuthUser` (`401`/`403`), idempotency, paging, rate limiting (`429`), API version (`406`, keeping `requested` and `supported`), module off (`404`, keeping `module`), privacy lookup failure (`503`), and the two missing-`If-Match` `428`s. A guest's `404` is now axa's `not_found`. Before, its body differed from the one a missing record answers, which would have told a guest that a hidden record exists. `tests/guests.rs` now checks the two match byte for byte.
- The UI reads `detail` first, then `message` (SvelteKit's own), then `code` and `title`. Before, it read `description` first.
- Also from axa 0.6: an unmatched path's `404` and a wrong method's `405` are problems. An unmapped unique or foreign key violation answers `409`, not `500`. `KEEP_ALIVE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (75 s) sets how long an idle connection is kept, and a load balancer that keeps them longer needs a higher value. `Cli::parse` now refuses data loss as `cli::parse` does.
- Verified: `cargo fmt`, `clippy -D warnings` and 1,241 unit tests; all 297 database tests (214 in `mod` plus every real-token binary); UI lint, check and 1,007 unit tests; Playwright 908 passed, plus 1 axe colour-contrast failure on the automations page that passed 7/7 on a rerun. On the production binary: `401`s are problems naming the reason, `405` is a problem, `406` keeps its members, and SIGTERM exits 0.
- Flaky, not caused by this change: `requests::scale::capacity_views_do_not_fan_out_with_scale` failed once in a full run and passed alone and in a second full run.
- Not done: the restore drill. Not taken up from 0.6: `axa::extract` (Axum's rejections remain problems with no `detail`), `axa-ratelimit` (the service keeps its own `rate_limit`), `Task::development_only` (no task here loads fixtures), and `Page::bounds`. The Playwright stubs still send the old `{error}` bodies. They pass because the UI shows text by status, but they no longer look like the service. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 7 November (axa 0.5)

- axa moves from 0.4 to 0.5, which refuses `db down`, `db reset` and `db truncate` itself in `cli::parse`, on the same rule: both the process's `APP_ENV` and `--environment` must be `development` or `test`, with `AXA_DESTROY_DATA=true`. The service's own copy of that guard and its unit test are gone; a test now fails if `bin/main.rs` stops reading its arguments through `axa::cli::parse`, since `Cli::parse()` compiles and refuses nothing (spec §8.3a).
- Verified on the binary: `production` and `prod-eu` with the variable set, `test` without it, and `--environment` either way round all refuse, exit 1 and connect to nothing; `test` with it gets past the guard. `cargo fmt`, `clippy -D warnings` and 1,241 unit tests pass.
- Not verified: the database suite was not rerun. Not taken up from axa 0.5: `TRUSTED_PROXIES` and `ClientAddr` (the service keeps its own `trusted_proxy`), `MAX_CONNECTIONS`, the `auth_refused` audit line and the obligation check in `axa-keycloak` and `axa-paseto`, which this service does not use. axa 0.6 is out and not looked at. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 6 November (axa 0.4)

- axa moves from 0.1 to 0.4, with `openapi` added, so the service's minimum Rust version is now 1.97. Swagger UI is one exact version with integrity hashes, where it was `@5` from unpkg. `/_readiness` is axa's: it names what failed, limits each check, and reuses an answer for 5 seconds. `server::run` puts requests and jobs under one shutdown limit, where a hung job could hold the process. The route census uses `mounted()` in place of reading a debug string. `--help` now describes this service, and `check` exists (spec §8.3a).
- Fixed in the previous change's guard: `--environment test` changed the environment it checked, so `APP_ENV=production ... --environment test db reset` could have passed. Both must now allow it, and the variable is now `AXA_DESTROY_DATA`, the name axa uses.
- Before deploying: in production, a `DATABASE_URL` that leaves the machine must set `sslmode`, or the service refuses to start. **`compose.yaml` runs `APP_ENV=production` against the `postgres` container with no TLS, so its production stack will not start until that is decided.** `DB_CONNECT_TIMEOUT` is now 5 s and `DB_IDLE_TIMEOUT` 10 min, up from 500 ms. A request or job still running 25 s after the stop signal is cut off. Each queued job gets one attempt unless a worker says otherwise, and `Queue::connect` alters `axa_queue`, so the role needs that permission.
- Verified: 297 database tests and 1,241 unit tests pass; the fresh schema has 0 differences in 5,197 lines, with `cruet` now at 1; the Swagger hashes match the files on unpkg. On the binary in production: the bypass is refused, `/_readiness` is `503` naming `cache` with Redis down, `/_health` is `200`, the API and Swagger policies are this service's own, and SIGTERM exits 0.
- Not verified: Swagger UI in a real browser; the container image, `compose.yaml` and the chaos test; real Redis or SMTP. `check` prints axa's `HTTP_CSP`, which this service does not send. A `401` from the login guard carries no `x-request-id`. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

### 6 November (guards that named only `production`)

- `db down`, `db reset` and `db truncate` now refuse unless `APP_ENV` is `development` or `test` and `AXA_DESTROY_DATA=true` is set. Before, `db reset` in production dropped every table, the audit log with them, and exited 0. Loco had no guard here either, so this was on `main` too.
- The service now refuses to start with login off in any environment but `development`, `test` and `container-dev`. Before, the check named only `production`, so `APP_ENV=prod-eu` with `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_REQUIRE_AUTH=false` served `/api/plans` to anyone. Also on `main`.
- Redis is now required on the same terms, so an unknown name can no longer drop to a memory cache that replicas do not share (spec §8.3a, NFR-12). A deployment on a custom `APP_ENV` with no Redis will now stop booting. This is on purpose, and it should be checked before deploying.
- Verified on the real binary: both faults were reproduced first. Then, in `production`, `prod-eu` and `container-dev`, all three commands refused even with the variable set; `test` without it refused and left the data alone; and `test` with it ran. `prod-eu` with login off or no Redis refused to boot, and `db status` still works in production. Unit tests cover each rule.
- Not verified: the whole database suite was not rerun for this change, only `cargo test --lib`, `hardening` and `enforcement`. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.** Not done: the rest of the axa 0.4 upgrade list.

### 6 November (sqlx: the shared layer and nine controllers)

- `src/db.rs` is now the only way to sqlx (spec §8.3b). It counts every statement in `query_count`, because that count saw SeaORM only, and the guards in `financials` and `strategy` would have passed with nothing to see once their routes moved. A census test refuses the raw pool anywhere else. `AuditModel::record_on` writes the audit row on sqlx, on the pool or in a transaction. Every entity derives `sqlx::FromRow`. `controllers/director_summary.rs` moved to `db::pool`.
- Moved to sqlx: `saved_views`, `schedule_health`, `access_review`, `role_tasks`, `webhook_deliveries`, `includes`, `distribution`, `my_work` and `task_get`. `includes` and `task_get` still ask SeaORM for a plan's modules, and `schedule_health` still loads its network through SeaORM helpers.
- Tested: `tests/sqlx_layer.rs` shows the count sees the pool and a transaction, an audit row written on sqlx passes `verify`, and it leaves with a dropped transaction. `tests/requests/includes.rs` now covers every name with a row in it. It did not before, and a wrong column only fails when a row comes back.
- Verified, before those last include assertions: 297 database tests pass (295 plus the 2 new), `cargo test --lib` passes (1,239), and the fresh schema has 0 differences from the Loco-built one. After them, only the `includes` test and the gates were rerun.

Column names in `query_as` are still checked by tests, not the compiler. Statements in the SeaORM helpers are counted, but this adds no count guard for the moved routes. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**

- `controllers/director_summary.rs` now uses plain sqlx on the pool SeaORM already holds, so the move can land a controller at a time with the tests passing. `src/db.rs` hands out the pool. The two entities it reads derive `sqlx::FromRow`.

### 5 November (Loco replaced with axa)

- The service no longer uses Loco. It is plain Axum with [axa](https://crates.io/crates/axa) 0.1, and SeaORM stays until its own change. `App::run` in `src/app.rs` is the whole boot path, written out. Configuration comes from the environment: `config/*.yaml` is gone, and `APP_ENV` replaces `LOCO_ENV` (spec §8.3a).
- `src/cache.rs` replaces Loco's cache and `email::Mailer` uses `lettre` directly, because axa has neither. Plain `start` still only serves. Queued jobs now use `axa_queue`.

CORS, HSTS and the request timeout are left with this service, and a misspelt `APP_ENV` keeps login on.
- Verified: `pg_dump --schema-only` of a fresh database is identical to one built by the Loco code, 0 differences in 5,197 lines. 295 database tests pass, the same count as CI on `main`. The binary refuses production with no database, no Redis or login off; a misspelt environment keeps login on; with Redis down `/_health` is `200` and `/_readiness` is `503`; SIGTERM exits 0.
- Not verified: real Redis and real SMTP were not used, so Redis storage and STARTTLS are compiled, not exercised. The container image, `compose.yaml` and the chaos test were edited and not run. The restore drill was not run locally. **Nothing is pushed, so CI has not seen this.**
