### 5 November (SM-2: times on an issue)

- An issue now carries `reported_at`, `acknowledged_at`, `resolved_at` and `closed_at`, written by the service as a pure rule from its moves, and `occurred_at` and `detected_at`, stated by a person. Stating or correcting one is an edit, and the audit entry holds the values before and after. A save that leaves them out keeps them, so a screen that does not know them cannot clear them.
- Nothing is back-filled: an existing issue has no times, saving it adds none, and closing one resolved earlier records the closing only.
- Not done: `first_response_at` is not built, because issues have no comments and nothing yet counts as a response; it moved to SM-6. No report reads the times, so the count of issues a report leaves out moved to SM-5. No screen shows or sets them (SM-16). `occurred_at` and `detected_at` work on every issue type until SM-3 adds the incident, and a status field rule cannot yet name them.
- Not verified: the whole database suite was not run locally, only `issue_configuration`, the issue and control tests in `mod`, and the unit tests; CI runs the rest. PE-2 still waits on the owner's answer to PE-D2.

### 5 November (PE-1: what a product plan touches)

- Measured, not inferred: of 101 aggregate views, a bare `Product` plan changes 24 and one with data of every kind changes 58. Nine of the 24 are records that should show it, such as the audit trail and the regulator extract; 15 are decision views that a mere proposal should not move. `census_of_what_a_product_plan_touches` names both lists, so a new view must be sorted. This changes the advice: "counts in no portfolio total" would have hidden records from auditors and real spending from totals, so PE-D2 now turns on the stage.
- `/api/tpc` has no entry in the 24: a bare plan has no TPC row. The census captures as one caller, an administrator.
- The second test in `tests/private_aggregates.rs` failed CI's restore drill, which runs that binary without `--test-threads=1`, because the file had been single-test. Both tests are now `#[serial]`.
- Not done: the hook is not built, because it needs the stage from PE-2, so leaving a product out of the 15 is not yet proved and PE-D1 could still be reopened. `controllers/cpm.rs` and `engineering/mod.rs` show no `HiddenScope` call and need looking at. PE-D2 as revised is the owner's to confirm. Not verified: views outside the 101, such as director pack sections; that the 58 figure holds for a product with fewer kinds of data.

### 5 November (PE and SM: four decisions recorded)

- The owner confirmed that a product is a plan (PE-D1), that this is a service record and not a help desk (SM-D1), and that bundled assessment templates ship as drafts marked unreviewed (PE-D5). The project is all free open source, so no Cyber Resilience Act role is claimed for it, and legal advice comes before any commercial step (SM-D5).
- Not done: the PE-1 census is at a first reading: plans are loaded in 101 places, and `HiddenScope` looks like the hook, but that has not been tried, so PE-D1 could still be reversed. PE-D2 to PE-D4, PE-D6, SM-D2 to SM-D4 and SM-D6 are open, including no inbound email. No template reviewer is named.

### 5 November (SM-1: security.txt)

- `/.well-known/security.txt` is on the public site, with the contact and policy in `SECURITY.md`, per RFC 9116. The Pages upload leaves out dotfiles by default, so the deploy now sets `include-hidden-files`, and a test checks that it does. A test fails when `Expires` is under 60 days away; it expires on 2027-10-01 and must be renewed by hand.
- The site's copy of `spec/index.md` was stale after the proposal below, which failed CI's site job. Refreshed here.
- Not done: the file is unsigned, which the RFC recommends and this project has no published key for. The running service serves none; a deployer sets their own contact.

### 5 November (PE and SM: product evaluation and service management proposed)

- Researched and assessed, for the owner to decide: a register and evaluation of products, and a service record of incidents, requests, service levels and security reports. Two new specs, [`spec/product-evaluation.md`](spec/product-evaluation.md) and [`spec/service-management.md`](spec/service-management.md), `plan.md` §85 to §87, and PE-1 to PE-16 and SM-1 to SM-17 in `tasks.md`. About half of the example design exists under other names, so the advice is a thin profile on the plan record and six real gaps, not new modules.
- Not done: **nothing is built, and every decision is open.** Not verified: no comparator was run and none of their source was read; HaloITSM's own guides were read only on assets and custom fields; EUR-Lex would not load, so the Cyber Resilience Act was read on a mirror; the Digital Omnibus, the GDPR text and the ISO standards were not read; the commercial field was not scanned. The advice that a product can be a plan rests on a census not yet done (PE-1). Nothing here is legal advice.

### 5 November (GP-8: the site in Arabic and Hindi)

- The public site now has a home page, the four topics and all its own words in Arabic (\`ar-001\`) and Hindi (\`hi-in\`, copied to \`hi-001\`), and the 11 role briefs in Arabic. Hindi already had its briefs. Arabic is the site's first right-to-left language: an \`ar-001\` page is right to left in its prerendered HTML, and a reader who chooses Arabic on an English document gets the site's words right to left around a left-to-right page. \`ar-001\` has no regional copy, as in the interface. Both languages are in the locale picker and the "Translations" line, and \`site-ar.csv\` and \`site-hi.csv\` list the text for a speaker.
- **Every word is a machine draft, written by Claude on 2026-11-05. No speaker has reviewed any of it,** and each page says so in its footer. The drafts were checked by script for the same headings, lists, tables, numbers and links as the English, which says nothing about their sense. New terms have no reviewed precedent, such as the Arabic for "fails closed" and the Hindi for "role briefs"; the Hindi follows the briefs where they differ from the application ("वितरण" where the interface has "डिलीवरी"). The tutorial signs in at \`/ar-001/signin\` and \`/hi-001/signin\`, not the English address, as Welsh and Mandarin do.
- Not done: the nine comparison product pages stay in English. Arabic has no regional locale. Not verified: 200 browser tests pass, with axe on every Arabic and Hindi home page and topic, but nobody who reads either language has looked at a rendered page, so line breaks, bidirectional mixes of Latin and Arabic, and table layout in Arabic were not judged by eye.

### 5 November (CI: two new advisories accepted, not fixed)

- \`cargo deny\` began failing on RUSTSEC-2026-0440 (\`astral-tokio-tar\`) and RUSTSEC-2026-0441 (\`pin-utils\`), both published after the last green run and both "unmaintained", not vulnerabilities, with no safe upgrade. The first is test-only, through \`testcontainers\`; the second comes only with the optional \`fluvio\` feature. Neither is in the default build, so they are accepted in \`deny.toml\` with that reason.
- Not done: neither crate is replaced; a \`testcontainers\` or \`fluvio\` release that drops them would let the entries go. Not verified: \`cargo deny\` was run by CI, not here, unless the tool is installed locally.

### 5 November (OC-25: what a review of the series found)

- An independent review of OC-13 to OC-24 found more. `GET /api/tpc` named private plans and their DIPP, and the census called it filtered; it now filters, and its comparison seeds a row. The filters built on every pid of a hidden plan used one bind per pid and would have failed past Postgres's limit, taking the inbox with them; they now pass one value. Proposal edits, tradeoffs, pipeline actions, promotion and lessons now answer `404` for a proposal that became a hidden plan, as the read did. `POST /api/notifications/{pid}/read` no longer shows a note the inbox hides. Confirming a held import row checks its parent, and a held row no longer shows a hidden parent. A keyless import no longer treats a hidden plan as a duplicate, so a guessed title gets the answer of no match. A hidden canvas item's watches and notices now go with the plan.
- OC-23 went too far and this restores it: notices sent to someone by name stay even if they cannot read the plan, because they must act or be told. That is a request to approve, a process step and the break-glass notice, as well as an invitation to review.
- OC-15 to OC-17 went too far on deleted plans and this restores it: a canvas, proposal, review or rule on a deleted plan stays reachable, as it is listed.
- **This changes what callers can do:** `parent_ref` naming a plan that does not exist is now `404`, as for a hidden one, where it used to be stored. A plan already under a parent keeps it, so a member of a child need not read the parent to edit the child.
- An organization reviewer now sees their review in the list. Census corrections: process runs are `left-open`, and seven routes were misclassified.
- Not done: an import keyed to a parent that does not exist still creates the row, so an administrator's import can tell it from a hidden one. A job queued before this runs with nothing hidden. `owned_pids` still loads every row of each hidden plan's tables per request, which costs on a large deployment; it is not a poller today. Not verified: no test covers a handling mark on these routes, a promoted-proposal review, the review queue's filters, or the keyless path, which needs the search engine. The limit of 65,535 binds was reasoned, not reproduced.

### 5 November (OC-24: every route outside the guard says how it treats a hidden plan)

- `plan-guard-census.txt` lists all 396 mounted routes outside `/api/plans/{pid}`, whatever the method, with how each treats a plan the caller cannot read, and a unit test fails on one that is missing or no longer mounted. The old census covered parameterless GETs, which is how these gaps went unseen. The private-plan comparison now seeds a review, so `/api/reviews` has something to find, and the wrong reasons in that census are corrected. The review checklist says a route outside the guard must check for itself.
- Not done: **the census is a declaration, not a proof.** A class is what was traced or asserted, and only what `tests/private_routes.rs` and `tests/private_aggregates.rs` exercise is tested. Two routes are marked `left-open`: a stored director pack is shown as issued even if a plan in it turned private later, and an uncleared administrator can read one too. Whether a frozen pack should disappear is left as a decision. Also left: `data_quality` in a pack counts the hidden plans; a process run's starter and named people act without a plan check; a requester or author can withdraw an approval or delete a comment after losing access. Not verified: the sections inside a director pack beyond `benefits`, `deescalation` and `key_persons` were not read, and query parameters naming a plan were not all traced.

### 5 November (OC-23: what you were told goes when your access does)

- Notifications store their words, some quoting a task or escalation title, and watches store a pid, so `/api/me/inbox`, `/api/notifications`, the Atom feed and `/api/me/watching` kept showing a plan after the caller lost access or the plan turned private. They now leave those out of their rows, counts and pages. The rows stay stored and come back with access. An invitation to review stays, because it is what lets an outside expert in. `GET /api/timesheets` no longer counts a hidden plan's entries in `omitted_unreadable`. Predates OC-1.
- Not done: notifications already sent to a sink, such as email or chat, are out of reach. Routes outside the plan guard are still being worked through in this series. Not verified: a notice about a non-plan subject, such as a canvas item, is not treated as the plan's unless its pid is one `owned_pids` lists; the timesheet count has no test of its own.

### 5 November (OC-22: a sweep a writer runs stays on their side)

- `POST /api/threshold-sweep` answered any writer with every programme's pid, name and crossed thresholds, and opened exceptions on private plans. `POST /api/status-updates/remind` counted and reminded across private plans. `POST /api/board/snapshots` handed back estate totals that include private plans, the figures `GET /api/board/trends` keeps to administrators. A sweep or reminder started by hand now covers only the plans the caller can read, and the scheduled runs still cover all of them. A snapshot now answers with its `id` and `taken_at` only. Predates OC-1.
- Not done: routes outside the plan guard are still being worked through in this series. Not verified: the reminder's filter has no test that a due reminder on a private plan is skipped, only that the route answers; the threshold sweep's filter does.

### 5 November (OC-21: a reference is no way into a private plan)

- References to a record in another plan were checked for existence only. A writer on a public plan could add a `blocks` relation onto a private task, which then could not finish; a `precedes` relation probed its due date. Time entries took any `task_pid`, so minutes landed on a private task. Linking a private KPI to your own benefit put its name and latest reading on the portfolio's benefits page and in director packs. A scorecard's KPI list and a DevOps recovery's `incident_pid` told you whether a hidden pid existed. Each now gives the answer of a record that does not exist. A refused-to-finish message no longer names a blocker the caller cannot see, though it still blocks. Predates OC-1.
- A time entry's `task_pid` must now be a task of that plan, which is new: a pid from another plan, or none that exists, used to be stored and is now `422`.
- Not done: relations and benefit links already stored stay, so an existing `blocks` onto a private task still blocks it; removing one needs the plan's member. Replacing a scorecard's KPIs keeps the hidden ones, which the caller cannot see to remove. The `precedes` refusal can still print a hidden task's pid when an existing relation is broken. Routes outside the plan guard are still being worked through in this series. Not verified: the benefit and director-pack read path after a hidden KPI link; only the refusal has a test.

### 5 November (OC-20: a template is hidden with its plan)

- Saving a plan as a template copies its tasks and dates, and templates had no link back to the plan, so a template of a private plan could be read, copied into a new plan, revised or deleted by anyone. `plan_templates` now records `source_plan_pid` (migration 105), updated on revision, and a template is hidden with that plan: out of the list, `404` on every route. A template outlives a deleted plan, as it did before. Predates OC-1.
- Not done: **templates saved before this have no source and stay visible.** The audit trail does not name the plan, so it cannot be recovered; anyone who has saved a template from a private plan should delete it and save it again. A plan's own link to its template is not re-checked for template sync. Routes outside the plan guard are still being worked through in this series. Migration 105 was run down, up and again on a test database.

### 5 November (OC-19: sprints and calendars keep a private plan out)

- `/api/sprints/{pid}/ceremonies`, `/commitment` and `/commit` sit outside the plan guard and looked at nothing, so anyone holding a sprint pid could read retrospective notes or use up a sprint's one commitment. They now answer `404`. `/api/feeds/calendar.ics` listed the owner's tasks, and that plan's milestones and sprints, with no check, so a feed outlived its owner's access; it now leaves those plans out. Predates OC-1.
- **A calendar is narrower than the screens:** a feed URL carries no token, so its owner is judged with no clearance and no administrator's right. A plan with a handling mark stays out of the calendar even for someone cleared to see it, because a calendar URL is often shared.
- Not done: routes outside the plan guard are still being worked through in this series. Not verified: the handling-mark case in the feed has no test of its own; the private-plan case does.

### 5 November (OC-18: a field of the body is no way into a private plan)

- Five routes took a plan's pid in the body and checked at most that it existed: the batch MS Project import, KPIs, ways of working and governance frameworks, DevOps events, and a plan's `parent_ref`. Each now gives the answer the guard on `/api/plans/{pid}` would. A batch import reports a hidden plan exactly as one that does not exist, in a dry run too. `POST /api/plans/merge` and `POST /api/plans/import` were open to an administrator a handling mark should have stopped; merge now answers `404`, and an import, which runs after its request, records what was hidden from the caller and gives a row reaching such a plan the error of a pid that names nothing. The review queue no longer names a plan the reader cannot see. Predates OC-1.
- **This changes what a service token can do:** a pipeline posting DevOps events for a private plan now needs an administrator's token or a place on the access list.
- Not done: routes outside the plan guard are still being worked through in this series. An import keyed by identifier or owner code on a hidden plan reports the row as failed, which tells an administrator something matched. Not verified: the handling-mark case itself has no test here; these tests use a private plan and a writer, and the review queue's filter has no test.

### 5 November (OC-17: a rule on every plan needs an administrator)

- A rule's `plan_pid` was checked only for existence, so anyone who could write could attach a rule to a private plan that later moved tasks and sent notices. Listing rules and their runs, and switching a rule on or off, looked at nothing, and `/api/scheduled-actions` took any subject. Each now answers `404` for a plan the caller cannot read, the lists leave those rows out, and scheduling on a subject that does not exist is `404` where it used to be accepted.
- **This changes what writers can do:** a rule with no plan runs on every plan, private ones included, so creating, switching or deleting one now needs an administrator. Anything doing that with a writer's token gets `403`. Rules already stored stay in force, including any a writer made. The audit feed now also hides reviews and promoted proposals of a hidden plan. Predates OC-1.
- Not done: routes outside the plan guard are still being worked through in this series. The automations screen still offers "every plan" to writers, who get the refusal. Not verified: a rule on every plan that fired on a hidden plan's review, as opposed to a task move.

### 5 November (OC-16: a private plan's reviews are private)

- Nothing under `/api/reviews` looked at the plan: anyone signed in could read scores, comments and reviewers, read the consensus, invite a reviewer, and accept or submit on someone's behalf. Each now answers `404` for a plan the caller cannot read, or a proposal that became one, the list leaves them out of its rows and its total, and an invitation to such a plan gets the `422` of a plan that does not exist. The invited reviewer keeps their way in without being on the access list, because the invitation is the disclosure decision. Predates OC-1.
- Not done: anyone who can read the plan can still accept or submit for a named reviewer. The reviews screen works that way and an outside expert may have no login, so that is left as a decision. Routes outside the plan guard are still being worked through in this series. Not verified: reviews of a promoted proposal have no test of their own.

### 5 November (OC-15: a private plan's canvas is private)

- Nothing under `/api/canvases` looked at the plan: anyone signed in could read a private plan's canvases, add and edit items, and promote one, which wrote a risk into the plan. Every route now answers `404`, a body `plan_pid` included, and the list leaves those canvases out. Comments, attachments and the like on a canvas item, or on a proposal that became a plan, passed as belonging to no plan, so they had the same gap; they now follow the plan. Predates OC-1.
- Not done: routes outside the plan guard are still being worked through in this series. Not verified: reactions, watchers and attachments on a canvas item have no test of their own; they share the `resolve_subject` check that comments test.

### 5 November (OC-14: a title is no way to find a private plan)

- A proposal's duplicate check named any matching plan, so a guessed title found a private one; an objective's alignment listed private plans with their weights, and `GET /api/objectives` hands out the pids; a proposal promoted into a private plan stayed readable by pid and under `?status=promoted`. Each now leaves the plan out, or answers `404` for the proposal, and alignment totals count only what the caller can read. Predates OC-1.
- Not done: routes outside the plan guard are still being worked through in this series. Not verified: the handling-mark path here, beyond what the shared check covers.

### 5 November (OC-13: an inbox belongs to its owner)

- `GET /api/notifications` took the inbox to read from a free query parameter and `POST /api/notifications/{pid}/read` looked at nobody, so anyone signed in could read and mark any person's notifications. Each now answers only for the caller's own `person:` or `worker:` reference, or an organization they belong to; another inbox is `403` and another's notification `404`. **An administrator's token gets the same refusal:** a message can quote a plan that token's clearance does not reach, and no screen needs it. Predates OC-1.
- Not done: the operator interface has no inbox screen, so nothing there needed changing. Routes outside the plan guard are being worked through in this series, and `tests/private_aggregates.rs` still calls some of them safe. Not verified: an `organization:` inbox has no test of its own.

### 5 November (OC-12: no way round a task gate)

- A task could get past a role-restricted move three ways: created straight in a later status, imported from a file with one, or moved
  to an unrestricted plan, finished there and moved back. Under a workflow that restricts moves, a new or imported task now starts in the
  initial state, and moving a task between plans needs an administrator. These predate OC-1. Plans with no restricted moves are
  unchanged.
- Not done: tasks already past a gate stay where they are. Not verified: the two importers' new branch has no test of its own; the
  creation and move paths do. Task-level writes other than the board move, such as a plan merge, were not traced.

### 5 November (OC-11: a private plan's controls are private)

- `/api/controls/{pid}`, `/api/controls/{pid}/readings`, `/api/readings/{pid}/actions` and `/api/actions/{pid}/convert` sit outside the
  plan guard, and only conversion checked the plan. Someone outside a private plan who knew a pid could read its control readings, add
  readings and actions, and remove a control. This predates OC-1. Each lookup now answers `404`, with a test on every route.
- Not done: the same pattern was not looked for elsewhere. Other routes that reach a plan's records by their own pid may have this gap,
  and a census of them would be worth doing. Not verified: a handling-marked plan on these routes beyond what `readable_plans` covers.

### 5 November (OC-10: task workflows need an administrator)

- Registering or withdrawing a task workflow now needs an administrator, as an issue workflow does, so a role restriction on a task
  move holds. **This changes what writers can do:** anything that registers task workflows with a writer's token now gets `403`.
  Workflows already registered stay in force. A plan template still copies its source's workflow onto a new plan.
- Two refusal tests moved from the tokenless suite to `tests/issue_configuration.rs`, unchanged, to run as a real administrator. Tests
  that only need a workflow to exist seed one through the path plan templates use.
- Not done: a deployment running with no sign-in cannot register a workflow through the API at all. `task_bulk`'s move between plans maps
  statuses with no role gate; that predates this and was not changed. Not verified: whether any deployment scripts register task
  workflows with a writer's token.

### 5 November (OC-8: the Redmine comparison, checked)

- The Redmine column on `/compare/redmine/` now follows Redmine's own documentation, read on 5 November, and the page links to it. The
  table says what this project now does, and the summaries in eight languages no longer say that roles grant nothing.
- Not done: Redmine was not installed or run. The rest of that page, and the six other commercial and open-source product pages, are
  still general descriptions. Not verified: the wording of the changed Welsh and Chinese summaries, which are machine drafts.

### 5 November (OC-7: the interface for all of it)

- Administrators can now manage all of this in the interface: role permissions and translations on `/issue-settings`, and people,
  types and workflows on the new `/plans/{pid}/issue-settings`. One editor registers and changes workflows, with field rules and
  translated statuses. The issue form follows the caller's permissions and the status's rules, and the cross-plan list shows each
  status's real name.
- The issue form dropped `priority`, so any edit cleared a stored one. It now shows and sends it.
- Not done: people are added by user id, because there is no directory to pick from. Task workflows have no editor. Not verified: a
  real browser against a real service; the browser tests stub the service. The new strings in seven languages are machine drafts,
  listed for review, and no speaker has read them.

### 5 November (OC-9: what the independent review of OC-2 to OC-6 found)

- **A private plan's issue titles, statuses and owners were readable by outsiders** in `GET /api/plans/audit/recent`, and by issue pid
  through `GET /api/plans/{pid}/audit`, which never checked the pid was a plan. This predates OC-1. A failing test confirmed it; both
  are closed. Anything already fetched or copied cannot be recalled.
- `POST`, `DELETE`, `PUT` and the new `GET` on `/api/workflows` answer `404` for a plan the caller cannot read. Before this a writer
  could confirm a hidden plan exists and install a task workflow on it.
- Converting a control action into an issue needs `issue_add` and a plan the caller can read. A resolution counts as part of a move
  only when the move finishes the issue. A stale save gets `409` rather than undoing a move. Removing an issue under a restricted
  workflow needs an administrator on a plan with no members. Role memberships are deleted on erasure under the delete policy and are
  classed `PPL`. "One open issue per source" no longer answers for another plan.
- Not done: control readings and actions are still reached outside the plan guard, apart from conversion. An audit row about a role
  keeps the person's reference, which erasure does not search. Two identical memberships added at once give a `500`, not a `409`. Not
  verified: the `409` on a stale save has no race test, because the window cannot be forced through the API; the tests cover the
  sequential paths. Other plan-owned tables were not audited for the same gap as `issues`.

### 5 November (OC-6: workflows change in place, and custom words are translated)

- An administrator can change an issue workflow in place, in one audited step, rather than withdrawing and registering it.
- Issue types, roles and statuses can carry a label per language. The cross-plan issue list now receives each status's real
  label and translations from the service.
- Not done: task workflows are still withdrawn and registered again; a workflow's scope cannot change; two plans wording one status
  differently share one word in the summary's counts. The interface comes with OC-7. These labels are the organization's own
  words, so they are outside the translation reviews. Not verified: a right-to-left label in the interface.

### 5 November (OC-5: issue types by plan)

- A plan can limit itself to some of the deployment's issue types, as a Redmine project selects its trackers. A plan with no list
  offers every type, so nothing changes until an administrator chooses.
- Not done: only an administrator chooses; the interface comes with OC-7. Issues the service raises from a risk, a blocked task or
  a control action are not limited. Not verified: a very long list of types on one plan.

### 5 November (OC-4: field rules by status)

- A state of an issue workflow can make a field required or read-only, for everyone or for named roles, as Redmine's workflow
  field permissions do. Nothing changes until a workflow declares a rule.
- Not done: rules cover seven standard fields, not custom fields; a task workflow cannot carry them; the interface comes with OC-7.
  An issue raised by the service from a risk, a blocked task or a control action is not checked against required fields. Not
  verified: Redmine's exact order of evaluation; here read-only is read from the state before the save.

### 5 November (OC-3: role permissions and plan role membership)

- A role now carries permissions (`issue_add`, `issue_edit`, `issue_move`, `issue_delete`), and an administrator can give a person
  one or more roles on a plan, which combine, as in Redmine. Once a plan has a member, issue actions there need the permission.
  A plan with no members behaves as before, and no role starts with a permission, so upgrading changes nothing.
- A role held on a plan counts for that plan's role-restricted moves, on issues and on tasks.
- The token stays the ceiling: a role cannot lift a read-only token, open a private plan or make anyone an administrator.
- Not done: administrators alone manage membership; permissions cover issue actions only; groups cannot hold a role; an issue raised
  from a control action is not checked. The interface for this comes with OC-7. Not verified: a real identity provider's tokens;
  the tests use signed test tokens.

### 5 November (OC-2: the status filter confirms no hidden word)

- `?status=` no longer tells a caller whether a private plan's workflow declares a word: such a word now gets the same `422` as
  one nobody uses. A plan's own list accepts that plan's statuses and deployment-wide ones, not another plan's.
- Not done: nothing else about the filter changed. Not verified: the response time of a filter on a custom status with very many
  distinct plans, which now reads the plans it names.

### 4 November (OC-1: organization-defined issue types, statuses and roles)

- An organization can define its own issue types, statuses and role-restricted moves, by issue type, as Redmine does with
  trackers and its workflow matrix. Issues now follow the custom workflows that already existed. A role in the new catalogue
  grants nothing; permissions still come from the token. See `spec/issue-configuration.md`. The site's comparisons say so.
- Registering or withdrawing an issue workflow now needs an administrator. **The migration withdraws existing issue workflows**:
  they had no effect, and anyone who could write could create one. An administrator registers the ones wanted.
- Changing an issue's type under a workflow that restricts moves needs an administrator. An independent security review found
  that a writer could otherwise step round a role-restricted move by changing the type and back; a test now covers it.
- `GET /api/workflows` no longer shows a plan's workflow to someone who cannot read that plan. A `PUT` that omits an
  issue's `kind` no longer resets it to `concern`.
- Not done: roles carry no permissions; no per-project role membership, per-status field rules, per-plan issue types, or
  translated custom labels. Task workflows still need no administrator, a gap that predates this. The new UI strings are
  machine drafts in seven languages, unreviewed. The Redmine descriptions come from general knowledge, unchecked against its
  current documentation.

### 4 November (the request body generator, after the doc comments)

- `scripts/generate-openapi-bodies.py` lost 30 request bodies once `aec010a` put a doc comment between a type's `derive` and
  its `struct`, so regenerating would have dropped them; it now skips doc comments as it skips attributes. `openapi_bodies.rs`
  and `openapi.json` are regenerated: 26 routes gain the descriptions written in `aec010a`, and no route or body changed. The
  service's fmt and library tests pass, and clippy passed on the same generated bodies.
- Not done: `lychee` is not installed here, so the `docs` job's offline link check was covered locally only by
  `scripts/check-links.py`. The service's database tests were not run for this change.

### 4 November (GP-7: neutral wording on the site)

- The site's pages and words now read as a neutral analyst's summary, in English, Welsh and Mandarin. The facts and caveats
  are unchanged. Not done: the role briefs and reference documents keep their wording; the Welsh and Mandarin remain
  unreviewed machine drafts. The tutorial's commands have still not been run for this site.

### 4 November (GP-3 to GP-6: a welcoming site, in three languages)

- The site is now for newcomers: a home page, benefits, features, comparisons and a tutorial on the seeded demonstration,
  with the reference documents under `/docs/`. Every claim comes from the repository, and the home page and each
  comparison repeat the production readiness verdict.
- Lily's whole PickerBar on every page, all 57 themes, and search in the browser.
- English, Welsh and Mandarin site words; home pages and topics for eight locales, with slugs in the language,
  `.locale-peer-id` and sitemaps. `scripts/export-site-translation-review.py` lists the words for review, and CI checks it.
- `/compare/` and nine product pages. There is no importer for Trello, Planview, Smartsheet, Leantime or Redmine, and each
  page says so.
- Not done: the Welsh and Mandarin are machine drafts no speaker has reviewed. The tutorial's commands were not run for
  this site. No compared product was installed or run, and six descriptions come from general knowledge, unchecked against
  each product's current documentation. 15 of the 57 themes fail contrast on the home page (light and dark pass). The
  role briefs keep English slugs. Hindi has no site words, home page or topics. Nothing on the site shows a time, so the
  time zone choice has no effect yet.

### 4 November (GP-2: the documentation site is published)

- The site is live at <https://project-portfolio-management.github.io/>, deployed from the new exported repository
  `project-portfolio-management/project-portfolio-management.github.io` by `make github-pages` (`make publish-github-pages` since 13 November). Its copy of
  `spec/index.md` had gone stale after UE-1 and was refreshed first, which also clears the `site` CI failure.
- Not done: the axe suite ran on the local build, not against the live host; publishing is by hand, so the site can
  lag `main`; the exported repository has no branch protection. Not fixed here: the `docs` CI job still fails because
  `openapi_bodies.rs` is stale, which is unrelated to the site.

### 31 October (supply-chain gates matched across the family)

- Every GitHub Action is pinned to a commit SHA (it was a movable tag). `scripts/check-actions-pinned.py --actions-only`
  and `zizmor` run in CI (`pins` job, which also checks images). Checkouts no longer persist credentials; release jobs use no build cache.
- A whole-history secret scan (`gitleaks`, pinned by SHA-256) runs in CI (`secrets` job). Its five reviewed findings are
  listed with reasons in `.gitleaksignore`; all are test keys or scaffold defaults.
- Every container image reference (compose files, Dockerfiles, CI service containers, scripts, `.woodpecker.yml`) now carries an
  `@sha256` digest (16 references; the same digests as WPM where the tag matches). `scripts/check-actions-pinned.py` checks images
  too, and skips `*:local` images the compose file builds itself. Dependabot's docker entries keep the digests current.
- README: "Works on its own".

### 31 October (matcher 0.2.2)

- **Matcher 0.2.2 published; the service uses it.** `project-portfolio-management-matcher` 0.2.2 (WW-1.10: codes keep every script; Soundex declines non-Latin names) is on crates.io, published at the product owner's direction, and the service now depends on `0.2.2` instead of 0.2.1, so plan codes in another script now reach the duplicate check. Verified locally: the matcher's tests, `cargo publish --dry-run`, the service's clippy and library tests and the matching test binary. Not verified: the database test run against 0.2.2 (read CI). A published version cannot be deleted, only yanked.

### 31 October (WW-1.4 in the UI, the first day of the week)

- **Calendars start the week on the reader's day (WW-1.4, accepted 2026-10-31; the service part was done earlier today).** The delivery calendar and the working calendars gave Lily's CalendarView no locale, so every week started on Sunday. They now pass the page's locale, so the week starts on that locale's day (Monday for the world locales, by CLDR), and the Display page has a new "First day of the week" choice (the language's usual day, Monday, Sunday or Saturday), kept with the other display preferences on the device and the account and passed to the widget as the locale's `-u-fw-` extension. `src/lib/week-start.ts` holds the rule. Seven new catalogue keys in every language, machine drafts and unreviewed (the review CSVs, Welsh included, are regenerated). Stated limits in the UI spec: a browser without `Intl.Locale` week information (Firefox at the time of writing) ignores the choice; timesheet weeks, week numbers, the Gantt's weekly ticks and the API's buckets stay ISO 8601 (Monday first); the weekday editors list Monday first, the stored mask's order. Tests: a unit test (locale defaults for `en-001`, `en-US`, `ar-EG`; a choice wins; the extension round-trips; the preference is parsed and junk dropped) and a browser test (Monday first in `en-001`, Sunday first after choosing Sunday on the Display page). Gates run: lint, check, all unit tests, the full browser suite (989 passed locally; one earlier run had a `document-title` axe failure on the deployment-moves page, which this change does not touch, and it passed three times alone and in the rerun). **Behaviour change:** a calendar that started on Sunday now starts on Monday for the world English locale. **Not done:** the choice does not reach the other grids; not checked in Firefox or Safari. **Not verified:** CI before the push.

A new "Which law applies" section asks the deployer to name its law (a `‹buyer›` marker) and lists common ones with the standard each points to: the EU's Web Accessibility Directive and European Accessibility Act (EN 301 549), the UK's Public Sector Bodies Accessibility Regulations 2018, Section 508 and the ADA, the Accessible Canada Act and Ontario's AODA, Australia's Disability Discrimination Act, India's RPwD Act with GIGW, Japan's JIS X 8341-3, Israel's IS 5568 and Brazil's Inclusion Law with eMAG. It adds that the automated audit tests WCAG criteria only, not a national standard's other clauses. ### 31 October (WW-1.12, any OpenID Connect provider)

- **The API accepts access tokens from any OpenID Connect provider, not only Keycloak (WW-1.12, accepted 2026-10-31).** New `src/auth/oidc.rs`, in every build (no cargo feature): with `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_OIDC_ISSUER` set, a bearer JWT whose `iss` is that issuer is verified against the provider's own discovery document and JWKS. Fail-closed: HTTPS only (loopback excepted); the discovery `issuer` and the token's `iss` must equal the setting exactly; `OIDC_AUDIENCES` is required and one must be in `aud`; RS, PS, ES and EdDSA only (`none`, HS and `oct` keys refused); `exp` required, `nbf` honoured, 60 seconds of leeway; a present `email` must be verified unless `OIDC_REQUIRE_VERIFIED_EMAIL=false`; roles or groups from `OIDC_ROLES_CLAIM` (default `roles`, dotted paths allowed) reach ABAC only through `OIDC_ROLE_MAP`. Keys are fetched at boot and again for an unknown key id, at most once a minute. A configured issuer satisfies the start-up gate (as a PASETO key URL does). `jsonwebtoken` 9 becomes a direct dependency (already in the tree; it uses `ring`). NFR-18, the service spec and `operations/deployment.md` state that the accepted set names no product or country. Tests: four unit tests (a token verifies and maps only listed groups; wrong issuer, audience, expiry, `nbf`, unverified email, no subject, unknown key, HS256 signed with the public key and a swapped payload all refused; settings fail closed; dotted role paths) and `tests/oidc_provider.rs`, which boots the service against a stand-in provider on loopback (writer 200, administrator-only route 403 then 200 for a mapped admin, wrong audience and expired 401). Gates run: fmt, clippy, unit tests, `oidc_provider` and `enforcement` on a throwaway Postgres database. **Not done:** one issuer per deployment; the interface's sign-in flow was not changed or re-checked; not tried against any real provider; the external `authentication-verifier` crate is still not audited. **Not verified:** CI before the push.

### 31 October (WW-1.11, handling marks in any script)

- **A handling-mark scheme can be written in any script (WW-1.11, accepted 2026-10-31).** `HANDLING_MARKS` codes were ASCII letters and digits only; they are now letters and digits of any script (`char::is_alphanumeric`), plus space, `-`, `_` and `/`, 1 to 40 characters, and two codes differing only in case are refused in any script. `X-Handling-Mark` could not carry a non-ASCII mark (the header was silently left off); it now sends one as the RFC 8187 extended value `UTF-8''` + percent-encoded UTF-8, and an ASCII mark as before. Codes and clearances are compared exactly as written. Tests: a unit test (Chinese, Arabic, German and Cyrillic schemes; a case-only duplicate refused; the header value encoded and accepted by `HeaderValue`). Gates run: fmt, clippy, unit tests, `tests/handling_marks.rs` on a throwaway Postgres database (ASCII scheme, unchanged). **Not done:** no Unicode normalization of codes or clearances; no request test with a non-ASCII scheme (the scheme is read once per process from the environment). **Not verified:** CI before the push.

### 31 October (WW-1.10, plan codes in any script)

- **A plan code in another script no longer collapses to empty, and Soundex no longer encodes a Latin fragment of a non-Latin name (WW-1.10, accepted 2026-10-31).** In the matcher source, `normalize::code()` applies NFKC and keeps alphanumerics of every script, so the deterministic same-owner code rule (R-1) and the code component can match `项目-2026`, `Проект 7` or `परियोजना-१`; full-width forms equal ASCII. Behaviour change: an accented code keeps its accents (`café-1` is now `CAFÉ1`). `phonetic::soundex` removes accents and spells out `ß` `æ` `ø` `œ` `ł` `đ` `þ` first, and declines a name holding any other script's letter. Stated limits, in the matcher spec (§8, §9) and the service spec: phonetic matching is Latin-only; case folding is Unicode's default, not Turkish or Azerbaijani; matching and search do not segment Chinese, Japanese or Thai into words. Tests: two matcher unit tests. Gates run: matcher fmt, clippy, all tests. **Not done:** no matcher release (the service depends on the published 0.2.1, so its matching and search behave as before until one is cut; publishing is not part of this work); no phonetic algorithm for other scripts; no segmentation. **Not verified:** CI before the push.

### 31 October (WW-1.9, the PDF's Latin-1 limit is stated and visible)

- **The PDFs' Latin-1 limit is a named limit, and a PDF says when it hit it (WW-1.9, accepted 2026-10-31, the "named limit" branch).** `pdf.rs` embeds no font, so the board pack, plan summary, director pack and meeting PDFs carry Latin-1 only (Welsh `ŵ` and `ŷ`, Arabic, Chinese, Hindi and other scripts become `?`) and their fixed words are English. That stays; it is now stated as the limit in `pdf.rs`, `director_pack_text.rs`, the service spec and the OpenAPI text, with the record's JSON and the interface's translated view as the route for other scripts. New: when any character was replaced, the PDF's last line counts them and names that route, so a reader is not left with silent question marks. Tests: a unit test (a Welsh title and Chinese line give a count of 3; a Latin-1 document has no note). Gates run: fmt, clippy, unit tests, the PDF, meetings, `director_packs` and `capacity_heatmap` database tests on a throwaway Postgres database (the meeting golden file is unchanged, being Latin-1). **Not done:** no Unicode font, shaping or right-to-left layout in the PDF; the note is English. **Not verified:** that a PDF reader shows the note as intended (only the text layer was checked); CI before the push.

### 31 October (WW-1.8, erasure policy per deployment, neutral export names)

- **A deployment chooses whether erasure pseudonymises or deletes, and the exports are named for no one law (WW-1.8, accepted 2026-10-31).** New `POST /api/admin/subjects/{ref}/erase` (administrators only; a preview unless `?apply=true`; one transaction) follows `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_ERASURE_POLICY`: `pseudonymise` (the default; the same stable salted pseudonym as `…/pseudonymise`, which keeps its fixed policy) or `delete`, which deletes the rows that exist only for that person (`subjects::OWN_ROWS`: pack subscriptions, feed tokens, notification preferences and destinations, notifications, planned slots, reactions, saved views, watches) and replaces every other reference, the audit trail's actor included, with a random version-8 UUID that is neither returned nor recorded. Shared records (tasks, risks, audit rows) are never deleted. Any other value answers `503` and changes nothing; the erasure is audited as `subject_erased` with counts only. The per-plan export and its code are now "data-subject access" instead of "GDPR right-of-access"; `spec/12-compliance.md` §12.2a says how the laws it lists name the rights and that each maps to the same routes. The DPIA (risk 7), the DTAC checklist row, `operations/deployment.md`, the OpenAPI text and both specs say so. Tests: unit tests (the setting, a typo refused, the random marker, the person-only list inside the column list and free of shared tables); a request test (unset is pseudonymise, a typo is 503, a preview deletes nothing, Ada's own watch and notification deleted and Bob's kept, her task and audit row re-pointed to a reference that is neither her nor her pseudonym); the administrator-only gate covers the new route. Gates run: fmt, clippy, unit tests, the subjects request tests and `finance_gate` on a throwaway Postgres database. **Not done:** no screen; free text, uploaded files, audit snapshots and backups are still not searched; the names of the rights per law were written from general knowledge and not checked against each law's text; no law was read to decide which policy any country requires. **Not verified:** CI before the push.

### 31 October (WW-1.7, retention periods per deployment)

- **Every retention period is a deployment setting, and the scheduled purge reads them (WW-1.7, accepted 2026-10-31).** The periods were defaults in `retention.rs`; the task could override them per run but the scheduled `retention_purge` job passed none, so a deployment could not change them by configuration. Now `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_RETENTION_<RULE>_DAYS` (`NOTIFICATIONS`, `WEBHOOK_LOG`, `SINK_LOG`, `IDEMPOTENCY_KEYS`, `INSIGHT_SNAPSHOTS`, `ADOPTION_SNAPSHOTS`; 1 to 36 500 calendar days) sets each one for the task and the job; a command-line `<rule>_days:` still wins. A value outside the range fails the run instead of purging by the default. Each default now states its reason (`Rule::basis`); none was taken from a law. The audit trail's proposed 6 years is recorded as known gap 5 in `governance/retention-schedule.md`: no rule purges `audit_logs`, so it is policy only. Tests: unit tests (unset keeps the defaults, two set, a bad one refused naming the setting; every rule states a basis). Gates run: fmt, clippy, all unit tests, the retention request test on a throwaway Postgres database. **Not done:** no per-organization period; no purge rule for `AUD`, `OPS`, `PPL`, the rest of `COM` or `DOC`. **Not verified:** the scheduled job and the task were not run against a database with a setting; CI before the push.

### 31 October (WW-1.1 follow-up: page catalogues load per locale)

- **CI stayed red after the previous fix, and this is the cause and the fix.** Run 41190266757 (`d79d6a5`) failed six axe tests again, in English as well as in other locales (colour contrast on `.lede` and `.muted` before the theme applied, no document title, unnamed paging buttons): the pages were audited before they had finished hydrating. WW-1.1 had put all six new page catalogues into `i18n.svelte.ts`'s static imports, so every page loaded about 2.4 MB of JavaScript (the largest chunk went from about 0.7 MB to 2.46 MB) whatever its language. Now the Arabic, German, Spanish, French, Hindi and Chinese page catalogues are loaded only for their locale (`loadLocale`, a dynamic import merged into that locale's table); the root layout's load awaits it before the page is drawn, a locale switch loads it too, and `t` and `tn` draw again when it arrives. English and Welsh stay static, as before. The largest chunk is 0.72 MB again; each language is its own 0.24 to 0.41 MB chunk. Unit tests load every catalogue first (`tests/unit/setup-locales.ts`). UI spec: the locale section says so. Gates run: UI lint, check, vitest (974 passed, 3 skipped), Playwright. **Not done:** text rendered on the server outside a page load (for example by a hook) in one of the six locales reads English page text if that catalogue was not loaded yet in that server process; no test checks that a page loads only its own catalogue (the chunk sizes above were read from one build). **Not verified:** CI before the push.

### 31 October (WW-1.1 follow-up: the every-locale spec runs on its own)

- **CI's UI job went red after WW-1.1, and this is the fix.** On run 41187807776 (`1983adb`), 10 axe tests (calendar, critical chain, deployment moves, director packs, email deliveries, kanban, organizations, start queue) found no title, no `main` and no `h1`: the page had not been drawn when axe ran, because `every-locale.spec.ts` (about 900 page loads) was running beside them on the same preview server; 978 passed. `playwright.config.ts` now runs that spec as its own project, `every-locale`, which depends on the `chromium` and `mobile` projects and so starts after them; the same single `playwright test` command runs everything. Gates run: Playwright locally, every project (988 passed). **Not done:** the ten tests' own waits were not changed (they wait for the network to go idle, not for the heading), so a slow runner could still catch them early. **Not verified:** CI before the push; if `chromium` or `mobile` fails, `every-locale` is skipped, not run.

### 31 October (WW-1.6, small-cell thresholds per deployment)

- **The small-cell minimum and the suppression floor are deployment settings, and the answer names them (WW-1.6, accepted 2026-10-31).** `MIN_GROUP_PEOPLE = 5` and `DEFAULT_SUPPRESSION_FLOOR_MINUTES = 2400` were constants that nothing could override. Now `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_MIN_GROUP_PEOPLE` (2 to 1000; 1 is refused, as it would publish one person's figure as a group's) and `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_SUPPRESSION_FLOOR_MINUTES` (0 to 100 000) set them, with the old values as stated defaults; `GET /api/capacity/utilization` answers with `suppression: {floor_minutes, min_group_people}`. The code comments, the OpenAPI description, the service spec and `operations/deployment.md` say why each default was chosen (a common disclosure-control convention; one nominal week) and that no law sets either. Tests: unit tests of both settings and of a group of six withheld under a minimum of ten; the request test checks the answer names the defaults. Gates run: fmt, clippy, unit tests, the effort request tests and `private_aggregates` on a throwaway Postgres 18 database. **Not done:** no per-organization or per-plan value; no request test runs with a non-default setting (the settings are read from the environment, which the shared request-test process does not change). **Not verified:** CI before the push.

### 31 October (WW-1.5, no country's financial year by default)

- **The financial year is the calendar year unless the deployment says otherwise (WW-1.5, accepted 2026-10-31).** `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_FINANCIAL_YEAR_START_MONTH` defaulted to 4 (April, the UK government's year); unset it is now 1 (January), as the contact relationship service's fiscal year starts (CRM-T171). **Behaviour change:** a deployment that relied on the April default must now set `4`, or `GET /api/portfolio-money` and the director pack's funding section group by calendar year (labelled `2026` instead of `2026/27`). `financial_year::parse_start_month` holds the rule; the OpenAPI description, `operations/deployment.md` and the service spec say so. Tests: a unit test (unset is January; 4, 7, 10 and 12 taken; bad values fall back; July and October years labelled), and `tests/funding.rs` now sets an April year itself for its 31 March / 1 April checks and checks that with no setting the year is 1 January to 31 December. Gates run: fmt, clippy, unit tests, `tests/funding.rs` on a throwaway Postgres 18 database. **Not done:** no per-organization setting in the database or the UI (CRM has one; one deployment is one organization here); the UI's own date grouping was not checked for an April assumption. **Not verified:** CI before the push.

### 31 October (WW-1.4, the working week from the plan's calendar)

- **Planned hours follow each plan's own calendar and the organization's working day (WW-1.4, accepted 2026-10-31).** Service only. `GET /api/resource-plan/hours` counted every Monday to Friday at a fixed 480 minutes, whatever the plan's calendar said; now a plan's calendar (its weekday mask and holidays) decides its working days, and a day is the organization's `POST /api/working-time` length. Where neither is set, two new deployment settings fill in, both stated as defaults and not as a rule: `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_DEFAULT_WORKING_WEEK` (`1111100`, Monday first) and `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_MINUTES_PER_DAY` (480), read by the new `src/working_time.rs`; a bad value falls back to the default. The MS Project export of a plan with no calendar wrote `1111100`, but such a plan is scheduled on every day, so the file now says so. `operations/deployment.md` lists the two settings. Tests: unit tests of the two settings and of planned hours across two plans with different weeks (Sunday to Thursday, Monday to Saturday) and a 450-minute day; the request test now sets a plan's calendar (Monday to Saturday with a holiday, then every day) and the organization's day and checks the planned minutes, and the export test checks all seven days work for a calendar-less plan. Gates run: fmt, clippy, unit tests, the request suite and `private_aggregates` on a throwaway Postgres 18 database. **Not done:** no week-start setting in the UI (its grids stay Monday first and the calendar widget Sunday first); the 480 minutes that tie work, duration and units together in `scheduling_rules.rs` stay fixed, because stored task data depends on them; the MSPDI file still carries 480 minutes and 08:00 to 17:00; capacity and utilization elsewhere were not re-read for a Monday-to-Friday assumption beyond the ones the audit named. ISO 8601 weeks stay the stated default (a limit: the international standard, and the API's week buckets). **Not verified:** CI before the push.

### 31 October (WW-1.2, data-protection law by region)

- **The compliance mapping names no one country's law as the default (WW-1.2, accepted 2026-10-31).** Docs only. `spec/12-compliance.md` §12.1 and the §12.2 framework row now say "the applicable data-protection law" (GDPR and the UK GDPR as two examples); new §12.2a is a table of 13 regions (EU and EEA, UK, Switzerland, Canada, the United States, Brazil, the rest of Latin America, India, China, Japan with South Korea and Singapore, Australia and New Zealand, Africa, the Middle East and Türkiye) giving the law, what engages it here, and what it drives for erasure, residency and transfer, and breach notice, with what the software gives every row and what is the deployer's. The HIPAA, NHS and ONC "not engaged" rows stay as stated limits. `governance/record-of-processing.md` (the Article 30 line, section 4) and `governance/dpia.md` (row 11) name the applicable law and cross-border transfers instead of "UK GDPR / EU GDPR" and "outside the UK or EEA". **Not verified:** the table was written from general knowledge on 2026-10-31 and was not checked against any law's current text, deadlines included; it says so, and that it is not legal advice. **Not done:** US state laws are named by one example, not listed; the persona documents and the DTAC folders still name UK bodies (WW-1.19, WW-1.21).

### 31 October (WW-1.1, every locale complete)

- **Every supported locale has the whole interface text, not only Welsh (WW-1.1, accepted 2026-10-31).** New page catalogues `src/lib/i18n-pages.ar.ts`, `.de.ts`, `.es.ts`, `.fr.ts`, `.hi.ts` and `.zh.ts` (4,008 keys each, typed as complete records of the English keys, so a missing one is a compile error), spread into `ar-001`, `de-001` and `de-de`, `es-001`, `fr-001`, `hi-001`, `zh-001` and `zh-cn`; until now those eight showed English for every page and component text. **All of it is machine-written by Claude (the AI assistant) and unreviewed** (each file's header says so). Review records, one per locale, in `governance/translation-reviews/` (status, scope, how a review is done, known weak spots, an empty log), with a working CSV each written by the new `scripts/export-translation-review.py` (`--check` runs in CI's docs job); Welsh keeps its own record and CSV. NFR-19 now asks the same of every locale; the UI spec's locale section (it still listed 13 locales from an old plan) and the language statement say what is true now. Tests: `tests/unit/catalogue-coverage.test.ts` checks keys, placeholders and copied English for each catalogue (Arabic, Hindi and Chinese may leave only names and code; German 114, Spanish 52 and French 137 strings are the same word as the English, such as Status, Plan or Description, and those counts may only fall); `tests/unit/review-records.test.ts` (a record for every locale but English, listed in the index, none claiming a review it does not log, a working file each); `tests/e2e/every-locale.spec.ts` opens every page route in English and then in each other locale and fails on a line of English (lines that are the same word in German, Spanish or French, 23, 11 and 23 of them, such as Status, Ticket or Documents, are listed in the spec); three unit tests that relied on the English fallback (`i18n.test.ts`, `plurals.test.ts`) now check that no locale falls back, and the two source scans skip every catalogue file, not only Welsh's. **Not done:** no speaker or translator has read any of it; plural groups hold only `_one` and `_other`, so Arabic's zero, two, few and many read the `_other` wording; the Lily widget label packs are unchanged (WW-1.17); text the service writes stays English; the existing Welsh export script does not read single-quoted catalogue values (the new script does), which was not checked against the Welsh CSV. Gates run: UI lint, check, vitest (974 passed, 3 skipped), Playwright (the 888 existing tests and the 100 new ones pass; the new spec adds about 3 minutes locally); the doc scripts with `--check`. **Not verified:** CI before the push; the translations were not read in place beyond the browser test's check that a line differs from English; right to left beyond Chromium.

### 31 October (Redis 8 for tests)

- **Tests and CI use Redis 8 (owner decision, 2026-10-31).** `redis:7-alpine` became `redis:8-alpine` in the service's `compose.test.yaml` and in CI's `ui` job service container, pinned by major tag as the repository pins its other images. Redis 8 is under a choice of AGPL-3.0, RSALv2 or SSPLv1; the new "Services used only for development and testing" section of `LICENSE.md` says so and that it is used for development and tests only, as do comments in both files; the root `AGENTS.md` gate line says Redis 8. Run locally against Redis 8.10.3: the UI's `tests/unit/session-store.test.ts` with `REDIS_TEST_URL` (25 passed, the three real-Redis tests among them), the OIDC sign-in browser spec with `OIDC_SESSION_STORE=redis` (7 passed; Redis's command counters showed the store's GET, SET, GETDEL and DEL), and the service (the local debug build of 2026-10-30 07:05 UTC; the two service commits since change one line of text each, in `openapi.rs` and `wpm_bridge.rs`) started in `production` against it: `/_readiness` answered 200 while Redis ran and 503 after it was stopped. **Not done:** the root `compose.yaml` (the example deployment) stays on `redis:7-alpine`, because the decision was for development and tests and moving a deployment changes the licence it runs under; images are pinned by tag, not digest (as the others are). **Not verified:** the local runs used Homebrew's `redis-server` 8.10.3, not the container image, because Podman on this machine failed to mount any new container (an overlay `input/output error`, for Redis 7 and 8 alike, rootless and rootful), and restarting its machine would have stopped other projects' containers; the `8-alpine` image itself runs first in CI. The service has no automated test against Redis (its tests use the in-memory cache), and the cached critical-path answer was not exercised.

### 30 October (numbers, dates and plurals as the workforce UI has them)

About 60 hand-rolled `toFixed`, `toLocale*`, glued "%" and ISO-sliced dates in about 50 files now go through the module, and about 45 `n === 1 ? …_one : …_other` choices go through `tn`; the five English "(s)" strings are plural groups, with machine-written Welsh logged unreviewed in `governance/welsh-translation-review.csv`; `cpm-view`'s English-order "n business day(s)" uses `businessDays`; the Gantt list's dates no longer shift a day west of UTC (`toLocaleDateString` on a UTC midnight). Tests: `tests/unit/locale-format.test.ts` (WPM's, with PPM's locales: German, French, Hindi, Spanish, Welsh, Chinese, Arabic arrows; the views in German and Welsh; a scan of `src/` that fails on new hand-rolled formatting, with four allowed keys and an SVG path; a count of catalogue strings with "%" after a placeholder that may only fall); `tests/unit/plurals.test.ts` (CLDR categories for English, Arabic, Welsh, French, Chinese; fallbacks; every group's English forms; no "(s)"; a scan for a plural form picked by hand); the unused-key census counts a key used through its plural base. Five existing unit tests and ten browser specs (critical path, critical chain, cutover, deployment moves, documents, earned value, large plans, lifecycle, people impact) now expect "14 Oct 2026" where they expected "2026-10-14", and the lifecycle spec the plural wording. **Not done (for the owner, or later):** 30 catalogue strings in English and Welsh still put "%" after a number placeholder (English placement in every locale); numbers placed into page-catalogue strings are not locale-formatted (`interpolate` prints them as given); Welsh has only one and other per plural group (it also has zero, two, few, many); dates the scan does not see stay ISO (several table columns, the costs page's bucket headers, the network diagram's "MM-DD", "→" ranges in the variance and leveling tables, the capacity window and burndown dates, the critical chain page's "critical path finishes on"); the other eight locales still fall back to English page text (WW-1.1); Gregorian only. ### 30 October (money in the ISO 4217 exponent in the UI)

- **The UI scales money by the ISO 4217 minor unit, the service's, not CLDR's.** `src/lib/money.ts` took a currency's exponent from `Intl.NumberFormat().resolvedOptions().maximumFractionDigits`, which comes from CLDR; for 16 codes (AFN, ALL, COP, HUF, IDR, IQD, IRR, KPW, LAK, LBP, MGA, MMK, PKR, SOS, SYP, YER) CLDR shows 0 places where ISO 4217, and the service's `src/currency.rs`, has 2 (3 for IQD). So "1500" forints typed in a form were sent as 1500 minor units, which the service reads as 15.00 forints. The UI now holds the ISO table (`MINOR_UNITS`, the same as `currency.rs`, as the CRM UI does since its commit `37ef205`), asks `Intl` only for a code the table does not hold, and formats with `minimumFractionDigits` and `maximumFractionDigits` both set to the ISO exponent, so what is shown, typed and stored agree. The header comment says the family's three `money.ts` files (this one, CRM's and WPM's) share the rule. Spec: the UI spec's Money section and entity spec §5 (the modelling decision on money). Tests (`tests/unit/money.test.ts`, 10 new): the table equals `currency.rs`, read from the service directory and parsed; the 16 codes; the `Intl` fallback; round trips typed, stored and shown for HUF, IDR, COP, JPY, KWD and CLF (in `en-GB`, and back through `de-DE`); a forint amount keeps its two places in `hu-HU`. Six of them failed on the old `money.ts`. Gates run: UI lint, check, vitest and Playwright; the doc scripts with `--check`.
  **Stored amounts that may be wrong.** The `Intl` exponent came in with `956d9ed` (2026-10-29 15:00 UTC, WW-1.3), on `main` after the `v0.2.0` tag, so only a deployment running `main` from then until this change is affected. In it, an amount typed into the UI in one of the 16 currencies was stored 100 times too small (1000 times for IQD). The forms that write money are a plan's costs (task cost lines), cost-rate tables (rates), governance (budget lines and their low and high estimates), risk cost impacts (critical path), proposals (amount requested) and scenarios (budget cap). An operator should list the records in those currencies created or changed through the UI in that window and check each against its source; one that is 100 (or 1000) times too small is corrected by hand through the UI or the API. Amounts sent to the API directly, and amounts in every other currency, are not affected; the old code only displayed correct amounts in those currencies rounded to whole units. **Not done:** no data migration, deliberately, because a stored amount cannot tell whether it came from the UI or from the API; no script lists the affected rows; no deployment was inspected. For a code not in the table the service still uses 2 and the UI asks `Intl` (the same as CRM; they differ only for a code neither table lists). WPM's `money.ts` was not checked or changed here. Amounts in non-2-exponent currencies stored before `956d9ed` (the fixed `/100`) were not re-examined. Not verified against a running service or in CI before the push.

- **A documentation site for GitHub Pages, built and not published (GP-1).** New subproject `project-portfolio-management.github.io/`, the family pattern of the workforce repository's `spec/monorepo-github-pages/`: SvelteKit 3 with `adapter-static` (all 126 pages prerendered), Svelte 5 runes, Lily `ThemePicker` (light and dark, from the system preference), `TextSizePicker` and, on the role briefs, `LocalePicker`, which opens the same brief in the chosen locale. Pages: `index.md`, `README.md`, `INSTALL.md`, `operations/wpm-bridge.md`, `spec/index.md`, the skill's `SKILL.md` and the 110 role briefs with a list per locale. Links between those documents stay on the site, other repository links go to GitHub, raw HTML is shown as text. The colour tokens are the UI's with the `49675dc` contrast fix; the content security policy is a meta tag with SvelteKit's hashes, and no element has a `style` attribute. The documents are copied into `src/content/docs.json` by `scripts/sync-content.mjs` so the exported repository builds alone (`--check` in CI). `make github-pages` runs `bin/make-github-pages` (renamed `make publish-github-pages` and `bin/publish-github-pages` on 13 November; `git subtree push --prefix=project-portfolio-management.github.io github-pages main`, after checking the remote, the content copy and a clean directory); the exported repository's own `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` deploys it. New CI job `site` (content check, `svelte-check`, build, Playwright with axe); a Dependabot entry for the site. Spec: `spec/github-pages-site.md`; tasks GP-1 (ticked) and GP-2 (publish, open); `llms.json` lists the spec and the site's README, which has the publish steps. Gates run: the site's content check, `svelte-check`, build and Playwright (67 tests: every page answers 200; axe WCAG 2.2 AA tags with no serious or critical finding on the 6 documents, 10 locale lists and one brief per locale under light and dark; four pages under a forced `dir="rtl"`; the locale switch, `lang`, link rewriting, phone width, no CSP report); the doc scripts with `--check`. The UI's gates were not run: nothing in the UI changed. **Not done:** no `github-pages` remote, no exported repository and no push (the owner does these; GP-2); the exported repository's deploy workflow has never run; the site's own words are English only; no brief locale is right-to-left, so right to left is tested only by forcing `dir`; only the `light` and `dark` themes; the first paint (or a visit without JavaScript) uses the light fallback tokens whatever the system preference; `frame-ancestors` cannot be set from a meta tag; 100 of the 110 briefs are checked for a 200 answer but not audited with axe.

The error banner's fixed `#fef2f2` background, which measured 1.6:1 under the dark theme's light danger text on 26 routes, is now a tint of the page background, as the success banner's is. `tests/e2e/a11y.spec.ts` now audits every page route (100) under the dark system preference as well as the light: before the fix all 100 failed under dark (the sign-in button on every page, then the error banner on 26), after it all 200 pass.
