### 30 October (family bridges, FB-5)

- **Withdrawing a figure from the workforce service (FB-5).** Service only. Under the family contract decided 2026-10-30, a programme's demand total of zero on a linked pool is sent to WPM as `fte_centi: 0` (WPM withdraws its claim), every peer-demand send carries `sent_at` (the time of the PPM write, so WPM can ignore an older event arriving late), and unlinking or relinking a pool withdraws, best effort, every figure sent to the old WPM pool, each send recorded. Entity spec §8.5a rules 5 and 6, service spec §9.10a, `tasks.md` FB-5. Tests: a unit test against a stand-in WPM (exact bodies), `tests/wpm_bridge.rs`. **Not verified:** against a running WPM (its side is being built in parallel; FB-8); CI. **Not done:** no retry; the withdrawals on unlink are not shown on `/skill-pools`.

### 30 October (family bridges, FB-6)

- **The bridge on screen (FB-6, in part).** Interface only; the service is not changed. `/skill-pools` lists the pools with their link to the workforce service's pool and sets or removes it (`PUT /api/skill-pools/{pid}/peer-link`): the id is checked on the page as a lower-case hyphenated UUID, 409 and 422 are said plainly, removal is confirmed, and the page says the link is set on both sides by a person who can see both and never matched by name. `/capacity-heatmap` draws `GET /api/capacity-heatmap` pool by month with the service's word and shape beside the tone, and for a linked pool the source ("from the workforce service"), its reading (live, last received with its age, hidden, unknown with the reason, never zero) and what was entered here beside it, never added. Both in the top navigation. Entity spec §8.5a table, UI spec (cross-app bridges), `tasks.md` FB-6. English and machine-written Welsh (unreviewed, in `governance/welsh-translation-review.csv`). Tests: `tests/unit/capacity-heatmap-view.test.ts` (10), `tests/e2e/wpm-bridge.spec.ts` (5, axe; link set, refused before sending, 409, 422, clear with confirm, heatmap live/last received/hidden/unknown, Welsh). **Not built:** the demand send outcome (`peer_demand_send`) is not shown: the interface has no form that sets a capacity commitment. **Not verified:** against a running service or WPM (the browser tests stub the service; FB-8); a screen reader; CI.

### 30 October (family bridges, FB-2 caller-keyed store)

- **A kept WPM supply figure reaches only the caller whose token fetched it (FB-2 fix, WPM-D135).** A live end-to-end run found that when WPM could not be reached, `GET /api/capacity-heatmap` fell back to the last figure received for any caller, so a figure fetched under one caller's token reached another. Service: the in-process store in `src/wpm_bridge.rs` is now keyed by caller as well as pool and month: the authenticated subject (`sub:<sub>`), else the SHA-256 of the bearer token (`token:<hex>`; the token itself is never kept); a request with neither keeps and recalls nothing. It is bounded at 1 000 callers (the one not seen for longest is dropped) and 2 400 figures per caller (the oldest-received is dropped). **Design choice:** the views that make no call (director pack, drum, pipeline, start check) now show **no kept figure** (a linked pool's supply is `unknown` there, with the reason) instead of the reader's own: the pack is stored and read by others, and the start check looks at every plan for no one caller, so keying those by caller would still pass one caller's figure to another. Entity spec §8.5a rule 4 (the leak is closed; what remains is listed), service spec §9.10a, `tasks.md` FB-2, the heatmap's OpenAPI text and `openapi.json`. Tests: 4 unit tests (subject else token hash, never the token; A's figure not recalled for B; both caps; through `supply_at` with WPM unreachable: A sees its own `last_received`, B and a caller with no key see `unknown`, a no-call view sees `unknown`); `tests/wpm_bridge.rs` now checks that a second caller with WPM down gets `unknown`, not the first caller's figure. **Not done or not verified:** figures are still lost at restart; one subject's figure is still shown to the same subject under another of its tokens, even one WPM would answer differently; the drum and pipeline now treat a linked pool's supply as unknown, which can change which pool is the drum when that pool is linked (no test covers a drum over a linked pool); the subject key is `sub` alone, so two issuers (PASETO and Keycloak) giving the same `sub` would share figures (not seen, not tested); no live check against a running WPM (FB-8); CI.

### 30 October (family bridges, FB-4)

- **People impact in from the workforce service (FB-4).** Service: `GET /api/plans/{pid}/people-impact[?months=1..36]` asks WPM's `GET /api/capacity/people-impact?programme=thing:<pid>` under the caller's own token (2-second timeout, no redirects), sending `ends_on` from the plan's own `target_date` when it is a full date (none otherwise, with `plan_ends_on_reason`; WPM's `unknown` for the engagements part then passes through). WPM's answer is passed through unchanged with `source: "wpm"` (`as_of`, `months`, `floor`, the three parts with `known`/`reason`, hidden counts `null` with `hidden: true`, no total), or `status: "unknown"` with a reason and every part unknown, every figure `null`, when not configured, no token (nothing called), unreachable, slow, 5xx, unreadable or refused (4xx). Nothing is kept, so a refused caller never sees another caller's answer. Entity spec §8.5a rule 8 and table; service spec §9.10a; `src/wpm_bridge.rs`, `src/controllers/people_impact.rs`, `openapi_more.rs`, `api-routes.txt`, `openapi.json`. Interface: a "People impact (from the workforce service)" box on a programme's plan page, asked only on a button press; hidden shown as "hidden (fewer than N)", never zero; English and Welsh strings (Welsh machine-written, unreviewed, listed in `governance/welsh-translation-review.csv`; other locales fall back to English). Tests: 6 unit tests with a stand-in axum WPM on a local port (live with exact query and forwarded token, no end date, 403, unreachable, slow, 503, unreadable, no URL, no token), `tests/wpm_bridge.rs` extended (route end to end incl. 422 window, private plan 404 with WPM not asked, stub mode), `tests/unit/people-impact.test.ts` (3), `tests/e2e/people-impact.spec.ts` (3, with axe). **Not built:** no cache (none needed); the people impact is not in the director pack or any portfolio view; the reasons WPM gives are English text and are shown untranslated. **Not verified:** against a running WPM (FB-8): every test uses a stand-in answering with the shape read from WPM's `controllers/capacity/people_impact.rs`; the stand-in's answer is not checked against WPM's real output, so a later change to WPM's shape would read as `unknown` (unreadable) or pass through unnoticed for fields PPM does not check; CI. No new aggregate GET (the route has a path parameter, so `tests/private_aggregates.rs` does not enumerate it, and it is one plan's read behind the private-plan guard) and no new list for `PAGED_LISTS`.

### 30 October (family bridges, FB-1 to FB-3)

- **The portfolio service's half of the bridge to the workforce service (WPM): the pool link, supply in, demand out (FB-1 to FB-3).** Entity spec §8.5a (new) records the family contract and a table of every bridge (CRM → PPM ideas exists; PPM ↔ WPM pool link, supply and demand built here; WPM → PPM people impact not built; WPM → CRM hire and leave webhooks are CRM's). Service: `skill_pools.peer_pool_ref` and `PUT /api/skill-pools/{pid}/peer-link` (a lower-case UUID or null, never a name, one PPM pool per WPM pool, audited with no figure); for a linked pool `GET /api/capacity-heatmap` counts WPM's supply alone (`GET {WPM}/api/capacity/peer-supply`, under the caller's own token, 2-second timeout), labelled `supply_source: "wpm"` with `peer_supply` `live`, `last_received` (time taken and age), `hidden` or `unknown` (state `supply_unknown`, never zero), and what was entered here as `entered_here_supply_fte`, never added; a demand commitment on a linked pool sends the programme's total for the pool and month to `POST {WPM}/api/capacity/peer-demand` (`thing:<plan pid>`, hundredths of an FTE, an id per version) after the write, with the outcome in the `PUT` answer and on `GET /api/capacity-commitments` (table `peer_demand_sends`, migration 99). Stub-first: with no `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_WORKER_SERVICE_URL` nothing is called. New `src/wpm_bridge.rs` (unit tests for the rules, rounding, the event id, the readings, a slow and an answering stand-in); database test `tests/wpm_bridge.rs` (its own binary, with a stand-in WPM on a local port: link, 422/404/409, audit with no figure, live/down/slow/hidden/unknown, token forwarded, exact body, total across organizations, a refused send recorded, zero not sent, unlink returns local figures, stub mode). Tasks FB-1 to FB-8 in `tasks.md`. **Not built:** PPM's client for WPM's people-impact route (FB-4, next); a withdrawal of a demand figure (WPM refuses zero and has no withdraw route, so a total set to zero here is **not sent** and WPM keeps the last figure, FB-5); no outbox or retry for a failed send (the caller's token is never stored; setting the commitment again sends again); the UI (FB-6: the interface has no capacity-heatmap or skill-pool screen to put the labels and link control on, so no UI was changed); the worker-skills route `workers_client` calls does not exist on WPM and still reports `unknown` (FB-7). **Not verified:** **no call against a running WPM** (the stand-in answers with the shapes read from WPM's `src/controllers/capacity/peer.rs`; FB-8); two sends racing to WPM could land out of order there (PPM's record keeps the newer, WPM has no version check); supply figures are kept in process only (lost at restart, and one caller's figure is shown to the next caller for that pool); the drum, pipeline, start check and director pack use only figures already received and make no call; CI. `tests/list_paging.rs` fails on this tree because of `/api/tpc` now needing `currency` (WW-1.3, not this change); it was not touched.

- **A caller WPM refuses no longer sees another caller's kept supply figure (FB-2 fix).** The heatmap fell back to
  the last figure received whenever a live read failed, including when WPM answered 401 or 403 for this caller, so a
  figure fetched under one caller's token reached a caller WPM had refused (against WPM-D135). A refusal now reads as
  `unknown`; only a WPM that cannot be reached falls back. Unit test: a 403 and an unreachable peer are told apart.
  **Not changed:** the views that read kept figures only (director pack, drum, pipeline, start check) still show the
  last figure to any PPM caller who may read the pool, as before; and there is still no live check against a WPM.

### 29 October (money, WW-1.3)

- **Money is worldwide (WW-1.3, WW-1.13 currency items): one formatter and parser, no built-in currency.** Interface: `src/lib/money.ts` formats every amount with `Intl` in the reader's locale and the currency's own number of decimals (JPY 0, EUR 2, KWD 3, CLF 4) and reads what is typed in that locale (`1,5` and `1 234,50` where the locale uses a decimal comma). The hand-rolled formatters (`ppm-types.ts`, `whatif-view.ts`, the ledger and deployment-moves pages) and every money `/100` and `*100` (`simulation-inputs.ts`, `work-view.ts`, `LineRange`, `RiskInputs`, governance, scenarios, proposals, joint risk, change requests) now go through it. The five forms (costs, governance, cost rates, scenarios, proposals) start with an empty currency and refuse an amount without a three-letter currency before sending; the hint reads "for example EUR, JPY or GBP". Money inputs are text with `inputmode="decimal"`, not number inputs, so a comma can be typed. Service: `GET /api/tpc` needs `currency` (`422` when absent or malformed; `openapi.rs` and `openapi.json` say so); `currency.rs` holds the one shape check (`is_code`), `normalize` for hand-typed input, and the ISO 4217 minor-unit table (with CLF and UYW at 4) that `director_pack_text.rs` now uses; error text names EUR, JPY and GBP. Specs: `spec/05-domain-model.md`, `spec/total-project-control/index.md`, the interface spec ("Money"). Tests: `tests/unit/money.test.ts` (JPY, KWD, CLF, `de-DE`, `fr-FR`; the five forms start empty), updated unit tests, `currency.rs` and `director_pack_text.rs` unit tests, and a database test `tpc_triage_needs_a_currency_and_has_no_default`. **Not done or not verified:** the new database test and the changed `/api/tpc` entry in `tests/private_aggregates.rs` were **not run** (no test database here). The text outputs in the service (`director_pack_text.rs`) still group with `,` and `.` and put the code first. Key-result (OKR) currency values and the other number inputs that are not money are unchanged (key results take whole numbers in the unit they name; not audited as minor units). Amounts are shown as `Intl` currency format (a symbol, for example `£1,500.00`), no longer `1500.00 GBP`; a currency the runtime does not know falls back to the code and the minor-unit count. A code the ISO table does not list has two decimals. `governance::valid_currency` and the stored-code checks stay strict (upper-case) while `GET /api/tpc`, `POST .../tpc` and the baseline body still upper-case what they are given. `tz.rs` and `docs.rs` (the rest of WW-1.13) and the other WW-1 findings are not started; WW-1 is not ticked. The Welsh text is machine-written and not reviewed (`governance/welsh-translation-review.csv` lists the new and changed rows); other locales show the English text for the new strings. The interface suite ran in this worktree with a linked `node_modules`; `svelte-check` reports the `./$types` errors of a tree whose generated types are not synced (not caused by this change), and I checked that no other error remains.

### 29 October (spec)

- **Worldwide equity audit (WW-1, audit only): `governance/worldwide-equity-audit.md`.** 25 findings across the service, the interface and the documents, each with a proposed decision (configure, extend, or keep as a stated limit). The four that most clearly break §2.0: only Welsh has a completeness guarantee and tests while six other locales fall back to English silently; the compliance mapping covers UK, EU and US-health law only; money has a GBP default, `en-GB` formatting and a two-decimal assumption; the working week is Monday to Friday and the day 480 minutes in two modules. **Not done / not verified:** **no code, test or spec rule changed**; the decisions are proposals the owner has not accepted; the audit was three read-only pattern searches, the line numbers were not re-checked in context, and nothing was run; the end of the document lists what was not searched (among others the migrations' column defaults, most controller files line by line, and the interface's non-Welsh catalogues).
- **Spec §2.0 "Worldwide target": the target is worldwide, not the United States or the United Kingdom; no standard, protocol, convention or locale gates one country or region, and each must offer equitable capability to countries, regions and federations worldwide.** Also: a capability built for one jurisdiction is named for it, is configurable per deployment, and is never the only one. New open task **WW-1** audits for places that break this. **Not done / not verified:** this is a statement of the rule only; **no code or test changed, and no audit has been made**, so the repository still has uneven coverage (the UK and EU laws in `12-compliance.md`, ten locales, the small-cell minimum of 5 chosen without a stated basis). The rule has no automated check.

### 29 October

- **`/balance`: a portfolio balance view (NC-9).** A bubble chart with one bubble per portfolio (across: planned cost, up: target benefit value, area: open risk exposure), the imbalances stated in words, and the same figures as a table that is always on the page. The three figures come from reads that already exist (`/api/financials/variance` by portfolio, `/api/executive/benefits`, `/api/risk/heatmap` posture) joined on the portfolio; the service is unchanged. Money is shown in one currency at a time (a selector) and never converted or added across currencies. Five flags, thresholds in `balance-view.ts` and printed on the page: a portfolio holds 50% or more of the cost or of the risk (only with two or more groups); its share of cost, or of risk, is 20 points or more above its share of value (not raised when no group has value in that currency); or it has cost and no target value. A flagged bubble has a dashed heavy outline and a "!" and is named in the list. Tests: 17 unit (flags at and just below each threshold, the join, the layout), 5 end-to-end (chart, flags, table, currency switch, empty, failed load shows the catalogue sentence, Welsh, axe on each state); the existing `a11y`, `mobile`, `sitemap` and `hidden-sections` specs pass with the new route. A unit test found a real bug in my first version (0.3 - 0.1 is 0.19999999999999998, so a 20-point gap was missed); shares are now compared to a tenth of a percent. **Not done:** grouping by category or strategic aim, which is what the item asks for (no read reports cost, value and risk together by category or aim, and I did not change the service, so it is grouped by portfolio and the page says so); a drill-through from a bubble to its portfolio; Welsh and the other locales were not reviewed (the Welsh is machine-written and is in `governance/welsh-translation-review.csv`; the other eight locales fall back to English for these strings). **Not verified:** against a real service with real data (every test stubs the three reads), so whether the thresholds suit real portfolios is untested; the page was not checked in a screen reader; bubble labels can overlap when portfolios sit close together (the table and each bubble's `<title>` carry the names); the full Playwright suite was not run, only the specs named above.

- **NC-1, slice 1: a screen to raise and edit a plan's issues, and to raise one from a risk (`/plans/[pid]/issues`; NC-1 stays open).** The issue routes (IR-2, IR-3) existed only at the API; the all-plans register `/issues` is read-only. The new page lists the plan's issues, has one form for raising and editing (the service replaces every field on an edit, so all are sent; the page refuses a missing title, a close without a resolution, and a due date before the raised date before calling), and a list of the plan's risks to raise an issue from (a second attempt is a 409, shown as the catalogue's sentence). New: `IssueBody` and four client methods (the body is checked against `openapi.json` by `client-bodies.test.ts`), `src/lib/issue-view.ts`, 32 catalogue keys in English and Welsh (Welsh machine-written, appended to `governance/welsh-translation-review.csv`), a plan-page link in module `governance`, a section in the UI `spec/index.md`. Verified: lint, svelte-check, vitest, and Playwright for `plan-issues`, `issues`, axe, mobile, sitemap and plan-navigation specs. **Not done:** deleting an issue; a "raise as issue" button on the governance page's risk rows; raise from a task; `priority`; an owner picker (the owner is a typed `person:` or `worker:` reference); all later NC-1 slices. **Not verified:** a real service (the e2e stubs it); CI; the full Playwright suite; the Welsh is unreviewed.

- **NC-10: `/roadmap`, an impact / effort matrix and Now / Next / Later over ideas and proposals (UI only).** The service has **no impact or effort** for an idea or a proposal and none was added, so a person rates each open idea and each undecided proposal Low / Medium / High, and the ratings are kept in that browser's `localStorage` (not shared, not on the service). Unrated items are listed apart and never placed. The matrix is a real table; the "Rate the items" table (impact and effort selects per row) is its keyboard alternative and the way ratings are set. Now / Next / Later follows a stated rule (impact above effort is Now; equal and at least Medium is Next; else Later). Linked from `/ideas` and `/proposals`; Welsh strings are machine-written and added to `governance/welsh-translation-review.csv`. Verified: lint (cap 0), svelte-check, vitest (840 pass), `roadmap.spec.ts` (6 pass, including axe in `en-001` and `cy-001`). **Not done:** shared or service-saved ratings; drag on the matrix; a top-bar entry; other locales show English. **Not verified:** CI; a screen reader; a real service (answers stubbed); the full Playwright suite (see the final report for which other specs were run).

### 26 October (later)

- **The director pack sends "what changed" as figures; the page no longer has to read English for new packs.** The service adds `what_changed_items` (`{key, now, before}`, `before` null with no earlier pack) beside the existing `what_changed` sentences, which stay because the PDF text is made from them; `openapi.json` is regenerated. The page builds the lines from the items and falls back to `changedLine` (the regular expression) only for a pack issued before the field existed, since an issued pack is never rewritten. Tests: a service unit test of `changed_item`, an assertion in the database test `tests/director_packs.rs`, and a UI test that the items win over a conflicting sentence. **Not done or not verified:** the regular expression is still in `pack-view.ts` for old packs; the database test was not run here (CI runs it); the PDF still reads the sentences; the Welsh text is unchanged (the same catalogue keys); the pack body has no section in `spec/`, so the contract change is in `openapi.json` and `tasks.md`.
- **CQ-20 closed on its acceptance; the Welsh 409 test is stricter.** The two acceptance tests hold: 0 copies of `err instanceof Error ? …` remain (173 at the start), and a Welsh-locale 409 shows the catalogue's Welsh sentence. The test now also asserts that sentence is not the English one (before, it compared the result with the catalogue key only, which would pass if the Welsh entry were a copy of the English). Verified: that test file. **Not done, and said plainly in `tasks.md`:** about 55 of the 94 pages still load by hand rather than through `resource()` (23 use it), because most share one `error` with their actions or edit the loaded value in place; and `pack-view.ts` still parses the service's English sentences, which needs a service change. I stopped converting pages rather than convert them all: the helper's purpose (no service English on screen, one failure path) is met by `errorText` and the no-api-text test, and each remaining page is a behaviour change with no gain. **Not verified:** CI.
- **`/compliance` and `/financials` load through `resource()` (CQ-20, part, not ticked).** Each loaded two things one after the other and showed the first if the second failed; each is now two resources that load **at the same time**, with the page's error being whichever failed first, so the partial view is kept but the two requests are no longer in sequence (a change in order, not in what is shown). Verified: svelte-check, lint (cap 0), 828 unit tests, `ppm.spec.ts` and the axe spec (95). **Not verified:** CI; the full Playwright suite; `ppm.spec.ts` names these routes but I did not read whether it asserts their error banner. **Left alone:** `/plans/[pid]/handover` (preview and commit share one `status`). About 58 pages remain.
- **The four simulation pages load through `resource()` (CQ-20, part, not ticked):** `/cost-risk`, `/plans/[pid]/cost-risk`, `/plans/[pid]/joint-risk` and `/plans/[pid]/schedule-risk`. Their hand-written `busy` flag is now the resource's `loading`, and the "run" button calls `reload`; `schedule-risk` still records the day it ran after a successful run. Behaviour is unchanged except that the old error clears when a run starts, not when it succeeds. Verified: svelte-check, lint (cap 0), 828 unit tests, the scheduling, security-headers and axe e2e specs (95). **Not verified:** CI; the full Playwright suite after this change; **no e2e spec presses "run" on the two cost-risk pages or the joint-risk page** (I found none naming them), so their busy state and error banner are covered by type-checking and the axe render only. About 60 pages remain.
- **`/calendar` and `/plans/[pid]/tasks/[tpid]` load through `resource()` (CQ-20, part, not ticked); and the flaky `load-once` spec is fixed.** The calendar keeps its "reload when the kind changes" effect, now calling `resource`'s `reload` (which drops a late answer, as the hand-written guard did); the task page loads once on mount. **The flake:** `load-once.spec.ts` counted the first call straight after `networkidle`, which can fire before a slow page has made it, so under parallel load it read 0 (it failed for the untouched flow page too, and had failed in two earlier full runs). It now waits for the first call, then checks that no second one follows. Shown: 24 of 24 pass at 8 workers (the old form failed most repeats), and a deliberate second `reload` on the calendar fails it ("Expected 1, received 2"), so it still catches a double load. Verified: lint (cap 0), svelte-check, 828 unit tests, **the full Playwright suite: 733 passed**. **Not done:** about 64 pages remain; the two risk-simulation pages (`busy` flag, re-run on a button) and `/me` were left. **Not verified:** CI.
- **Fix: `/plans/[pid]/critical-chain` showed the service's error text, which the "no page shows API error text" rule said no page does.** It read `c.reason.message` from a settled promise; the rule's test matched only variables named `err`, `error` or `e`, so it missed it. **My miss:** I reported that rule as done when the test could not see this form. The page now uses `errorText` (a fixed localized sentence by status), and `tests/unit/no-api-text.test.ts` also matches `reason.message` and `cause.message`; it fails on the old page and passes on the new. Verified: lint (cap 0), svelte-check, 828 unit tests, the critical-chain e2e spec. **Not done:** the test is a pattern match, so a differently named variable (`result.failure.message`) would still pass it; I searched `src/routes` and `src/lib/components` for every `.message` and found no other offender, but did not search `src/lib` outside components. **Not verified:** CI.

Status: 8 Implemented (some with stated limits), 3 Partly, 3 Gap (milestone forecast read as on track, clinical use unnoticed, erasure). Verified: `check-links.py` finds no broken link; each cited test name was found in the repository. **Not done / not verified:** no severity, probability or acceptability is assigned; I read the test names, **not each test's body**, so a row may claim more than its test asserts; the database tests were not run here (CI runs them); the standard was not read; nobody with clinical-safety or regulatory expertise has reviewed it. Linked from the ISO 14971 scoping page.

- **ISO 14971 scoping assessment: `docs/international-organization-for-standardization/iso-14971/index.md`.** Says the standard is a manufacturer's duty, that PPM is not a device on the MHRA reading (so it is not required), and maps clauses 4 to 10 to the repository: intended use is the only part partly met; the rest is not built. Notes that PPM's "risk" (project risk) is not the standard's (harm), and what a deployer using PPM clinically would still need. Verified: lint, svelte-check, vitest, and the full Playwright run: 731 passed, 2 `load-once` tests failed and passed on a rerun alone (flaky, as before; I did not find the cause). **Not done:** the index keys on lists that can be re-ordered (the staged edits on `what-if`) are no better than before; a real key needs an id the service does not send. **Not verified:** CI.

It also notes the risk: a deployer who uses PPM clinically and says so takes on the duties. Regenerated output is byte-identical to the committed `openapi_bodies.rs` (220 bodies), and `--check` of all three generators passes. **My miss:** I did not run the three `--check`s before pushing the split, as AGENTS.md says. **Not verified:** CI on this fix.

- **Feed tokens expire and rotate; one rule for link tokens (new `link_token` module).** A feed token is refused 365 days after issue, and issuing a new one revokes the caller's earlier tokens in the same transaction. `link_token::check` is the shared rule (revoked, expired, and redeemed for a single-use token); `spec/09-api-surface.md` has a "Link tokens" paragraph. **Decision, stated:** feed tokens stay reusable, because a calendar app fetches the same URL on a schedule and single use would end the subscription. **Not done:** no single-use link exists yet (the feed token is the only link token in the service), so the single-use branch has unit tests only and no write-side redemption code. **Not verified:** the new rotation and expiry assertions in `tests/feeds.rs` compile and pass clippy but were **not run**, because the Postgres on this machine belongs to another project and has no test database; CI will be their first run. Unit tests (5) and clippy `-D warnings` pass.

Five routes that read `err.message` (merge, governance, email deliveries, key persons, kanban) now use `errorReason` or an `ApiError` status test, and `tests/unit/no-api-text.test.ts` fails on any `.svelte` file that reads an error's `.message`. **Cost, stated:** a reader no longer sees "title must not be blank", only "Something you entered is not accepted." Verified: svelte-check, the error-text, resource and no-api-text unit tests, the import and load-once e2e specs, and one full Playwright run in which only those two specs failed (the import spec asserted the old English; fixed). **Not verified:** CI; the full vitest and Playwright suites after the final edits. **Not done:** the `+server.ts` proxy and OIDC code still read `.message` (server side, never shown).

- **CQ-11: a statement counter, and a test that uses it.** `query_count` counts the statements run on the app's own connection (set in `after_context`, because a clone does not see them). The new request test lists a plan's budget baselines with one baseline and with four and asserts the same number of statements; with a per-baseline query put back it failed (6 against 9). **Not done:** counts for the other fixed endpoints; CQ-11 stays unticked. **Verified:** clippy, and all 211 database tests in the `mod` binary pass with the hook. **Not verified:** the lib tests and the other test binaries; the production cost of the counter. **Open:** CF-7 (waiting for the owner's choice among three options).

- **BR-10 follow-up: audited the tests that assert search hit counts.** Only `list_paging` asserts a count (query "Paging", total 5) and no other test uses that word; the other search tests assert on a pid being present or absent. No change to code or tests. **Not verified:** by a run; this is a reading. **Not done:** a per-test index.

### 25 October

- **CI fix: the restore drill failed on the meetings test's plan names.** Tests in one run share one search index, and the index keeps a plan's entry after the next test truncates the database. `search_reaches_secondary_fields_tolerates_typos_and_filters_by_kind` asks for "Apoll" with `fuzzy=true`; the meetings test (NC-14) also created a plan named "Apollo", and with that extra stale entry the real plan fell off the result page (0 hits, 1 expected). Reproduced locally with `scripts/restore-test.sh` running the whole suite on the restored copy (it failed the same way), then fixed by renaming the meetings test's plans; the same run now passes (200). **Not fixed:** the fragility itself, a test that depends on how many other tests used a similar name; the other tests that create "Apollo" plans still add stale entries.

- **CI: three new advisories accepted, not fixed (RUSTSEC-2026-0391, -0398, -0403).** `cargo deny` failed on `zstd 0.13.3` and `zstd-safe 7.3.0`, published today. They are reached only with `--all-features`, through the optional `fluvio` feature: `fluvio 0.50.1` (the latest release) pins `zstd` 0.13. The default build uses `zstd 0.14.3` and `zstd-safe 8.2.0`, which are patched. They are listed in the service's `deny.toml` with the reason and today's date, as the file's own policy asks; the matcher's does not pull them. **Not fixed:** nothing in this repository can move them; it waits for a `fluvio` release that uses `zstd` 0.14. **Not verified:** whether the affected code is reachable in a build with the `fluvio` feature on (I did not look); anyone building with `--features fluvio` carries these. Revisit when `fluvio` is bumped.
- **CI fix: the iPad-portrait CEO check failed on the palette's button.** The check passed on my machine and failed in CI (page 1133 px against 1080) because CI's fonts are wider and the top row wrapped. A shorter label did not fix it; the button is now an entry in the navigation menu, the dialog is mounted at the root, and the Ctrl-K shortcut is unchanged. Before this, the BR-14 and BR-15 pushes were red in CI (BR-14 on `pnpm run lint`, then both on this check). The lesson: a control added to the top row can break a layout test that only fails with other fonts; I can only see it in CI.
- **BR-17 done (per-user part): hide the sections I do not use.** The Display page lists every section of the top bar with a tick; unticking hides it for that person, on the device and the account; nothing is stored until one is hidden, so a section added later reaches everyone who did not hide it. Account writes now go through one queue. Verified: 9 unit tests and two e2e tests (hide, reload, show all; axe on the page). **Not done:** per-plan switches in the UI (the service has them; no screen sets them). **Not verified:** the account copy against a running service; two browsers at once.
- **BR-15 done: paste rows from a spreadsheet.** On the board, tab-separated rows (Title, Deadline, Notes) are previewed row by row; a row with a problem is shown with its reason and left out; "Check with the service" runs the Asana import as a dry run on the good rows, "Create N tasks" does it, and pasting the same rows again updates instead of duplicating. Verified: 9 unit tests, two e2e tests (including axe), and a service test of the exact CSV. **Changed:** the date is stored as the task's *deadline* (what the import does), so it is called Deadline, the board does not show it and the overdue counts do not see it; `GET …/tasks` now returns `deadline`. **Not done:** more columns, a due date. **Not verified:** the browser against a running service; the Welsh (machine-written).
- **BR-14 done: command palette.** Ctrl or Cmd with K (or the entry in the navigation menu) opens a modal palette: plans by name through the search API, four commands, the last five choices remembered in the browser; arrows, Enter and Escape; focus trapped and returned. Verified: 12 unit tests of the ranking and six e2e tests, including axe with it open; `svelte-check` 0/0. **Changed:** no "status page" command (no such UI route); "exceptions" is Escalations. **Not done:** locales other than English and Welsh (the Welsh is machine-written, unreviewed); other searches. **Not verified:** a screen reader.
- **BR-7 done: planning rules audit.** Six rules read against the code; the finding for each, with the code reference, is in `plan.md` section 82. One defect found and fixed: **CF-6**, resolving a placeholder was a lost update (two concurrent resolvers both got `200`, the later replacing the earlier); the update now names the placeholder it saw and the loser gets `409`. Verified: a six-way concurrent test that failed three runs out of three on the old code and passes on the new. **Open:** **CF-7**, a person with no availability declared is assumed to be at 100 % without saying so; an owner decision, not changed. **Not looked at:** the leveling apply path, the sandbox commit, the UI's resource screens.
- **BR-8 done: one review checklist, four thin reviewers.** `agents/review-checklist.md` holds the rules (security, database, Rust, UI, every change), each with its source; `.claude/agents/*-reviewer.md` (12 lines each) point at it and add none. Verified: the offline link check, the `llms` check. **Not done:** running a reviewer on a real change. **Not verified:** that each item still matches the code.
- **BR-13 done: five starter templates.** A stage-gated project, a programme with three workstreams, a benefits-realisation review, a change-request process and an incident response, listed by `GET /api/plan-templates` with `starter: true` and instantiated like any template (a start date is required). They cannot be deleted or revised (`422`) and a saved template cannot take their names (`409`). Verified: 4 unit tests and a request test that instantiates each and checks the plan, task and milestone counts. **Changed:** code, not rows, so no migration or seeding. **Not done:** other languages; a screen. **Not verified:** the content by anyone who runs such work; blueprint sync from a starter-made plan.
- **BR-4 done: reverse references.** `GET /api/plans/{pid}/references?kind=&pid=` lists the meetings and lessons that point at a task, risk, issue, milestone, change request or cutover decision of the plan, with a count equal to the list; a private plan is `404` for a non-member and so is a record that is not in the plan. **Changed:** lessons had no link to a record, so a lesson now takes an optional `link` (migration 98); lessons recorded earlier appear under nothing. Verified: a request test and a real-token privacy test. **Not done:** comments, tasks and risks as referrers; a screen. **Not verified:** a guest token; speed on a very large plan. **CI fix:** the first push failed `list_paging` (a GET the census could not call had no reason); the route is now named in `UNREACHED` with one, and each list is capped at 100. I had run only some test binaries before pushing; I have now run all of them.
- **BR-12 done: a narrow write scope.** A token with `scope=capture` reads and searches, and may `POST` only a status update, a comment or a lesson; every other write is `403`, an administrator included, and the policy file cannot widen it. Verified: a unit test of the allowlist and a real-token test (three posts `200`; create, edit, delete, a task and a template revise `403`; the same identity without the scope unchanged). **Changed:** a check before the policy, not a `Rule`, because rules never see the path. **Not done:** MCP tools. **Not verified:** a real template instantiation (no such route is listed); a guest token.
- **BR-11 done: one page of operating commitments** (`operations/commitments.md`). Backups, restore reach, RPO and RTO, the processor position and OIDC link to their evidence; uptime, support targets, a data processing agreement, a deletion deadline, a notice period and SCIM say "not offered". Verified: the offline link check (0 broken). **Not done:** any new commitment or number. **Not verified:** owner agreement with §81.1; a legal read.
- **BR-2 done: evidence-backed status updates.** A status update may carry up to 10 claims, each citing up to 5 sources (`task:<pid>`, `risk:<pid>`, `issue:<pid>`, `milestone:<pid>` and the like, or `metric:<key>`). Every source is checked on save (a false one makes the update `422`) and stored as an id; on read it is resolved to a title, or `unavailable` with no title once the record is gone, or to a metric's current value and definition. `GET /api/status-metrics` publishes how the numbers are counted: due today is not overdue, and a number that cannot be known is `null`, never zero. No health score. Verified: 4 unit tests and 2 request tests. **Changed:** no per-record reader check, because the service has none below the plan. **Not done:** a screen; claims in the director pack. **Not verified:** a custom task workflow; a guest reader. **Seen:** `updates_are_due_then_overdue_…` failed twice, then passed three times in a row on the same code; it compares the host clock with the database's `created_at`, and the Podman VM's clock may lag. **Cause found 2026-10-26:** the database clock in the Podman VM ran about 170 ms ahead of the host's, and the test's late cut-off was exactly `now`; it now uses `now + 1 minute`. Test only; no service code was wrong.
- **BR-3 done: action items from minutes to tasks.** `POST /api/plans/{pid}/meetings/{mid}/task-proposals/preview` judges proposed tasks against a held meeting's minutes and writes nothing; `…/commit` creates them in one transaction, linked to their outcomes, at most one task per outcome (a unique table), with the retry behaviour of an `Idempotency-Key`. A proposal is dropped, with a named reason, if its quoted excerpt is not in the minutes, its outcome is not an action or already has a task, its due date is outside the window, or its assignee is not a person of the plan. Verified: 4 unit tests and a request test of the preview, the refusal, the retry and the meeting view. **Changed:** no name matching, because the service holds no names. **Not done:** name matching; risks and issues from minutes; a screen. **Not verified:** two concurrent commits against the unique table; a plan with a custom task workflow.
- **BR-5 done: webhook replay protection and redelivery.** Every attempt sends `X-Mxi-Timestamp`, `X-Mxi-Signature-V2` (MAC over `"<timestamp>."` and the body) and `X-Mxi-Delivery-Id`, with the old `X-Mxi-Signature` kept for one more release; a receiver should refuse a timestamp more than 300 seconds old. New administrator routes `GET /api/webhook-deliveries` and `POST /api/webhook-deliveries/{id}/redeliver` (one attempt, audit first, same event id, new delivery id). Verified: 2 unit tests, and a test with a real loopback receiver and key that checks the headers, that V2 verifies, that a changed timestamp does not, and the `404`/`422` cases; the AsyncAPI document is updated. **Not done:** redelivery of notification-sink deliveries; storing the delivery id in the log. **Not verified:** the non-administrator `404`; a receiver in another language; `OUTBOUND_STRICT=on`.

- **BR-1 done: idempotency keys on writes.** A `POST` may carry `Idempotency-Key`; the same key and request returns the first answer again (`Idempotent-Replayed: true`) and writes nothing twice; a different request under the key is `409`; keys are per caller and a replay is decided after the auth, guest and privacy guards; only a `2xx` answer is kept; a key lives one day. New table `idempotency_keys` (migration 95), a retention rule, two rows in the personal-data inventory. Verified: 5 unit tests of the decision, a request test (replay, conflicts, release on refusal, a meeting, the 1 MiB limit) and a real-token test of per-caller keys and the guards. **Not done:** a required key; keys on `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`. **Not verified:** a concurrent pair of requests; more than one replica.

- **BR-6 done (partly): a cross-plan regression test.** `a_record_of_one_plan_is_not_reachable_through_another_plans_path` tries plan B's task, component, milestone, risk, issue, meeting and lesson under plan A's path on 19 routes; each must answer `404` and plan B must be unchanged. Shown to fail by removing the plan check in `update_risk` (it answered 200), then restored. The other classes from the Brainitive review (attachment names and types, outbound destinations, search scoping) were read against the code and already have tests, so no new test and no defect found. **Not done:** the other roughly 75 nested routes; rich-text sanitising; the MCP `fetch` that does not exist yet.

- **BR-10 done: the fuzzy-search test no longer depends on other tests' plan names.** It clears the shared search index before asserting counts. Verified against the condition that failed the restore drill (an "Apollo" plan in the meetings test): the whole suite on a restored copy passes, 201 of 201. **Not done:** other count-asserting tests on the shared index were not audited.

- **Research: the Brainitive public site (BR-11 to BR-17).** Second source on the same subject: the home page, pricing block and privacy page of www.brainitive.app (marketing and policy text, nothing run). Seven more items in `tasks.md`: one page of operating commitments, a narrow write scope, starter templates, a command palette, paste from a spreadsheet, development activity on a task (blocked on task keys) and hidden modules; `plan.md` §77 to §81, `spec/brainitive-lessons/index.md` lessons 11 to 17. **Not verified:** the vendor's claims (uptime, backups, the limits and prices, which it calls planned); I did not sign in or see the product. Nothing was built.

- **Research: Brainitive (BR-1 to BR-10).** A review of the open-source team workspace [armendzekjiri/brainitive](https://github.com/armendzekjiri/brainitive) (AGPL-3.0, ideas only): the map in `spec/brainitive-lessons/index.md`, the assessment in `plan.md` §71 to §76, ten new backlog items `BR-1` to `BR-10` and two amendments (NC-2, CQ-11) in `tasks.md`. Order of work: a security sweep, idempotency keys, signed webhook timestamps and redelivery, then evidence-backed status and action outcomes to tasks. **Not verified:** everything said about how Brainitive behaves comes from its own documents; I read its docs, 18 plan files and three middleware files, not its tests or most of its code. Nothing was built.

- **Rust dependencies and the CI action updated.** Service: `tantivy` 0.26 to 0.27 and `rstest` 0.27 to 0.28 (the two Dependabot bumps of PR 27), plus 33 locked crates (tokio 1.54.1, redis 1.8.0, uuid 1.29.0, zip 9.1.0, typed-path 0.13.0 and others). Matcher: 9 locked crates, including `unicode-normalization` 0.1.26 (PR 24). CI: `bnjbvr/cargo-machete` v0.9.1 to v0.9.2 (PR 26). The Rust toolchain (1.99.1) and the other actions (floating major tags) were already current. Verified: clippy `--all-features` clean, 1121 lib tests, the whole database suite (201 passed, which exercises the search index on tantivy 0.27), the paging, privacy and read-only-scope tests; the matcher's clippy and tests. **Not verified:** that a search index written by tantivy 0.26 on disk opens under 0.27 (the tests build a fresh one; the index is derived and rebuilt at boot, which is the fallback, but this was not tried against a real old index); the restore drill and the container build, which run in CI. **Not done:** `redis:7-alpine` and `ubuntu:24.04` images (newer majors exist) and `typescript` 7.

- **UI dependencies updated, Lily design system included.** `@lilydesignsystem/svelte-headless` 0.5.1 to 0.6.1, `svelte-locale-translations` 0.1.0 to 0.3.0, `themes` 0.5.0 to 0.6.0, `vite` 8.3.4 to 8.3.5 (supersedes Dependabot PR 25). The new themes lighten the base text colour, which took the muted-text token from 4.5:1 down to 4.07:1 in the axe check on the governance page, so `--mxi-color-muted` now mixes 88% of the text colour instead of 78% (`src/app.css`). Verified: `pnpm run check` 0 errors, vitest 787 passed, Playwright 722 passed. **Not verified:** the other Lily themes in the picker for contrast (the e2e suite checks the default theme); `director-packs.spec.ts` "passes axe in zh-cn" failed once in a full run and passed in two reruns on its own, so it is recorded as a flake, not understood. **Not done:** `typescript` 6.0.3 to 7.0.2 (a major version; left for its own change); the other Lily packages are already at their latest.

- **NC-13 extended: links between files, pay, and new custom fields.** In the batch import (`POST /api/import/mspdi-batch`): a `CrossProject` predecessor link is placed as a dependency between the two plans' tasks when the file it names is in the job (matched by file name, extension and path ignored) and the task exists there; the rest are listed with the reason. Resources' standard and overtime rates, calendars and per-use costs are merged across files (a disagreement is a conflict and is not taken); with `resource_map` (who each resource is: a `person_ref` or a `role`), finance access and a currency, rates are written to rate tables from `rates_effective_from`; a calendar is written only for the caller's own `person_ref`; a task's `FixedCost` becomes a fixed task cost once. With `create_fields`, a mapped custom field that does not exist is declared with the type that fits its values. The single-file importer is unchanged except that a cross-project link note now names the file and task. Verified: 4 more unit tests (cross-link placement and file-name matching, rate conversion and conflicts, type inference), a request test (dry run, apply, and a re-import into the same plans that doubles nothing: a link, a rate table, a fixed cost, a field), the OpenAPI/paging/privacy censuses, the whole database suite (201 passed, directly and on a restored copy), clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** week, month and year rates; per-use cost; resource skills; another person's calendar; a successor link in the other direction; a screen. **Not verified:** a real Project Online export (the cross-project fixture is hand-written to the `CrossProject`/`CrossProjectName` elements as the MSPDI schema describes them); rates in a currency other than the file's declared one; the finance and read-only refusals for these new parts (the test runs with enforcement off); the cost of a ten-file job with many links.

- **Many MSPDI files in one job (NC-13, ticked).** `POST /api/import/mspdi-batch` reads up to ten MS Project files, lists the resources of all of them once, imports each into an existing plan or a new one under a given parent, and stores custom fields (enterprise ones included) on the tasks through the caller's `field_map`; a dry run is the default and says what it would do and what it leaves out. The reader now reads `ExtendedAttributes`; the single-file importer's body became a function the batch calls. No schema change. Verified: 3 unit tests (the pool merge, the field mapping, value typing), a request test of three related projects imported into three new plans (pool merged once, 2 tasks and 1 link each, the cost centre stored, an unmapped field reported, a re-import into the same plan changes nothing, a mapping to a missing field reported, a bad second file writes nothing), the OpenAPI/paging/privacy censuses, the whole database suite (200 passed), clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** links between projects; resource rates, calendars and costs; creating custom-field definitions; a screen. **Not verified:** a real Project Online export (the fixtures are hand-written to the MSPDI shape the reader already handles); the job at the ten-file, five-megabyte ceiling; a failure part-way through a real apply (the stop-at-first-failure path is not exercised).

- **Meetings (NC-14, ticked).** A plan plans a meeting with an agenda, records it held with minutes and outcomes (decision, action, note) once, or cancels it; agenda items and outcomes may point at a task, risk, issue, milestone, change request or cutover decision of the same plan. `GET …/pdf` prints it, deterministically (no render time), from what the JSON shows. New tables `meetings` and `meeting_items` (migration 94), retention class `COM`, five rows in the personal-data inventory. Verified: 4 unit tests of the rules and the printed text, a golden-PDF test (the rendered bytes equal `tests/fixtures/meeting-golden.pdf`), a request test (plan, links, refusals, hold, `409`s, PDF text and identical re-render, cancel, order), the OpenAPI/paging/privacy censuses, the whole database suite (199 passed), clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** attendees; editing held minutes; a screen; purging (retention is stated, not enforced). **Not verified:** the PDF in a PDF reader (checked by byte equality and the extracted text only); non-ASCII text beyond Latin-1 is written as `?` by the PDF writer; the whole database suite passed (199). **Found, not fixed:** `tests/personal_data_inventory.rs` is not run by CI (it is not `#[ignore]`d, and CI runs only ignored tests) and fails at HEAD for about 25 older columns (escalations, frameworks, lessons, status updates, tolerances and others) missing from `governance/personal-data-inventory.csv`; this change adds only its own rows.

- **Blueprint sync (NC-12, ticked).** A plan made from a template remembers it; a template can be revised from a plan tree (`POST /api/plan-templates/{pid}/revise`); `GET /api/plan-templates/{pid}/plans` shows the plans made from it and the changes each has waiting; `GET /api/plans/{pid}/template-diff` lists the task additions, changes and removals, marking those the plan's people have edited; `POST /api/plans/{pid}/template-sync` applies the ones named, refusing an edited task unless it is named in `overwrite_local` too. New table `plan_template_links` and `plan_templates.version` (migration 93). Verified: 4 unit tests of the diff, local-edit, re-keying and shape rules, a request test of one template change applied to one plan and refused for another that edited the task (then forced by name), the OpenAPI/paging/privacy censuses, the whole database suite (198 passed), clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** milestones, components, plan fields, workflows and task dates; plans made before this (no link); events for tasks a sync creates; any screen. **Not verified:** a template with several levels of plans (the test uses one plan); a sync in a deployment with many links.

- **Read-only tokens (NC-15, ticked).** A token carrying the attribute `scope=readonly` reads and is refused every write, delete and destructive action with `403`, whatever else it holds (an administrator included), by a rule the service puts in front of every policy. The authentication service mints the attribute; nothing here does. Verified: a unit test and a real-token request test; clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** service accounts; narrower scopes (one plan, one area). **Not verified:** that every read-only `POST` (search, duplicate checks, previews) is classed as a read, so a read-only integration may be refused some of them.

- **The service can run its own scheduled jobs (NC-5, ticked).** With `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_SCHEDULED_JOBS` naming them and the scheduler ticker on, the service runs `tolerance_escalations`, `exception_breaches`, `retention_purge` (a dry run unless applying is switched on), `threshold_sweep` and the weekly `exceptions_digest`. A replica claims a job with a PostgreSQL advisory lock and a `scheduled_job_runs` row, so replicas run it once; every run is audited and counted in `scheduled_job_runs_total`. Off by default. New table (migration 92); the threshold sweep's body is now a function the endpoint and the scheduler share. Verified: unit tests, a request test of two schedulers giving one run, the audit entries and the metric, clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** the notice to the plan lead; an endpoint listing the runs. **Not verified:** real replicas on separate connections; the ticker; a failing job.

- **Status updates on a cadence (NC-4, ticked).** A plan sets how often it expects an update; a person posts an on-track / at-risk / off-track call with a narrative (append-only, audited); the service says when the next is due and whether it is overdue, lists the due plans, and reminds the last poster once per due date (`POST /api/status-updates/remind`, and the scheduler ticker). The director pack gains a `status_updates` section read as at its cut-off. New tables `plan_status_cadences` and `status_updates` (migration 91). Verified: 5 unit tests of the due rules, a request test of due, overdue, one reminder, the clock restarting and the pack section at two cut-offs, the OpenAPI/paging/privacy censuses, clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** any screen; removing a cadence. **Not verified:** the section inside a real drafted pack (only the section function was called); the scheduler's reminder pass.

- **Weekly portfolio-exceptions digest (NC-17, ticked).** `GET /api/exceptions/digest` and a weekly `exceptions_digest` task gather the red and amber tolerances, the open threshold exceptions and the live escalations (overdue first) into one message, sent as an `exceptions_digest` notification through each recipient's own sinks, once per recipient per week. Recipients are the `person:` references in `PROJECT_PORTFOLIO_MANAGEMENT_DIGEST_RECIPIENTS`. Verified: unit tests of the ordering and wording, a request test with one item of each kind and the once-a-week send, the privacy and paging censuses, clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** recipients as a setting; per-recipient content; a screen. **Not verified:** that a private plan is absent from the sent digest; a real sink delivery.

- **CloudEvents webhooks and an AsyncAPI document (NC-11, ticked).** A webhook target can set `format` to `cloudevents_structured` or `cloudevents_binary` (default `native`, unchanged); the HMAC signature still covers the exact body sent. `asyncapi.json` (AsyncAPI 3.0) describes the live stream and the webhook shapes. Verified: unit tests of each format against the CloudEvents 1.0 required attributes as written in the test, and of the document (current, references resolve, every published live kind described); clippy `--all-features`. **Not verified:** any real delivery; conformance by the official CloudEvents SDK or the AsyncAPI linter. **Not done:** `time` (the envelope has none); serving the document; CloudEvents on the live stream.

- **Lessons-learned register (NC-8, ticked).** A plan records lessons at a gate or at closure (`POST`/`GET /api/plans/{pid}/lessons`, withdraw with `POST …/{lpid}/withdraw`; append-only, audited). `GET /api/proposals/{pid}/lessons` shows the lessons of past plans whose names match the proposal's title, each with the match score and breakdown. New table `lessons` (migration 90). Verified: a request test (similar proposal lists the lessons with evidence, unrelated lists none, withdrawal), the OpenAPI/route/subject censuses, clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** any screen; matching beyond the title; limiting to closed plans; automatic recording at a gate decision. **Not verified:** that a private plan's lessons stay hidden at intake (relies on the shared fence, not exercised).

- **Efficient frontier (NC-7, ticked).** `GET /api/scenarios/frontier?currency=` runs the scenario generator over a sweep of budget caps (`caps=` or an even `steps=` spread) and returns value (summed Smart Score) against cost for each, with the same per-plan rationale as one `POST /api/scenarios/generate` at that cap. Nothing is saved. Verified: unit tests, a request test where every point equals the single-cap run, the OpenAPI/route/paging/privacy censuses, clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** any chart or screen; an optimal selection. The greedy selection is not optimal, so the frontier is monotone on the fixture, not in general; a point that buys nothing more is flagged `dominated`.

- **WSJF and RICE scoring (NC-6, ticked).** Two more ways to rank plans beside the Smart Score, from estimated inputs stored per plan: `PUT`/`GET /api/plans/{pid}/scoring-inputs`, `GET /api/plans/{pid}/score?model=wsjf|rice` (score, formula, each component explained, the inputs still missing with their reasons) and `GET /api/prioritization?model=smart|wsjf|rice`. A score with any input missing is `null`, never zero. New table `scoring_inputs` (migration 89). Verified: 7 unit tests of the formulas, a request test of the ranking by each model, the OpenAPI/route/paging/privacy censuses, clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** any screen; configurable weights. **Not verified:** the privacy of the new ranking variants beyond the shared plan fence.

- **A refusal is worded in the reader's language first (CQ-20, part, not ticked).** In a non-English locale, an error from the service now shows the catalogue's sentence for that kind of refusal (not allowed, not found, conflict, changed since you loaded it, too large, not accepted, too many requests) with the service's English reason after it in brackets; a server fault shows only the localized "the service had a problem". In English nothing changes. 15 hand-written copies of the old expression in 11 files now use `errorReason()`. Welsh for the 10 new strings is machine-written. Verified: 787 unit tests (including Welsh with a 409), `svelte-check`, lint (no errors), the complete Playwright suite (723). **Not done:** the per-code mapping (a 409 about one thing reads like a 409 about another); the `resource()` helper; the English reason is still shown in brackets.

### 24 October

- **`tba.rs` is split by section (CQ-15, part, not ticked).** The 3,401-line time-based-analysis module is now `src/tba/` with one file per section and its tests apart. A move only: the public paths, the behaviour and the tests are unchanged (1075 lib tests and the full database suite pass). **Not done:** `engineering.rs`, `visibility.rs` (no unit tests yet) and `auth.rs`. Also: the previous push failed CI's clippy step on a doc comment missing backticks (fixed in the next commit); I had added that test after my last clippy run.
- **The UI client's request bodies are checked against the service, and creating an automation rule is fixed (CQ-9, part, not ticked).** The service writes its OpenAPI document to `openapi.json`; a UI test compares the field names and required-ness of 71 request bodies the client sends with the service's schema for each route. **It found that the automations page could never create a rule:** it sent `action_kind` and `action_value` where the service requires `actions: [{ kind, value }]` (a 422 every time), and the rules table read a field the service does not return, so its Action column was blank; the e2e and unit tests had encoded the same wrong shape. Fixed in the client, the page and both tests. Verified: 783 unit tests, `svelte-check`, the automations e2e spec, the service's own test of `openapi.json`. **Not verified:** the other 42 bodies (free-form or union types are not compared); response shapes (undocumented). The complete Playwright suite (723) and lint (no errors) pass.
- **Every mounted route is documented; two integrity verifiers are administrators only (CQ-8, part, not ticked).** Routes nobody had documented (46 paths, plus missing methods on documented ones) now get an operation built from the handler's doc comment; the list of undocumented paths falls from 52 to the 2 docs routes. Writes that take no body are proved by the handler's signature. **Behaviour change found by the privacy census once these routes became visible to it:** `GET /api/compliance/records/verify` and `/audit/verify` were open to any signed-in reader and covered private plans (totals, and the rows that differ); they now need `access=admin` (403 otherwise). Verified: the full database suite (191 plus every other binary), 1074 lib tests, clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** response schemas (the generated operations say the shape is not documented); the generated summaries are only as good as the handlers' comments (some say "No description has been written for this route yet").
- **156 more request bodies are documented, derived from the handlers' own types (CQ-8, part, not ticked).** `schemars` is derived on 316 request types and a script writes `src/openapi_bodies.rs`, so a route's documented body is the type its handler deserialises. Writes without a documented body fall from 227 to 71. New dependency: `schemars` 1.x (already in the lockfile through another crate). The Docs job now fails if the generated file is stale. Verified: the full database suite (191 plus every other binary), 1074 lib tests, clippy `--all-features`. **Not done:** the 71 remaining writes, the 52 undocumented paths, response schemas, moving documentation beside controllers. **Not verified:** the generated bodies against a real OpenAPI validator (a unit test only checks they are plain 3.0 shapes); nested types the matcher crate owns appear as free-form objects.
- **A write whose audit row cannot be written is refused, everywhere a caller can be refused (CQ-3, ticked).** The last foreground writes moved into their transactions (bulk import request and cancel, scheduled-action creation, board snapshot, task order, per-item task bulk edit; MS Project export fails closed; automation's dependent shift is skipped). Seven audits of things already done with no caller stay log-and-keep, named in a shrink-only test. **Not atomic, stated:** MS Project import fails the request on a failed audit but its many writes are not one transaction (it is idempotent). Verified: the full database suite (191 plus every other binary), 1073 lib tests, clippy `--all-features`. **Not verified:** the log line of the log-and-keep path; any helper write outside its transaction that no test reaches.
- **More writes refuse on audit failure; a one-connection deadlock found and guarded (CQ-3, part, not ticked).** Timesheets, start control, change requests, buffer snapshots, calendars, RACI, labour costs, attachments, and template instantiation (undone if its audit fails). 14 calls in 8 controllers remain on the log-and-keep path. A transaction plus a read through the pool deadlocks on a one-connection pool (tests and development), which failed ten tests; fixed in three handlers and in an old case in `processes.rs`, and a unit test now fails for any controller that does it. Verified: the full database suite (191 plus every other binary), 1073 lib tests, clippy `--all-features`. **Not verified:** that a production pool under load never starves for the same reason (the test only forbids the pattern; it does not measure pool pressure); stored attachment bytes are not rolled back with a refused upload (an unreferenced blob remains).
- **19 more audited writes refuse on audit failure (CQ-3, part, not ticked).** Approvals, availability, controls, deployment-move decisions, engineering, financials, milestones, schedule baselines, thresholds, workflow registration; 26 calls in 16 controllers remain on the log-and-keep path. Helpers that wrote through the app context (`settle`, `persist`, `register_control`) now take a connection so their writes join the transaction. Verified: the full database suite (191 plus every other binary), 1072 lib tests, clippy `--all-features`. **Not verified:** any helper write in these handlers that no test reaches.
- **The sensitive audited writes refuse on audit failure, and so do three sensitive reads (CQ-3, part, not ticked).** By hand: the time-zone setting, framework edit/link/unlink, the escalation steps. The in-memory time zone now changes only after the setting is stored and audited. **My addition to the owner's rule:** a data-subject export, the access review, and a request served by a break-glass grant are not served when their audit row cannot be written (spec NFR-6 says so). 45 calls in 26 controllers still log and keep. Verified: the full database suite (191 plus every other binary), 1072 lib tests, clippy `--all-features`.
- **More audited writes refused when their audit fails (CQ-3, part, not ticked).** 26 more writes (176 of about 230) now write their audit row in their transaction; 54 calls in 32 controllers remain on the log-and-keep path, listed in the shrink-only test. Verified: the full database suite (191 in the main binary and every other binary) and 1072 lib tests, clippy `--all-features`. **Not verified:** that no helper write in the moved handlers sits outside its transaction where no test reaches it (the script skipped handlers that pass `ctx` to a helper, and I scanned the rest).
- **A write whose audit row cannot be written is now refused, for 150 of about 230 audited writes (CQ-3, part, not ticked).** The owner decided (plan §70 q.2, spec NFR-6): the audit row goes in the change's transaction; if it cannot be written the change rolls back and the caller gets `500 audit_unavailable`, counted in `audit_write_failed_total`. An outage of the audit table now stops those writes, by design. Moved by script, then checked by the full database suite, which found three helper writes left outside the transaction (an escalation event, a framework version, cutover criteria); those are fixed. **Not done (after a second batch the same day, 54 calls in 32 controllers):** the rest still log and keep the write (listed in a test that only shrinks); audited reads stay best-effort. **Not verified:** that no helper write in the 150 sits outside its transaction where no test reaches it. One full database run failed `cross_plan` while booting the test app; it passed alone four times and the next full run passed, cause not found.
- **Table rows are cast in one place (CQ-21, ticked, with a limit).** 180 `as unknown as T` casts of a table row in 41 files became `rowOf<T>(row)` (`src/lib/row-of.ts`); two `as unknown as` remain in `src`, and a unit test fails if another appears. No behaviour changed. **This moves the cast; it does not make it safe:** `rowOf<T>` still trusts `T`, so a table given the wrong type still compiles. The checked version (a generic `DataTable<T>` or `columns<T>()`) is not built. Verified: `svelte-check`, prettier, 782 unit tests, and the complete Playwright suite (723). **Not verified:** that any individual `T` is the right type; no test could.
- **Frameworks have versions, and a shared one has an owner right (CF-4, ticked).** Editing a ways-of-working or governance framework no longer changes every plan that follows it. An edit appends a version (`framework_versions`, append-only); a plan keeps the version its link pins until it is moved, and its escalation path comes from that version. `GET …/{pid}/versions`; the in-force answer gains `version`, `latest_version` and `behind`; `PUT` of a plan's link takes an optional `version`. Creating, editing or deleting an organisation-wide framework needs `access=admin` or `framework=edit`. Migration 88 makes every existing framework version 1 and pins every existing link to it, so nothing changes on upgrade. Verified: 1071 lib tests, clippy `--all-features`, every database test binary (190 in the main one), a new request test with real identities that fails when the pin is ignored, and the migration's SQL run by hand on a scratch database that already held a framework and a link (both became version 1; running it twice changed nothing). **Not verified:** the migration through the migrator on a populated database. **Not done:** any UI for versions; a notice to plans that are behind.
- **Acknowledging and resolving an escalation are rights, and resources (CF-4, part).** New ABAC areas `escalation_acknowledgement` and `escalation_resolution`: an administrator, or a caller with the attribute `escalation=acknowledge` or `escalation=resolve`; separate, and not widenable by a deployment policy. New `GET`/`PUT /api/plans/{pid}/escalations/{epid}/acknowledgement` and `…/resolution`: the same act and audit entry as the `POST …/acknowledge` and `…/resolve` that remain, idempotent, a resolution never overwritten (`409`), the acknowledgement cleared when the escalation goes up a level. **Behaviour change: until the identity service issues `escalation=acknowledge` / `resolve`, only administrators can acknowledge or resolve; plain writers get `403`.** Verified: lib tests, clippy `--all-features`, every database test binary, a new request test with real identities (which fails when the check is disabled). **Not done:** shared-framework editing rights and versions (the rest of CF-4); membership of the addressed body; any UI change (the buttons still show to everyone and fail with 403); the `403` is not mapped to Welsh text. The census in `list_paging.rs` needed the two new single-record GETs on its allow-list.
- **A plan sub-page follows the address (CQ-18, ticked).** Moving from one plan to another on the same screen (for example a link from one plan's WBS to another's) kept the first plan's data on 22 pages that load once on mount. The root layout now restarts the page when its `pid` or `tpid` changes. Verified: a new Playwright test that failed before and passes now; the complete Playwright suite (723 tests); 781 unit tests (one test's mock of `page` gained `params`). **Not verified:** the other 21 pages individually.
- **Large plans are no longer silently shortened in the UI (CF-1, ticked).** The WBS page now says when it lists only part of a plan, the earned-value page says task names may be missing (its figures are summed by the service over every task), and the critical-path page offers the critical part when the full schedule is refused as too large. Verified: 781 unit tests, `svelte-check`, four new Playwright tests on stubs. **Not verified:** a real 2,001-task plan end to end; the full Playwright suite; the other pages that read the task list.
- **The database refuses overlapping availability ranges (CF-3, ticked).** An exclusion constraint on `person_availabilities` (migration 87; needs the `btree_gist` extension, which it creates) backs the service's existing lock-and-check. Verified: a test inserting two overlapping ranges from two connections at once keeps exactly one, and fails when the constraint is swapped for `CHECK (true)`. **Not verified:** the `409` the service gives if the database refuses (no test reaches it); the migration against existing overlapping data (it fails, by design, until they are fixed); a managed Postgres that forbids `CREATE EXTENSION`.
  One full database run failed `scale::capacity_views_do_not_fan_out_with_scale` (`/capacity/utilization` counted 4 queries at 5 plans and 6 at 60); it passed alone four times and in a second full run (190 of 190). I did not find the cause, so it is an unexplained flake, not a fix.
- **Deleted 19 unused page keys (CQ-23, ticked).** Calendar and board export labels, the gantt and board view labels, and a few others had been replaced by the shared `p.table.*` keys and nothing read them; they are gone from the English and Welsh catalogues and the shrink-only list is empty. Verified: 781 unit tests (the census, and the check that the list holds only still-unused keys), `svelte-check`. **Not verified:** the Playwright suite; I relied on a search of `src` showing no reader, so a key built in a way the census cannot see would be missed (the census already treats run-time prefixes as used).
- **Confirm questions use the Lily alert dialog (CQ-23, part).** Deleting an objective, key result or KPI, and merging plans, no longer call the browser's `confirm()`, whose text and buttons ignore the chosen locale. One `ConfirmHost` in the layout shows a Lily `AlertDialog`; Cancel has focus, Escape means no. Three new catalogue keys (Welsh machine-written, in the review CSV). Verified: 781 unit tests, `svelte-check`, eslint on the touched files, and a new Playwright test on the merge page. **Not done:** the review-score `window.prompt`; the other three sites have no e2e of their own; the complete Playwright suite and axe were not re-run.
- **Audited every use of "today" (CQ-13, ticked).** Each of the 93 `deployment_today()` calls is a portfolio-wide view or default, a date stamped on a record, or a scheduler run, and all stay deployment-wide; the two personal views (`/api/me/tasks`, the notification digest) already take the caller's zone. The classification is in spec §7.x. No code changed. **Not done:** no `clippy.toml` disallowed method (the census test bans the raw form, and a disallowed `date_naive` would also ban `local_today`); no request test per endpoint showing its default follows the zone; no UI screen for the setting. The audit was by reading call sites and names, not by running each endpoint.
- **An administrator sets the time zone that defines "today" (CQ-13, part).** Because the software is used in several zones at once, "today" for portfolio-wide views and defaults is now an explicit setting, not the UTC date: `GET`/`PUT`/`DELETE /api/settings/today-time-zone` (anyone signed in may read; only `access=admin` may change; a non-IANA name is `422`; changes are audited). It lives in a new `deployment_settings` table and each instance re-reads it every minute. Until set it is UTC, so nothing changes by default. `tz::utc_today()` is renamed `tz::deployment_today()` at all 93 call sites. Verified: lib tests (including UTC−10 and daylight saving through the pure function), clippy `-D warnings --all-features`, and a new request test with real admin and non-admin identities. **Not done:** no screen in the UI for it; the 93 sites are all deployment-wide, none audited yet to see which should follow the caller's own zone; a change takes up to a minute to reach other instances; a stored value edited by hand to something invalid is logged and ignored.
- **Fixed CI after CQ-14.** One `#[expect(clippy::unused_async)]` in `auth.rs` is unfulfilled in the all-features build CI uses; it is an `allow` again. I had run clippy with default features only.

### 23 October

- **The threshold sweep reads its plans in one query (CQ-11, part).** It fetched each threshold's plan separately. The other two places the task named (`includes.rs`, `task_get.rs`) run one query per requested include, not per row, so they are unchanged. Verified: clippy, lib tests, the 190 request tests and the `organizations` test, which runs the sweep. **Not done:** no test counts queries, so nothing proves the sweep stays at one; the results are not compared before and after beyond the existing assertions.
- **Every lint exception in the service is now `#[expect]` (CQ-14).** 355 `#[allow]` became `#[expect]`, so an exception that is no longer needed fails the build; 33 such were removed. Verified: clippy `-D warnings` over the workspace and every target, and the lib tests. **Not done:** the 173 `missing_docs` exceptions remain (the items are undocumented); two `allow`s stay with a reason (`wildcard_imports` in `models/behaviors.rs`, `dead_code` in the shared OTLP test module); the database tests were compiled but not run; the matcher and UI were not changed.
- **Search-index writes share a commit (CQ-10).** Concurrent creates add their documents and then share one index commit (an fsync) instead of one each; each still returns only when its own write is searchable. `POST /api/plans` under 32 connections: about 54 req/s against 7, p50 about 0.55 s against 3 to 4 s. Verified: new concurrency test, 1069 lib and 190 database tests, clippy. **Not verified:** the comparison is noisy (four runs, a fresh database for the new build only, one machine); p99 had two slow-tail runs (4.2 s) I did not explain; a crash between an add and the shared commit loses the same writes it always could (the reindex task repairs it).
- **Measured what CQ-10 changed, and it did not help the tail (CQ-10, part).** `POST /api/plans` under 32 connections: p99 about 8 to 9 s after moving index writes to the blocking pool, against 5.5 to 9 s before; the before build also failed about a quarter of requests with 500, the after build none. The cost is the per-create index commit. **Not done:** batching commits; explaining the 500s; any repeat on quiet hardware (four to six noisy runs, growing database). See `BENCHMARKS.md`.
- **A read whose visibility lookup failed now answers 503 (CQ-6).** `hidden_plans` and `readable_plans` still return sets, but a failure marks the request and a middleware turns a successful `GET`/`HEAD` into `503 unavailable` instead of a `200` with fewer rows. Verified: new database test on `GET /api/plans`, lib tests, clippy and all 190 database tests. **Not done:** writes are not converted; only the list route is tested, not each caller; work moved onto another task would not carry the mark. The first suite runs failed because a native Postgres on this machine had taken `127.0.0.1:5432`; they passed against a throwaway container on 5452.
- **Dropped serde_json's `arbitrary_precision` feature (CQ-16, part).** Nothing in the service used it.
